## Snapshot
Lumentum is the cleanest US large-cap expression of the AI-datacenter photonics buildout. Two reportable segments — **Cloud & Networking** (the engine: optical/photonic chips, components, modules, subsystems for hyperscalers and NEMs; ~86% of FY25 sales, of which Components ~65% of total company revenue) and **Industrial Tech** (cyclically soft, ~14% of FY25, -15% YoY) (LITE 10-K, FY25, 2025-08-19; segment shares per Citi LITE initiation, 2025-08-29; TD Cowen, 2026-03-11). What matters is the AI stack: indium-phosphide (InP) EML/CW/ultra-high-power (UHP) lasers, cloud transceivers (1.6T), optical circuit switches (OCS), and the four growth legs management keeps repeating — cloud transceivers, OCS, scale-out CPO, and scale-up CPO. The franchise pivot is real: legacy cyclical telco supplier → "foundational engine of the AI revolution" (Hurlston, Q2FY26, 2026-02-03). NVIDIA took a direct equity stake (+$2.02B to cash) on top of a take-or-pay laser supply agreement (Q3FY26, 2026-05-06). The buy-side debate is no longer demand — it's whether they can build it (execution/capacity), whether InP merchant supply eventually overshoots, and whether the multiple holds.

## At a glance — product · buyer · supplier
| | |
|---|---|
| **Sells (top 3)** | 1) InP EML/CW laser chips (Components ~65% of rev) · 2) Cloud transceivers (1.6T) · 3) Optical circuit switches (OCS) |
| **Main buyer(s)** | Hyperscalers + NVIDIA (equity stake + take-or-pay) & NEMs; concentrated — Customer A 16.0% / Customer B 15.4% of FY25 net rev |
| **Key suppliers** | InP substrate from Sumitomo (LTA); semicap reactors/etch tools; China (70%+ of global InP output) |

## Earnings preview — Q4FY26 / FY26 (reports 2026-08-11 AMC) — ✅ RESOLVED 2026-08-11

> **✅ RESOLVED — the print landed 2026-08-11 AMC. Scoreboard vs this section's own bogeys is in `## Current state` and `## Changelog` (2026-08-11). Short version: the June quarter beat every base case ($1.006–1.010B rev / 50.4% GM / $3.23 EPS), and the September guide of $1.225–1.275B (mid $1.250B) cleared UBS and MS but only *met* the Jefferies $1.25B+ bar and missed the intraday $1.3B whisper — hence a flat-to-down tape. Section retained unedited below as the pre-print record.**

**House call — structurally bullish, tactically a high bar.** The June quarter looks low-risk: every fresh preview reviewed expects a beat or a result near the top of management's range. The stock is more likely to trade on **September revenue guidance and the quality of gross-margin conversion**. Published September guide bogeys span **UBS $1.15–1.20B**, **MS $1.175–1.20B**, and **Jefferies $1.25B+**. A guide can therefore beat Bloomberg and still feel soft if it lands inside the UBS/MS range but below the Jefferies whisper. The clean bull is a September guide at/above the Jefferies bar **with** evidence that OCS/EML/pump mix offsets lower-margin transceiver growth; the clean bear is a guide near consensus accompanied by gross-margin drag or an OCS/CPO capacity slip. _Capstone synthesis of the cited previews, not company guidance._

**Event:** results Tuesday **2026-08-11 after market close**; call **5:00pm ET** ([Lumentum IR reporting-date release](https://investor.lumentum.com/financial-news-releases/news-details/2026/Lumentum-Announces-Reporting-Date-for-Fourth-Quarter-and-Fiscal-Year-2026-Results/default.aspx), 2026-07-13). Price reference **$886.35** (Bloomberg, 2026-08-10).

### Why the setup is harder than the quarter

- **Demand is confirmed; execution is the variable.** Management reported an EML supply-demand imbalance **greater than 30%**, record EML shipments **+100% YoY**, and a target for **>50% EML-unit growth by the Dec-2026 quarter** (Hurlston, Q3FY26 call, 2026-05-06). JPM expects a beat-and-raise and argues stronger Telco/DCI and EML mix can offset faster, lower-margin Google transceiver growth (JPM / Joseph Cardoso, 2026-08-06).
- **OCS creates the near-term step-up.** Working post-call marks are June OCS **~$70M**, rising toward **~$170M per quarter in 1HFY27**, with a Dec-quarter run rate **≥$200M and possibly ~$250M** (JPM, 2026-05-08; LITE IR, 2026-05-11). The print must confirm that OCS—not only lower-margin transceivers—drives the September guide.
- **Margins are the fulcrum.** Transceiver GM was described as **in the 20s**, with line of sight into the 30s, while corporate non-GAAP GM reached **47.9%** in Q3FY26 (Hurlston, 2026-05-07; Ali, 2026-05-06). MS expects June GM near **50%** but less sequential accretion because most new capacity is initially in transceivers; September GM near **51%** assumes better OCS/CPO mix (MS / Meta Marshall, 2026-08-03).
- **The CPO debate is now timing and laser content, not TAM.** Management's IR meeting placed first scale-up CPO deployment in **late 2027**, but LITE's Director of Silicon Photonics R&D placed trials around **2028** and hyperscaler deployment around **2030** (LITE management via Aurelion/PhotonCap, 2026-07-29, paraphrased-not-verbatim; LITE expert call, 2026-07-23). Jefferies expects scale-up CPO/NPO shipments in **2HCY27** and says Greensboro can support **>$5B incremental revenue**, yet is skeptical on timing after the Kyber push-out (Jefferies / Blayne Curtis, 2026-08-07).
- **Scarcity is real today; durability is the valuation question.** The July IR meeting restated demand **~30% above physically available supply** and LTAs through FY27, while also saying Japanese EML capacity rose **eight-fold in 2.5 years** (LITE management via Aurelion/PhotonCap, 2026-07-29, paraphrased-not-verbatim). PhotonCap argues qualified yield at top power bins, not nominal floor space, remains scarce. Jefferies flags the opposite 2028 risk: CPO/NPO delays could leave new capacity oversupplied, with pricing the first casualty (Jefferies / Blayne Curtis, 2026-08-05).

### Scenario map — published thresholds, not a new house model

| Outcome | What it would look like | Likely debate after the print |
|---|---|---|
| **Bull** | June near/above high end; September guide **≥$1.25B**; GM tracks the MS **~51%** bogey; OCS ramp and CPO/NPO timing intact | Earnings revisions extend; scarcity converts into durable yield/mix. |
| **Base / choppy** | June around **$1.0B**; September guide **$1.175–1.20B**; margins improve but no formal pull-forward of Analyst Day targets | Fundamentally fine and above Bloomberg, but below the Jefferies whisper. |
| **Bear** | June merely within guide; September guide around **$1.15B** or lower; GM stalls; transceiver, OCS, pump, substrate, or CPO execution slips | The market pivots from scarcity to execution and eventual InP overcapacity. |

_Thresholds: UBS, 2026-08-10; MS, 2026-08-03; Jefferies, 2026-08-07; Bloomberg, 2026-08-10. Scenario interpretation: Capstone._

### Questions that matter on the call

1. **September bridge:** how much sequential revenue comes from OCS, transceivers, EMLs, scale-across pumps/DCI, and initial CPO?
2. **OCS proof:** did June reach **~$70M**, and are **~$170M quarterly in 1HFY27** plus **≥$200M Dec-quarter run rate** intact (JPM, 2026-05-08; LITE IR, 2026-05-11)?
3. **Margin quality:** when does transceiver GM move from the **20s into the 30s**, and can corporate GM compound as transceiver mix rises (Hurlston, 2026-05-07)?
4. **Capacity versus qualified yield:** reconcile **eight-fold Japanese EML capacity growth in 2.5 years** with demand **>30% above supply**; quantify 200G/lane/UHP yield rather than wafer starts (LITE management via Aurelion/PhotonCap, 2026-07-29; Hurlston, 2026-05-06).
5. **CPO/NPO/MPO:** reconcile **late-2027** scale-up deployment with the internal expert's **~2030** hyperscaler view; explain whether shorter MPO links preserve the same high-power-laser content (LITE management via Aurelion/PhotonCap, 2026-07-29; LITE expert call, 2026-07-23; Jefferies, 2026-08-07).
6. **Pump bottleneck:** is the **3–4x output/revenue ramp over 4–6 quarters** intact (Hurlston, 2026-05-06; JPM management call, 2026-05-07)?
7. **Substrate security:** why add AXT alongside Sumitomo, and how much output depends on Chinese export permits as NPO tightens demand (AXT Q2FY26 call / buy-side notes, 2026-07-31; Jefferies, 2026-08-05)?
8. **Concentration:** identify the exposure behind customers at **26% and 12% of Q3FY26 revenue**, given Citi said NVIDIA was low-single-digit percent and not top-ten (Aurelion/PhotonCap, 2026-07-29; Citi / Papa Sylla, 2026-07-16).

**Positioning/valuation:** UBS scores sentiment **8.5/10**, implied move **~9.7%**, and is **Neutral, $960 PT** (UBS sales commentary relaying David Vogt, 2026-08-10). MS calls the setup more balanced at **~20x FY28 P/E** versus a **~25x buy-side CY28** framing earlier in the quarter (MS / Meta Marshall, 2026-08-03). Jefferies has LITE at **~22x CY28 EPS of $37**, **+130% YTD**, while noting relative underperformance since April 1 (Jefferies / Blayne Curtis, 2026-08-07). JPM is constructive but calls COHR's setup cleaner (JPM / Joseph Cardoso, 2026-08-06). **Good fundamentals are consensus; clearing the whisper while defending margin durability is the non-consensus requirement.**

This wiki has already logged the identical homophone once on this page (*"Intel Light"* = Innolight, 08-27) and has previously inverted a trade on it. Logged as a decode, not as content.** **What IS a LITE-relevant fact from the same passage: **[[NVDA]] pulled AXT in directly** — *"**Last fall, we were asked to go to NVIDIA and visit with management from NVIDIA** because even though **they're NOT A CUSTOMER of ours**, they buy modules that have indium phosphide lasers in the module, and we sell to the companies that can make those things. **Coherent and LUMENTUM come to mind.** So, they just wanted to **PREP US THAT WE NEED TO GO FAST**."* ➤ **NVIDIA reaching two tiers up its own supply chain, past Lumentum, to tell a substrate maker to accelerate — in autumn 2025, before the LTAs. So that market, **it can be GOOD FOR US BOTH WAYS.**"* Fisher: *"the higher the speeds, then you move beyond what gallium arsenide can do from a physics perspective, and you're into the realm of indium phosphide."* ➤ **Relevant to the share arithmetic management gave on 08-27 — EML-based transceivers falling from 70-80% at 800G to 40-50% at 1.6T on SiPh adoption. If a high-end VCSEL/SiPh module still carries an InP detector, the InP CONTENT erosion is smaller than the EML SHARE erosion, which is a mild positive for the *"raw number of EMLs is still increasing"* half of management's own framing. ⚠️ Substrate-level testimony about module content — indicative, not a content model.**

**🆕🔴 2026-08-19 (/run-inbox) — THE ARCHITECTURAL DERIVATION BEHIND LUMENTUM'S OWN OFC SLIDE IS NOW PUBLIC: FUNDA MAPS THE "2:1 TO 10:1" FUTURE SCALE-UP RANGE ONTO TORUS DIMENSIONALITY, AND GF SECURITIES REACHES THE SAME 1.5→6 PORTS-PER-CHIP ARITHMETIC INDEPENDENTLY *WITH A BUY ON LITE*. ⚠️ THE 6D THESIS ITSELF IS NOT NEW TO THIS PAGE — IT WAS LOGGED 08-18 FROM SemiAnalysis; WHAT IS NEW IS THE DERIVATION, THE RATINGS, AND A RESOLVED ATTRIBUTION.**

_(Sources: **FUNDA**, "Deep|Optics: 6D Torus Points to a 4x Uplift in Optical Content per TPU", **2026-08-19** — independent Substack, **no rating/PT/estimates**; and **GF Securities (HK) · Alicia Xia / Jeff Pu / Evan Lee**, "AI Networking: Google's 6D Torus & Beneficiaries", **2026-08-19** — sector report, **LITE rated Buy, no PT given in the note**.)_

- ✅🔴 **THE ATTRIBUTION FIX FIRST, BECAUSE THIS PAGE AND THE OPTICAL-CPO THEME BOTH FLAGGED IT AS AN OPEN QUESTION: the 08-18 "Companion excerpt (⚠️ SOURCE UNCONFIRMED)" that quantified Virgo — OCS Port-to-xPU 0.2:1 → 1:1, module ratio 1:1–1:2 → 1:3–1:4, in-rack switches one → four-six, 130K+ TPUs at ~47 Pb/s — IS FUNDA.** Today's note reproduces it near-verbatim, including the *"precisely the third use case in Lumentum's OFC framework (Figure 2 below)"* phrasing and the *"fivefold increase"* wording, and cross-references FUNDA's own earlier "Deep|AI Infra 2026" report (2026-04-14). ➤ **Cite it to FUNDA — an independent paid Substack, no rating/PT/estimates, NOT a bank and NOT SemiAnalysis. The ratio framework is still Lumentum's own (OFC Figure 2); the Virgo application is FUNDA's.** ⚠️ **One consequence worth stating plainly: what looked like two sources converging on 08-18 was partly ONE source (FUNDA) plus SemiAnalysis. Today's genuinely independent second vote is GF Securities.**
- ⚠️ **UNIT GUARD — "2x" AND "4x" ARE NOT COMPETING ESTIMATES: SemiAnalysis (08-18) said the 6D move doubles ICI LINKS per chip 6 → 12 (2x, copper + optical combined). FUNDA/GF (08-19) say wrap-around OPTICAL PORTS per chip go 1.5 → 6 (4x). Both are correct; FUNDA states both. The optical share rises faster than the link count because ring edge length collapses 4 → 2 and every chip becomes a boundary node. The number that matters to LITE is the 4x on PORTS.**

- 🔴🔴 **THE PART THAT MATTERS MOST FOR THIS PAGE: FUNDA reconstructs Lumentum's OWN OFC roadmap slide from first principles and the numbers fill in exactly.** Lumentum's published framing has **Scale-Up today at a 1.5:1 OCS port-to-xPU ratio** driven by *"topology enablement and cluster resiliency"* (i.e. today's OCS mainly closes the wrap-around links of the 3D torus and reconfigures around failures), with **future Scale-Up at 2:1 to 10:1** driven by *"increased bandwidth; multiple customers and use cases"*, and **Spine/Super-Spine (Scale-Out/Scale-Across) rising from 0.2:1 to 1:1**. ➤ **FUNDA's point: "Lumentum gave the ratio range without spelling out the architectural assumption behind it; higher dimensionality is what fills that range in: 4D maps to 3:1, 6D to 6:1, and 10D to 10:1."** ✅ **This is the first outside-in DERIVATION of the ratio ladder this page has been quoting as a management/marketing claim. It converts "2:1 to 10:1" from a range with no mechanism into a discrete, testable map from torus dimensionality — and it means the top of Lumentum's own range implies a 10-DIMENSIONAL torus, which nobody is forecasting. Treat 6:1 (≈4x today) as the aggressive-but-derivable case and 10:1 as an architectural ceiling, not a base case.**
- **The arithmetic, because the intuitive answer is wrong and the error direction is understatement:** the 64-chip rack stays the building block; in 3D it is **4×4×4**, each dimension a closed ring of edge length k=4, and only the wrap-around link closing each ring leaves the rack optically ⇒ optical ports per chip per dimension = 2/k = **0.5**, so **1.5 across three dimensions** (most chips are interior, copper-wired). In 6D the same 64 chips become **2⁶**: edge length falls to k=2, **both** chips in a two-ring are boundary chips ⇒ **1 optical port per chip per dimension, 6 total, and 100% of chips need optical connectivity.** ➤ **The 4x is two 2x factors multiplied — dimensions ×2 AND ports-per-dimension ×2. Doubling dimensions alone would give 3, not 6.** GF reaches the same place independently: **external optical links per 64-chip block 96 → 384**, per-chip ports **1.5 → 6**; adding scale-out at ~1:2.5 it puts the **aggregate TPU-to-port ratio at 1:8.5**. ⚠️ **GF's 1:8.5 aggregate and Lumentum's/FUNDA's separate scale-up and scale-out ratios are different constructions — do not add them together or treat 1:8.5 as consensus.**
- 🔴 **LUMENTUM-SPECIFIC PRODUCT AND POSITION LINES, verbatim from the beneficiary map:** *"OCS systems: **Lumentum (R300 deployed at Google; next-generation product in customer sampling)**, Coherent (300×300 DLX), InnoLight"* and *"Optical modules and lasers: InnoLight (covering both IMDD and Coherent Lite), **Lumentum (supplying both EMLs and transceivers to Google; 200G EML capacity … through the end of 2026)**, Coherent (EMLs plus transceivers; first to introduce a 400G D-EML)"* (FUNDA). ✅ **Two things land on existing page claims: (1) "R300 deployed at Google" + "next-generation product in CUSTOMER SAMPLING" is third-party corroboration of the OCS franchise and of a follow-on product in the funnel — relevant to the ">$90.0m FY26 filed base → $400m 2H-CY26 → ~$250m/qtr CY27+" ramp this page is measuring; (2) the 200G EML capacity note is consistent with the FY26 10-K's "shift to 200G lane speeds" ASP-up disclosure and with the "all EML capacity locked in LTAs through FY27" line already on this page.**
- **COHERENT LITE IS THE SECOND-ORDER TRADE AND FUNDA DATES IT:** once OCS ratios rise sharply, **insertion loss (Palomar ~2 dB; R300 1.5-3 dB) consumes IMDD's already-limited link budget (~4 dB for FR4, zero margin in the worst case)** — so **Coherent Lite begins adoption with 2.4T systems in 2027, full transition at 3.2T (400G/lane) in 2028**, reinforced as scale-up superpods grow from 9,600 (8t) toward 10,000+ and 20,000+ where reach matters. ➤ **Note the self-referential loop, which is the genuinely useful insight: LITE's own OCS insertion loss is part of what breaks the IMDD link budget and pulls coherent forward. More OCS attach makes the coherent transition earlier, not later.** ⚠️ **Assumed module ASPs in FUNDA's cost table: ~$700 for 1.6T DR8 (IMDD) and ~$1,500 for 2.4T Coherent Lite — a ~2.1x ASP step, and the single most important assumption in the "value uplift exceeds 4x" claim. It is an estimate, not a quoted price.**
- **GF's competitive framing, which is a relative-preference statement this page should hold: GF rates [[LITE]] BUY and [[COHR]] HOLD on the same OCS-supply-chain thesis** — i.e. of the two names this wiki tracks together on CPO/UHP/OCS, GF prefers LITE. ⚠️ **No price target, no estimates and no model travel with the GF note — it is a rating and a thesis only. Do not attach a PT to it.** GF also frames the two roadmaps as convergent rather than competing: *"whether NVIDIA moves toward CPO/NPO or Google expands from 3D to 6D, both are Scale-up strategies aimed at enabling greater inter-chip communication"* — which is the same "NPO/CPO is additive" logic already on this page, arriving from the topology side.
- ⚠️ **WHAT THIS DOES *NOT* DO, AND IT SHOULD BE SAID PLAINLY: it does not validate a single dollar of the OCS ramp. The premise is explicitly "supply-chain speculations" (FUNDA) and GF concedes "the exact TPU generation for the adoption of 6D torus and initial penetration rate remain unclear." FUNDA names TPU 9t and 2027-2028; GF names no generation and no date. Neither cites a Google disclosure, and the ~$13,700/TPU optical+OCS content figure is self-labelled "a very optimistic, upper-bound estimate" covering modules/OCS/cabling only.** ➤ **The page's hard dated test is unchanged and is still the right one: F1Q27 OCS ≥$100m. What this note adds is a SECOND, longer-dated test — Google's official disclosure of the TPU 9t topology and actual per-TPU optical-port counts — which would either underwrite the 2:1-10:1 ratio ladder or leave it as marketing.** [FUNDA](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/funda_taurus.html) · [GF Securities](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/%E5%B9%BF%E5%8F%91%E8%AF%81%E5%88%B8-AI_Networking_Googles_6D_Torus_Beneficiaries-%E5%A4%8F%E9%9B%85%E6%A2%A6-%E8%8B%B1%E6%96%87%E9%A6%96%E5%8F%91%E8%A1%8C%E4%B8%9A-2026-08-19.html)

**🆕🔴 2026-08-18 (/run-inbox) — THE NVIDIA/LUMENTUM JOINT PAPER BEHIND HURLSTON'S CO-INTEGRATED-LASER COMMENTARY IS PUBLIC, AND AN INDEPENDENT ENGINEER'S VERDICT IS BLUNT: THE PACKAGING AND COST STORY IS EXCELLENT, THE *RIN* IS "UNUSABLE."** (Irrational Analysis — "Intel CPO Group is Alive? + Very Interesting NVDA/LITE/AEVA Bonus Papers", 2026-08-17. ⚠️ **Independent engineering substack, NOT a broker — no rating, no PT, no model; self-declared "heavily invested in the semiconductor industry."** The paper is explicitly read *"given the recent commentary by Hurlston on co-integrated lasers for NPO at the earnings call"* — i.e. this is the technical object behind the Q4 FY26 management claim already on this page.)
- **WHAT THE PART IS: a monolithic InP DFB laser array — 9 wavelengths on a 2.25mm × 1mm die — with an INDIVIDUAL SOA PER CHANNEL used both for amplification and beam-shaping, ~100 mW per channel output, a single TEC (so no per-wavelength thermal control), AR coating on the back facet, and ELSFP module packaging.** ➤ **The per-channel SOA is the design decision that matters and it is the OPPOSITE of Intel's shared-SOA approach reviewed on [[INTC]] — one wavelength per amplifier means no four-wave mixing. Lumentum avoided the failure that the reviewer says wrecks Intel's link.**
- 🔴 **THE COST/ASSEMBLY CLAIMS, WHICH ARE THE PART THAT SHOULD MATTER TO THE MODEL — AND THE REVIEWER'S REACTION IS UNPROMPTED SURPRISE: "What the fuck this entire thing only needs ONE ISOLATOR?! This is HUGE COST SAVINGS on the isolators needed AND ACTIVE ALIGNMENT COSTS."** Also **individual SOAs improve coupling efficiency** because bare DFBs "typically need two lenses for effective coupling," and the reviewer notes he has **"heard rumors about the coupling loss Lumentum claims to achieve — 0.5 dB to be precise. Too good to be true but maybe I am just in denial."** ➤ **This is the first outside-in support this page has for the NPO cost argument: one isolator instead of nine, and materially less active alignment, is a BOM-and-labour claim rather than a performance claim — and active alignment is exactly the manufacturing-cost line that decides whether co-integrated lasers can scale. It also gives a concrete hook for the 0.5 dB coupling-loss figure to be tested at the next technical disclosure.**
- 🔴 **THE PROBLEM, AND IT IS NOT A SMALL ONE: "Power variation is excellent! Spacing accuracy is excellent! SMSR is excellent! RIN IS SHIT."** One channel shows a relaxation-oscillation spike; excluding it, RIN lands around **−137 dBc/Hz**, which the reviewer calls **"so bad it is basically unusable for any real-world link,"** dismissing the paper's own mitigating language as **"Nvidia Research cope."** He also flags the structural yield objection: **"I hate monolithic DFB arrays… yield nightmare, can't properly control wavelength spacing, shit reliability,"** and on a 2.25mm × 1mm InP die with 9 wavelengths, **"the yield will be horrific, no need for math."** ➤ **Hold both halves at once: the PACKAGING architecture (per-channel SOA, single isolator, relaxed alignment) is validated, while THIS PARTICULAR monolithic implementation is judged not shippable on noise and yield. That is a research paper behaving like a research paper — and it means Hurlston's co-integrated-laser commentary should be read as a direction of travel, not as a qualified product. The page's late-2027 CPO/NPO timing is not contradicted; the risk it flags is that the first shippable version may not be the monolithic array.**
- **One genuinely useful physics constraint the paper supplies, which the reviewer says he tried and failed to model himself for a year: LASER WALL-PLUG EFFICIENCY OF ~10% IS THE CRITICAL THRESHOLD for CPO/NPO to make sense on energy savings — "the burden is on the laser power efficiency and the system efficiency degrades EXPONENTIALLY as laser WPE drops below 10%."** Also, counter-intuitively, **±10 GHz wavelength-spacing accuracy on a 200 GHz DWDM grid matters for OPTICAL CROSSTALK but is "basically irrelevant for power efficiency"** — while **power flatness (±0.5 dB) does matter, because ring self-heating makes thermal control of the ring bus difficult.** ➤ **Laser WPE ≥10% is now the single number to demand from any CPO/NPO supplier claim on this wiki, including Lumentum's own. It is checkable, it is rarely disclosed, and below it the entire energy-savings rationale for co-packaged optics inverts.** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/IA_18_ago.html)

**🆕🔴 2026-08-17 (COMPANY PRIMARY — FY2026 FORM 10-K, fiscal year ended 2026-06-27, filed/auditor-dated 2026-08-17) — THE FILING SETTLES THE PAGE'S LONGEST-RUNNING MEASUREMENT GAP: OCS NOW HAS AN AUDITED FULL-YEAR NUMBER (>$90.0m), AND BACK-SOLVED AGAINST THE 10-Q IT PUTS THE JUNE QUARTER AT ~$52m — ~26% BELOW THE ~$70m WORKING MARK THIS PAGE HAS CARRIED SINCE MAY. EVERY FORWARD OCS CLAIM ON THIS PAGE IS THEREFORE AN 11–14x RAMP OFF A SMALLER BASE THAN ASSUMED. Separately, the ENTIRE convertible stack is now a CURRENT liability with $757.8m of principal already requested for early conversion.**

_(Source: **Lumentum Holdings Inc., FY2026 Form 10-K**, fiscal year ended 2026-06-27, filed 2026-08-17 — **company primary, net-new to disk.** Local copy: [`LITE_10-K_2026-08-17_FY2026-ended-2026-06-27.pdf`](../LITE/LITE_10-K_2026-08-17_FY2026-ended-2026-06-27.pdf) · [HTML](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/LITE-10K.html).   - ➤ **THE POINT, AND IT LANDS ON THE TWO BRACKETED RELAYS SPECIFICALLY: the two OCS claims this page already flags as broker relays rather than call statements — the ">$1bn 2027 run-rate" and the "~$10bn TAM" — get MORE aggressive once anchored on a filed base, not less. Jefferies' complaint on 08-11 was that the $10bn TAM raise was *unexplained* (*"I hadn't heard them talk about a $10 BILLION OCS TAM… what caused you to increase that?"*); the filing now shows the company shipping **under 1% of it**. Neither relay is refuted — but both are now MEASURABLE, and the burden of proof has moved from the sceptic to the relay.**
  - ✅ **THE CHECKPOINT IS THE SEPTEMBER QUARTER AND IT IS UNUSUALLY CLEAN: from ~$52m, a "first triple-digit OCS quarter" requires ~2x q/q, and the $400M 2H-CY26 figure requires ~$200m/qtr average — ~3.8x the June quarter. If F1Q27 OCS does not print ≥$100m, the $400M figure is already in trouble, and the Thailand floor-space / contract-manufacturer constraint management flagged on 08-13 becomes the explanation rather than a footnote.**
- **Customer concentration is now a FILED annual figure, not quarterly colour: Customer A **26.6%** of FY26 net revenue (15.4% FY25, 18.9% FY24); Customer B **15.0%** (16.0%, 11.4%)** ⇒ **top-two = 41.6% of FY26 vs 31.4% of FY25.** ⚠️ **This supersedes nothing but it CONFIRMS what the page previously had only from a paraphrased management meeting (Aurelion/PhotonCap, 2026-07-29: "two customers at 26% and 12% of fiscal Q3 2026"): the 26% was not a one-quarter artefact — it is the full-year number. The 10-Q's own 9M split was 24% / 16%. See `## Risks`.** **Non-US shipment revenue 79.2% FY26** (81.0% FY25, 73.8% FY24). Backlog carries the standard disclaimer — explicitly *"not necessarily indicative of actual revenue or the level of orders for any succeeding period"* — which is worth quoting whenever the "sold out through 2028" line is used as a revenue anchor. Two retained risk factors matter for the China thread: *"Our ability to sell our products to a previously significant customer has been restricted,"* and certain significant **customers AND suppliers** have products subject to US export controls.
- 🔴 **CAPITAL STRUCTURE — the item with the nearest-dated cash consequence, and it is net-new to this page. ALL series of Notes remain convertible at holder option during Q1 FY27** (the stock cleared **130% of the conversion price for 20 of the last 30 trading days**), so **the entire outstanding balance was reclassified into CURRENT liabilities as of 2026-06-27** (it sat in non-current at 2025-06-28). Aggregate principal moved **$2,514.7m → $1,554.3m** across FY26: **2032 Notes $1,265.0m issued**; **$519.4m settled in CASH**; **$1,124.9m equitized**; **$581.1m via inducement** — leaving only **$289.3m of legacy 2026/2028/2029 Notes** alongside the **$1,265.0m 2032s**.
  - **As of 2026-08-14, early conversion requests total $757.8m of aggregate principal.** Mechanics, verbatim in substance: **principal is settled in CASH; the conversion value in excess of principal is settled in cash, shares, or a combination, at the COMPANY's election.** ⚠️ **House arithmetic, flagged as such: $757.8m is ~49% of the $1,554.3m outstanding at FY-end and it EXCEEDS the entire $289.3m legacy stack — so the 2032 Notes (conversion price **$187.77**, 130% trigger **$244.10**, against a share price near **$926**) are already being converted inside their first year. The cash obligation on principal is the line to run against the **$3.17B** cash balance this page carries from Q3FY26.**
  - **The two FY26 exchange transactions, with their stated dilution: 2026-04-07 — ~**5.7m shares** issued for ~$264.8m (2026 Notes) + ~$209.7m (2029 Notes) of principal plus excess conversion value, **~0.6m shares of INCREMENTAL dilution**; 2026-05-29 — ~**5.0m shares** for ~$650.4m (2028 Notes), **~0.8m incremental dilution.** So ~10.7m shares went out for ~$1,124.9m of principal retired, of which only ~1.4m is characterised as incremental.**
  - **89.7m shares of common stock outstanding as of 2026-08-14**; non-affiliate market value ~**$16,469m** at the FY-Q2 measurement date. ⚠️ **Do not swap this into the EPS model against the ~102M diluted-share count in the Q4FY26 guide (Ali, Q3FY26) — 89.7m is a BASIC count on a single date; the equitizations convert an if-converted diluted drag into actual basic shares, which is a different line.**
- **Housekeeping from the filing, and it dates a framing still live on this page: the two reportable segments (Cloud & Networking / Industrial Tech) applied only PRIOR to FY26 — the structure changed in Q1 FY26.** ⚠️ **The `## Snapshot` above still describes the FY25 two-segment split (~86% / ~14%). That remains true AS A FY25 STATEMENT, and is retained as such, but the 10-K confirms it no longer describes current reporting — the live cut is Components / Systems.**

**🆕 2026-08-17 (READ-THROUGH — FABRINET Q4 FY26 EARNINGS CALL, company primary for FN) — AN INDEPENDENT CONTRACT-MANUFACTURING CONFIRMATION OF THE NPO-BEFORE-CPO SEQUENCING LITE ASSERTED ON ITS OWN CALL, PLUS A CAPACITY LADDER THAT WAS RAISED $11.5bn → $12.5–14bn IN ONE QUARTER.** _(Source: **Fabrinet Q4 FY26 earnings call, 2026-08-17** — Bloomberg **INITIAL DRAFT** transcript, so treat wording as draft-quality. ⚠️ Fabrinet has no wiki page; referenced in plain text. Full readout on [themes/optical-cpo](themes/optical-cpo.md).)_

- ✅ **THE SEQUENCING CLAIM, FROM THE MANUFACTURER RATHER THAN THE COMPONENT VENDOR:** *"**NPO, I think probably represents a more near-term opportunity than CPO** from what we've seen with our customers"*; *"NPO technology sits somewhere between pluggable modules and CPO"*; and on CPO itself, *"**we're working on CPO today with a handful of customers and we're already building devices, albeit not yet at full-scale volumes**."* FN claims positioning *"to be the leader in manufacturing and packaging near-packaged optics devices,"* and notes that as NPO scales to **6.4 and 12.8 terabit**, **manufacturing complexity and yield become critical.** ➤ **This is the cleanest third-party corroboration on the page of the NPO-first framing LITE management gave on 08-12/08-13 (MS: "greater strategic emphasis on NPO"; Hurlston: "no delay in CPO; NPO being brought forward") — and it comes from the layer that has to physically build both, which is a different and harder kind of evidence than a component vendor's roadmap.** It also sits against Jefferies' *"CPO is not happening, it's all MPO"* (08-11) by splitting the difference: NPO nearer, CPO real but not yet at volume.
- **Demand scale: FN Q4 revenue $1.316bn (+45% y/y), above the top of guidance; FY26 revenue $4.6bn (+36%). Data-center revenue $669m, +68% y/y / +13% q/q = 51% of total** and now the largest category, with **ECI products the largest contributor at an annualised run-rate exceeding $1bn.** Comms infra $413m (+40% y/y). **Operating margin 10.9%, the highest in three years.** Q1 FY27 guide **$1.375–1.425bn (+43% y/y at the midpoint).**
- 🔴 **THE REVENUE-CAPACITY LADDER, AND IT WAS RAISED IN ONE QUARTER: each new building of ~1.2m sq ft carries ~$1.8–2.1bn of revenue capacity, and adding the announced additions to the Q4 exit run-rate gets to $12.5–14bn — versus the ~$11.5bn framed on last quarter's call.** The delta is **both more square footage AND rising revenue per square foot.** Named adds: **Building 10 complete by early 2027 (+2m sq ft; 250k sq ft already qualified), Pinehurst 120k sq ft office→manufacturing, a newly commissioned building +200k sq ft, and Great America Place in Santa Clara (two office buildings + ~130k sq ft of manufacturing, more than doubling the Silicon Valley footprint).** ➤ **For LITE this is the assembly/packaging capacity that has to exist for the transceiver and NPO ramps to land — and it is being added faster than the module makers' own guidance. ⚠️ Note the double-edge, consistent with the page's InP-overbuild bear: capacity ladders raised 20% in a quarter are how a shortage ends.**
- 🔴 **FN's ≥10% customers in FY26: Cisco 20%, NVIDIA 16%, Nokia 11%, Amazon 11%** — **Nokia is NEW to the list.** ⚠️ **And the datapoint that cuts against the simple AI-optics narrative: NVIDIA revenue at FN was DOWN 20%+ for the year** (analyst Savageaux on the call, unchallenged by management) **while total revenue grew mid-30s — i.e. "the rest of the business up nearly 60% on that basis."** ➤ **Read carefully before importing it: this is FN's NVDA line, which is a build-location and program-mix outcome, not a statement about NVDA demand. But it is a live reminder that the JPM 08-14 caution on this page — "skepticism on Fabrinet's ability to see an immediate boost from Lumentum's increased 200G EML capacity… continued reliance on [[AVGO]]" — was pointing at something real in FN's mix.** On 1.6T timing at its largest datacom customer, FN refused to answer: *"This is a call you have to talk to NVIDIA about."*
- **Two non-GAAP exclusions worth knowing before anyone quotes FN's EPS: a **+$56.7m non-cash gain** remeasuring an existing investment (⚠️ the investee name renders as garbage in the draft transcript — not named here) and a **−$57.4m provision for Thailand's top-up tax** under the global-minimum-tax framework (⚠️ transcript garbles the body as "the OSC"; almost certainly OECD; first-year application).** **Inventory build was attributed to growth and future ramps, explicitly NOT to a new supply constraint.**

**🆕🔴 2026-08-13/08-12/08-04 (THREE INDEPENDENT MARKS ON THE LASER STACK, INGESTED 2026-08-17) — THE STRONGEST EXTERNAL TECHNICAL ENDORSEMENT OF LITE'S PUBLISHED CPO-LASER DATA THIS PAGE HAS CARRIED, ARRIVING INSIDE A NOTE WHOSE SUBJECT IS [[COHR]]; A COMPETITIVE NEGATIVE THAT PLACES LITE *BEHIND* COHR ON THE InP RAMP; AND THE EML→SiPho PIVOT MECHANICS THAT BOUND THE CHINA-BAN TRADE.**

- ✅ **(1) THE ENGINEERING WALK-THROUGH OF LITE'S OWN PUBLISHED CPO-LASER DATA — bull, and unusually specific (Irrational Analysis, "Coherent Q4 FY26 Earnings — The opposite of Lumentum", 2026-08-13).** ⚠️ **SOURCE CLASS: opinionated independent Substack, self-declared long semis; the note's thesis is a BEAR case on [[COHR]] and the LITE material is the comparison. The engineering claims are the content; the invective is not.** What he reads out of LITE's disclosure: at **400mW optical output the drive-current spread is 1.2A to ~1.38A**; **power-conversion efficiency 21–24%** against an industry want of 25–30% *(which he notes LITE hits at 40C)*, with **peak efficiency at 150–200mW**; **RIN "over 10x lower noise than spec"** where the general CPO-laser spec is **−145 dBc/Hz**; **linewidth "excellent," best at mid-power (200mW), relaxation oscillation ~3GHz plus a feature at 15GHz** — all **specified at 50C, which he calls "frankly a pessimistic case."** On the module: **LITE's OFC 2026 ELSFP live demo showed module-level wall-plug efficiency of 13% against a 10% industry target**, though the demo held laser diodes at 30C with aggressive cooling; **derated to a realistic 40C die / 50C coldplate he puts WPE at ~10–11%, "which is still great."** ⚠️ **His ~80% gross-margin claim on LITE's CPO laser is HIS ESTIMATE from his own BOM model, NOT company-disclosed — and note it is a step DOWN from the 80–90% UHP-laser GM this page already carries from the same author (2026-08-11), so treat the band as ~80–90% author-modelled, unverified.**
- 🔴 **(1b) THE MOAT ARGUMENT, AND IT IS THE MOST USABLE PUSH-BACK ON THE "CHINESE LASERS ARE COMING" BEAR THIS PAGE HAS:** DWDM CPO needs **±30 GHz wavelength-spacing accuracy** (versus very lax CWDM spacing); off-target lasers must be **thermally tuned at ~0.1 nm per kelvin**, which pushes the TEC toward **reverse/heating mode with worse output power, efficiency and electrical noise.** ➤ **So CPO-laser yield is structurally hard for reasons that are physical rather than industrial — which is a materially better-specified version of the "≥100mW brick wall" argument Hurlston gave on 08-13 and of PhotonCap's RIN/COD/SMSR/FIT moat definition (07-29). It is the same conclusion reached from wavelength control rather than from power. ⚠️ Still one engineer's reading of public data, not a yield disclosure — and it is an argument about the DWDM flavour specifically.**
- 🔴 **(2) THE COMPETITIVE NEGATIVE, AND IT BELONGS ON THIS PAGE AS SUCH (FUNDA, "Deep|Optics: The Scale-Across TAM Is Taking Shape", 2026-08-12).** On Nokia's own earnings call, **an analyst compared the InP ramp at [[COHR]], "already doing pretty well," and placed LITE "A BIT BEHIND."** ⚠️ **That is a third party ranking LITE SECOND to COHR on the InP ramp — the opposite direction from the JPM 08-14 callback on this page ("Lumentum is expected to see MORE SIGNIFICANT gross margin expansion in FY2027 due to earlier program ramps"), and consistent with Aurelion's bear #1 (COHR's 6-inch = ~2.25x usable area, "Lumentum still runs 4 inch wafers and is trying to catch up," 07-29). Provenance is thin — a sell-side analyst's aside on a third company's call, relayed by an independent newsletter — but it is the second independent instance of LITE being placed behind COHR specifically on InP wafer size / ramp, and the page should not carry only the flattering side of that comparison. Cross-ref [[COHR]], where the SAME window carries an engineering BEAR case built on COHR's undisclosed CPO-laser yield. The two reads disagree, and the disagreement is the signal.**
- **(2b) FUNDA leads its scale-across note with LITE's own Q4FY26 framing, which is worth having in one place:** *"Expanding inferencing and training applications are driving full-rate connectivity between data centers, while political and regulatory constraints favor smaller, more modular builds. These 2 factors, among others, are **substantially increasing the demand for our pump laser solutions**"* (LITE FY26Q4 call, 2026-08-11). Demand-side corroboration from the same note, all plain-text (no wiki pages): **MACOM lifted its SAM to ~$15bn for 2027 (data center ~$6bn) and flagged a GENERAL SUPPLY SHORTAGE OF InP DFB LASERS, with its own CW laser reaching production late CY2027 at the earliest despite intense customer pull**; **Nokia is ramping its InP fab with yield and volume targets running through 2027** and attributes **EUR 2.8bn of order intake to DCI and scale-across fabrics.** ➤ **Read the MACOM item as the clean bull: the page's most-cited competitive entrant is dated to late-CY27 at the earliest, i.e. outside the FY27 LTA window — the same timing point PhotonCap made on 07-29.**
- **(3) THE BAN MECHANICS, AND THEY BOUND THE TRADE IN BOTH DIRECTIONS (Irrational Analysis, "[Market Memo] Orange Man Attempted Chinese Transceiver Ban", 2026-08-04).** ⚠️ **Independent, opinionated, self-declared positions; no rule text has been published.** Three items relevant here: **(i) only NEW MODELS would be affected and "everyone has already qualed all the models," so it "becomes issue at 3.2T which is far away"** — a materially narrower near-term impact than the tape implied on 08-04, and consistent with management calling the reports *"speculative"* on 08-13; **(ii) the workaround already exists — Innolight, Eoptolink, Accelink and others have built "more than a shell" US-registered entities with the footprint in SE Asia (Malaysia, Thailand, Vietnam), IP licensed in, and nearly all major production (TOSA/ROSA active alignment, PCB assembly, module assembly, burn-in) outside mainland China: "it's easy to get Chinese transceivers that are technically/legally 'not Chinese'"**; **(iii) 🔴 the EML→SiPho pivot mechanics, which is the sharpest content datapoint: "a SiPho transceiver has 50% REDUCED InP CONTENT versus an EML based transceiver while having significantly HIGHER ACTIVE ALIGNMENT content… Everyone has an MZI-based PIC for 1.6T. It is TRIVIAL to pivot manufacturing from EML based to SiPho based. The issue is active alignment capacity/throughput, not the design itself."** ➤ **For LITE that is a direct hit on the InP-content-per-module leg (see `## Risks`), and it explicitly names LITE as a Tower ([[TSEM]]) SiPho-PIC customer — so a pivot away from EML moves LITE's content from its own fab to a foundry's. ⚠️ It also undercuts the Chinese-capacity bear from the other side: the author argues Chinese active-alignment capability is an ORDER OF MAGNITUDE better than the West's ("TFC, Inno, Accelink, Eopto wipe the floor with the west"), so the pivot advantages exactly the vendors a ban is aimed at. Net stance of the note is nonetheless "whatever happens, this is good for Lumentum, Coherent, and AAOI."** Cross-ref [[TSEM]], [[AAOI]], [[AXTI]], [themes/china-export](themes/china-export.md).

**🆕🔴 2026-08-17 (INGEST) — GF SECURITIES PUTS THE FIRST UNIT FORECAST ON NPO OPTICAL ENGINES (11MN 2027E → 40MN 2028E). SIZED FOR LITE IT IS WORTH ~$0.1–0.3BN IN CY27E AND ~$0.8–3.4BN IN CY28E ON THE HOUSE ATTACH CURVE — BUT TAKEN LITERALLY IT IMPLIES MORE NPO REVENUE FOR LITE THAN ITS ENTIRE CURRENT CONSENSUS, WHICH IS THE TELL THAT IT CANNOT BE CROSS-MULTIPLIED WITH BOM CONTENT.**

_(Source: **GF Securities (Hong Kong) Brokerage**, "Figure 1: NPO OE Shipments Forecast (mn units)". **Pasted exhibit — broker CONFIRMED by Felipe at ingest 2026-08-17; ⚠️ NOTE DATE NOT CAPTURED, and the note is not on disk. The 2026-08-17 stamp is the INGEST date, not the publication date.** Full decode, guardrails and the method byproduct live on [themes/optical-cpo.md](themes/optical-cpo.md) — Aug-17 signal.)_

| NPO OE for scale-up (mn units) | 2027E | 2028E |
|---|--:|--:|
| Nvidia's 3.2T NPO | 6 | 19 |
| AWS' 6.4T NPO | 5 | 12 |
| Other CSPs' NPO | — | 9 |
| **Total scale-up NPO OE** | **11** | **40** |

- 🔴 **WHAT IT IS WORTH TO LITE — the conta, and the honest answer is a band, because the exhibit fails the TAM reconciliation gate.** Chain: `NPO optics pool × laser share 33% × LITE socket share 45%`. The 33% is Redburn's laser/non-laser BOM split; the 45% is an **ESTIMATE** grounded in LITE being the lead ELS supplier to [[NVDA]] with [[COHR]] second, ~6 meaningful NPO engagements, and NVDA's 400mW lasers absorbing *"roughly half of Lumentum's future Greensboro capacity"* (2026-08-13).
  - **On the house TAM bridge's own attach curve: CY27E ~$137–340m (1.7–4.3% of the $7,884m BBG consensus), CY28E ~$846m–3.4bn.**
  - **On GF's units taken literally: CY27E ~$2.35bn and CY28E ~$8.1bn — the latter exceeds LITE's entire CY27 consensus revenue.** ⚠️ **Not credible, and stated here precisely so it is not quoted as a house number.**
- 🔴 **THE EXHIBIT IS AN ATTACH CALL, NOT A CONTENT CALL — and that is the useful decode.** At 3.2T per OE and Rubin scale-up of 14.4 Tbit/s/GPU, GF's NVDA line implies **~20% NPO attach on rack-scale GPUs in 2027E and ~39% in 2028E**, versus the house bridge's **10% neutral default** and Redburn's implied **~6.9%** — i.e. **~4× the house attach** — with the AWS and other-CSP legs added on top of that. ➜ **Read it as the bull pole on the attach slider. The disagreement with this page is about penetration speed, not about what a socket is worth.**
- ⚠️ **AND IT DOES NOT RECONCILE. Priced at either self-consistent content pole on the theme page, GF's units imply $16bn of NPO optics in 2027E and $55bn in 2028E — ~7× the house 2028 figure and 62% of BofA's entire CY30 all-optical TAM, two years early.** For GF's units to fit the published TAMs, NPO optics would have to price at **~$32–44/Tbps against ~$374/Tbps for a 1.6T pluggable** — i.e. NPO would have to be ~10× cheaper per bit, which contradicts [[COHR]]'s *"content… very comparable"* and LITE's own parity claim. **The gap is in the units, not the price.**
- ⚠️ **THE READ THAT WOULD DISSOLVE ALL OF THIS: if "3.2T"/"6.4T" is a PLATFORM name rather than the per-OE line rate, every figure above scales down with the true rate.** Per-OE is the strong reading — SemiAnalysis has AMD's MI500 at **9 optical engines of 6.4 Tbit/s uni-di each** (2026-07-24), an exact match for the AWS row — but **confirm against the note text before this enters a model.**
- **WHERE IT SITS AGAINST MANAGEMENT'S OWN FRAMING, which is the part that makes it investable rather than academic:** LITE has said the **~$2bn/quarter run-rate targeted by the Sept-2027 quarter explicitly requires *"not a lot of scale-up optics"*** — carried instead by 1.6T, OCS and scale-out/scale-across pumps (2026-08-13). ➜ **So NPO revenue on any of these estimates is UPSIDE to the base case, not the basis of it.** Even the low end of the house band (~$137m CY27E) arrives against a company that has dated NPO content to **Q4 CY2027 with "a very robust ramp in CY2028"** — so the CY27 number is a stub quarter by construction, and **CY2028 is the year the estimate actually matters.**
- **Corroboration on the AWS leg, which this page has been thin on:** 650 Group's 08-14 panel already put NPO's early adopters as **[[AMZN]], [[NVDA]] and [[AVGO]]** running outside TSMC's COUPE gate. GF independently sizes AWS at **5 of the 11mn OEs in 2027E** — near-parity with NVDA. ➜ **Two independent sources now say the NPO pool is materially broader than NVDA, which is a gap in the house bridge's NVDA-centric Routes A and C regardless of whether GF's level is right.** Cross-ref [[COHR]], [[AAOI]], [[AXTI]].

**🆕🔴 2026-08-14 — THE JPM ANALYST CALLBACK ON LITE + [[COHR]] (Joe Cardoso, 10am ET, hosted by Joshua Meyers) PUTS A SIZE AND A DATE ON SOMETHING THIS PAGE HAS ONLY CARRIED AS A SHORTAGE STORY: LUMENTUM IS GOING AFTER THE MERCHANT CW LASER MARKET, AT 10-20% OF CHIP OUTPUT IN 2027, AT ACCRETIVE MARGINS — AND IT IS USING IT AS PRICING LEVERAGE.**
_(Source: Capstone internal write-up of the JPM post-earnings analyst callback, 2026-08-14; event confirmed by JPM's own invite — "TODAY 10am ET | **Joe Cardoso Analyst Callback on COHR & LITE** (plus recent LITE CEO & AAOI CFO meetings)", Joshua Meyers, 2026-08-14. ⚠️ **Summarised internal note of a SPOKEN call, not a JPM published research note — all wording is paraphrase.**)_
- **🔴 MERCHANT CW LASERS — the number and the motive:** *"Lumentum is **actively pursuing the merchant CW laser market**, driven by **internal manufacturing efficiencies**. This is expected to be a **meaningful contribution in 2027 (10-20% of chip output)** with **accretive gross margins**, and is **providing NEGOTIATION LEVERAGE across their portfolio, including EML pricing**."* ➜ **Two consequences, and the second is the more important one for the wiki. (1) For LITE it is a margin-accretive revenue line with a date. (2) For everyone else, this is the FIRST NAMED MECHANISM BY WHICH THE HIGH-POWER/CW LASER SHORTAGE ENDS — the shortage that has been the standing bull leg on [[AAOI]], [[COHR]] and this page since 08-09 (Irrational Analysis: "high-power laser shortage is really bad… HINT HINT LONG LUMENTUM"). The largest supplier putting 10-20% of chip output into the merchant channel relieves the constraint that made the trade work, and it is doing so partly AS A PRICING WEAPON. Watch this as the leading indicator that the laser lane is normalising.** Cross-ref [[AAOI]], [[COHR]], [[AXTI]].
- **⚠️ THE LTA POSITION, AND LITE IS THE NARROWER OF THE PAIR:** *"**Lumentum: Has LTAs across most of its portfolio, EXCLUDING transceivers** (though hints at some transceiver LTAs for other players). **Negotiated incremental pricing concessions in exchange for long-term commitments**, but **still sees price upside**."* Against **[[COHR]]**, where *"a large percentage of the biz is now covered by long-term agreements, **including transceivers**."* ➜ **COHR has taken the LTA architecture further across its book; LITE keeps transceivers on market pricing. That is a genuine strategic fork: LITE retains more ASP upside and less visibility, COHR the reverse. It also means the "durability bought with price concessions" trade is now explicit at LITE — the concessions are stated, and they are the cost of the backlog.**
- **✅ THE MARGIN VERDICT FAVOURS LITE, AND IT IS THE MIRROR OF MS'S 08-10 PAIR CALL:** *"**Lumentum is expected to see MORE SIGNIFICANT gross margin expansion in FY2027** due to **earlier program ramps (CPO, OCS) and favorable mix**. Coherent's margin improvement is tied to **6-inch Indium Phosphide migration and 1.6T ramps**."* Summary tagging: **BULL (Lumentum) / NEUTRAL (Coherent).** ⚠️ **Morgan Stanley preferred COHR to LITE into the prints on 2026-08-10 (already on this page). Both marks stand — the difference is horizon: MS was trading the print, JPM is underwriting the FY27 margin slope, and it credits LITE with the EARLIER ramps (which is also what the 08-12 sweep established when the $1.25B target model was pulled into the September quarter).**
- **🔴 CPO NOT DELAYED — now a stated consensus, and it is the third corroboration in 48 hours:** *"There is a **broad consensus among industry players that CPO is NOT delayed**, with **Calendar Year 2028 expected to be the material year** for its ramp."* ➜ **Joins the BTG/"Kathy" rebuttal of the SemiAnalysis push-out piece (08-13, logged above) and CEO Hurlston's "no delay in CPO" at the JPM forum (08-13). ⚠️ The honest caveat: "CY2028 is the material year" is a claim that cannot be falsified for two years.**
- **⚠️ MPO — the page now has the flavour-by-flavour ramp ladder from LITE's own framing:** *"MPO is seen as **ADDITIVE to the CPO TAM**, broadening the ecosystem beyond [[NVDA]]… **content is largely similar between CPO and MPO** from a supplier ecosystem perspective."* **MPO flavours (Lumentum's view): (a) ELS (External Laser Source) version — "similar to CPO, early indication for ramp in H2 2027"; (b) EMBEDDED version — laser packaged near the optical engine, ramping 2028, using a MEDIUM-powered laser (lower ASP than ultra-high-power, but HIGHER than 200G EML), "viewed as having a HIGHER BARRIER TO ENTRY."** MPO timelines are *"tied to the inorganic acquisitions/organic pursuits of companies going after the **merchant optical engine opportunity**."* ➜ **Reads directly onto the CEO's 08-13 forum comment that NPO is "maybe 6 meaningful engagements" with a HIGHER optical-lane count than NVDA's CPO — the ASP ladder now gives that engagement count a revenue shape.**
- **⚠️ GEOPOLITICAL, two-sided:** a potential **US ban on Chinese optical vendors** is *"a market share opportunity for Western OEMs, **but not straightforward**"*, with **China retaliation risk given the concentration of INDIUM PHOSPHIDE SUBSTRATE SUPPLY IN CHINA**; hyperscalers are *"already shifting to Western suppliers."* Cross-ref [[AXTI]].
- **Negative read-through in the same call:** *"skepticism on **Fabrinet's** ability to see an immediate boost from **Lumentum's increased 200G EML capacity** in the current quarter, noting past delays and continued reliance on [[AVGO]]. **The June quarter print for Fabrinet is likely to be tricky for datacom.**"* ⚠️ **Note this is a caution on FABRINET's timing, not on LITE's capacity — the EML capacity increase is treated as real.**

**🆕🔴 2026-08-13 (EVENING) — SECOND MANAGEMENT ACCESS THE SAME DAY: BTG PACTUAL HOSTED A POST-EARNINGS CALL WITH "KATHY" (Lumentum executive) — and it carries a REVENUE TARGET THAT IS NOT ON THIS PAGE AND A DIRECT REBUTTAL OF THE SEMIANALYSIS CPO PUSH-OUT PIECE. (Capstone note from the BTG call, 2026-08-13; distinct from the JPM forum with CEO Hurlston earlier the same day, logged below.)**

- **🔴🔴 THE HEADLINE, AND IT IS A PULLED-FORWARD TARGET: Lumentum has ALREADY achieved the $1.25bn revenue milestone and now guides to a ~$2bn PER QUARTER RUN-RATE BY THE SEPTEMBER 2027 QUARTER — earlier than the company's previous framing.** ⚠️ **And the load-bearing qualifier: that $2bn/quarter requires "NOT A LOT OF SCALE-UP OPTICS" (CPO/NPO) in the timeframe — it is carried by 1.6T transceivers, OCS and scale-out/scale-across pump lasers. CPO/NPO is therefore upside to the $2bn, not the basis of it.** ➜ **Cross-read against [[COHR]], which the same week guided to its first >$3bn quarter by the end of FY27 (FQ4 FY27, i.e. ~June 2027). Two vertically-integrated optics vendors are now guiding to step-function quarterly run-rates within a quarter or two of each other, both on 1.6T + OCS rather than on CPO.**
- **🔴 CPO — MANAGEMENT DIRECTLY REFUTES THE PUSH-OUT NARRATIVE, NAMING THE SOURCE.** Against *"recent market speculation (e.g. from the SemiAnalysis report)"* of a CPO push-out, Lumentum reports **INCREASED demand from [[NVDA]] for CPO, specifically for the scale-up timeframe starting 2H CY2027**; **"Nvidia is all in on CPO"**, which is expected to be **required for rack-to-rack connections in the "Rubin Ultra" timeframe (Phase 1 of CPO scale-up)**, becoming **"a very robust ramp in CY2028."** ✅ **This is the SECOND independent company-level rebuttal of the push-out story in 48 hours — [[COHR]]'s Jim Anderson said "absolutely no push-out of CPO demand… we've seen demand INCREASE and demand getting PULLED IN" on the 08-12 call, and Barclays confirms "no indication of a push-out" in COHR's December-quarter CPO revenue. Two competitors, two calls, same rebuttal. The push-out thesis is now carried only by the third-party research, against both suppliers' own statements.**
- **🔴 NPO — A SECOND NAMED CUSTOMER IS COMING, WITH A DATE.** Lumentum expects to **announce another "meaningful" NPO customer within the next ~4 months**, beyond NVIDIA. Mechanics: NVIDIA uses **400mW lasers for external light sources**, which absorbs roughly **half of Lumentum's future Greensboro capacity**; the new engagements may run NPO **"inside the box" with mid-power lasers (120–150mW)** or as external light sources. **NPO content starts shipping Q4 CY2027, with "a very robust ramp in CY2028."** ⚠️ **The differentiating claim: these new NPO opportunities are focused on "scale-up WITHIN the rack," implying HIGHER OPTICAL DENSITY than NVIDIA's initial hybrid approach (some rack-to-rack optical, within-rack copper).** ➜ Consistent with, and sharper than, Hurlston's *"~6 meaningful NPO engagements"* at the JPM forum the same morning.
- **🔴 THE MARGIN MECHANISM, WITH THE NUMBERS THAT MATTER: 1.6T transceiver GM is 30–35% vs 25% for 800G.** So the transceiver mix shift is **margin-accretive within transceivers** even though transceivers remain dilutive to the corporate average. Management is **deliberately capping the transceiver business at no more than ~25% of total company revenue near-term** to protect the blended margin. The larger driver is **"much better capacity utilisation" across the factories, especially the laser-chip fabs**, plus **targeted price increases on EML, pump lasers and narrow-linewidth lasers that are expected to be sticky.** ⚠️ **And the operating-leverage datapoint that reframes the model: Lumentum is hitting its margin goals at a LOWER revenue base than planned — ~$1.25bn vs the prior ~$2bn revenue level required for 50% GM.**
- **OCS — on track, and the constraint is floor space, not demand.** **$400m of OCS revenue in 2H CY2026 reaffirmed**, targeting a **~$250m/quarter run-rate in CY2027 and beyond**. Demand specifically from **[[GOOG]]**, which management believes is *"potentially driven by [[ANTHROPIC]]'s needs, although Lumentum only sees Google as the direct customer."* ⚠️ **The bottleneck is manufacturing scale: Lumentum is exploring CONTRACT MANUFACTURERS to overcome floor-space limitations in THAILAND for OCS production — an execution and margin risk that is net-new to this page.**
- **Customer concentration is broadening, with a second hyperscaler named-but-unnamed:** **Google is the "primary hyperscaler"** for both 1.6T transceivers and OCS; a **"number two hyperscaler" is also ramping 1.6T transceiver demand**. ⚠️ **Note the supply-chain-control signal: hyperscalers have asked Lumentum to build transceivers INTERNALLY rather than through CMs** — the opposite of the OCS situation above, and a sign these parts are treated as strategic.
- **FCC / China transceiver restrictions — the most balanced management framing on this page, and it is NOT a straight positive.** Management calls the reports **speculative**; if implemented it would be *"very disruptive for the industry"* short-term because **Chinese-headquartered companies produce ~65% of the world's transceivers**, and the definition is messy (**manufacturing location vs company HQ**). ⚠️ **The commercially honest part: Lumentum "would like to have this business" but it "needs to be margin-sensible, as current transceiver margins might not justify taking on all volume"** — i.e. **the company would not simply absorb the displaced volume at prevailing transceiver economics.** Lumentum says it is in contact with the US government advocating **"a more gradual and thoughtful shift"**, also mindful of its **significant Chinese laser-chip customers**. ➜ **Net: long-term positive for US suppliers, short-term disruptive for the industry, and explicitly conditional on the policy leaving a workable margin structure. Cross-ref [[COHR]], where BofA reaches the same conclusion from the outside ("COHR would be in the best position to benefit as it is third in global share").**

**🆕 2026-08-13 — MANAGEMENT ACCESS: CEO Michael Hurlston at a HW/Semis Mgmt Access Forum. Most of the substance CORROBORATES the 08-12 sweep below and is not repeated here; logged below are only the genuinely incremental items — the sharpest being that the AXTI deal was FORCED BY A CPO DEMAND SURGE, and a quantified China high-power-laser gap.** _✅ **ATTRIBUTION RESOLVED 2026-08-14 (wiki-ingest): the host and note-taker are now identified — this is J.P. MORGAN's Hardware & Semiconductors Management Access Forum, and the notes were circulated by JPM's Joshua Meyers ("My AAOI & LITE Notes from our HW/Semis Mgmt Access Forum", 2026-08-13 11:34 ET). The forum date is 2026-08-13, not approximate. The LITE session ran 8:30–9:15am PT and the [[AAOI]] session 11:00–11:45am PT — both time windows match this page's and the AAOI page's records exactly, which is what identifies them as the same event. JPM's own AMAT note of 2026-08-14 independently refers to "our Hardware & Semis Management Access Forum" held "earlier this week," and JPM's covering analyst for the space is Joe Cardoso.** Original caveats stand and are NOT withdrawn: Meyers flags **"THESE ARE MY RAW NOTES, so apologies for any mistakes"** — so wording remains **paraphrase, not verbatim**, and it is **sales commentary, not JPM Research**. Speaker attribution hard: CEO Michael Hurlston._
- **🔴 WHY THE [AXTI](AXTI.md) DEAL HAPPENED — a demand-driven forced move, not an opportunistic supply hedge.** *"**Demand signal on CPO has gone up a lot into the second half of the year, in a very surprising way; they had to do the AXTI deal because of that.**"* Supporting mechanics: they are **also using the UK fab and can route through Japan**; **high-power lasers come first from California, then a second wave from the UK, then a third from Virginia**; and the AXTI arrangement is **"a prepay that will be bought down."** Critically: **"substrates are still *a tension point*, and demand only seems to be going in one direction."** ⚠️ This is the counterparty's own causal account of the 2026-07-29 AXT capacity reservation (min annual InP capacity through 2031, $43.5m + $43.5m deposits) — read-through logged on [AXTI](AXTI.md).
- **🔴 THE CHINA HIGH-POWER-LASER GAP, QUANTIFIED IN mW — and it partly CONTESTS the "China has localized CW" claim.** Hurlston: **"haven't seen a lot of lasers coming out of China"**; LITE's target band is **70–100mW**; he **"doesn't see Chinese at 100mW"** — *"they're probably there, but they don't see Innolight saying they're getting 100mW from a competitor"* — and there is **"a big gap for NPO/CPO from 100mW."** ⚠️ **Reconciliation required** against the Jefferies expert call on [optical-cpo](themes/optical-cpo.md), which scored Chinese **CW-laser localization as "done"** (Yuanjie displacing Sumitomo into Innolight/Eoptolink/COHR). Both can hold simultaneously: **commodity/low-power CW is localized, the ≥100mW NPO/CPO tier is not** — which is the tier that carries the ASP. That power-tier distinction is the reconciliation and it matters for how durable the LITE/COHR light-source moat actually is.
- **NPO — the engagement COUNT and the lane-intensity claim (sharper than the "wide variety of engagements" already logged 08-12).** **~6 meaningful NPO engagements** leading to "nice revenue streams down the pipe" — and the load-bearing claim: **the intensity of those engagements (number of optical lanes) is actually HIGHER than NVDA's**; *"# of NPO optical lanes from north to south is considerably higher — more opportunity, more customers."* Mechanism for why NPO doesn't cannibalise the CPO thesis: **NPO is the "Tesla 3 versus Model X"** — most of the performance, cheaper — **but "eventually everyone will have to go to CPO,"** and building NPO **develops the confidence and competence that leads NPO players to move to CPO over time.** On CPO itself: **"no delay in CPO; NPO being brought forward"** — ⚠️ note this points the **opposite way from [AAOI](AAOI.md) management the same week** ("end-2027 some orders, but it's a 2028 story"); see the reconciliation on [optical-cpo](themes/optical-cpo.md).
- **ELS vs OE economics — the granular version.** **ELS uses the higher-power laser (healthy ASP at 400mW)**; **OE uses lower power** — same reliability/technical difficulty, slightly cheaper, so **laser content goes down** — but **packaging makes up "quite a bit"** so **ASP likely ends up similar** (they haven't formally mapped the delta). **ELS more than doubles ASP at a slightly lower margin**, is **margin accretive**, and is explicitly **"a beachhead."** ⚠️ The competitive caveat, which is net-new: **the packaging barrier to entry is LOWER for ELS than for OE**, so expect **more competition (Innolight, Eoptolink)** — but LITE holds **>50% of the content** (lasers being the bulk), so it expects to compete **at healthy margin levels.** Strategy: stay **chip-and-component-first**, but use packaging (incl. **heat management**) to grow dollar content, and **extend beyond lasers to PIC over time.**
- **CW at 1.6T is the near-term vein; 3.2T reverts to EML.** **Huge CW opportunity at 1.6T — [GOOG](GOOG.md) and [NVDA](NVDA.md) "already ramping"** — while **CW at 3.2T is "going to be difficult" and the market shifts back to EML.** ✓ Independently corroborates the Jefferies expert's 3.2T call (EML re-takes the lead as SiPh's frequency ceiling binds) **from the incumbent's own mouth**, and sharpens the May-2026 Hurlston "3.2T reverts to EML" line already in Risks. Supply mechanics: **CW is only ~10–20% of what they do in EML**; **EML on track for +50% y/y**; the change is **output capability — fabs running more efficiently than planned at the start of the year** — and they are **taking some incremental output for CW** because **customers are "really short CW,"** using that as leverage to **partner in other parts of the business.**
- **Product-line ranking, and pumps overtaking narrow linewidth.** **Narrow linewidth has been one of their four most important businesses and is by far the leading volume shipper — but pump lasers will overtake it next year.** Pumps are **one of their best gross-margin products**, they hold **~80% market share and "can't keep up,"** and **inferencing is the big driver** ("demand has been a huge surprise because of this"). Nuance worth keeping: demand is **still going up, but not as much as it has over the past 3 months** — and **leadtimes are even longer** (hence better LTAs) because this capacity takes longer to build. **WSS** is also a big business but **not growing as strongly.**
- **⚠️ LTA COVERAGE GAP — transceivers are the uncovered ~25%.** *"We really like LTAs"* because they give comfort to invest, and **a lot of the business is LTA-covered — but transceivers (~25% of revenue) is one area they DON'T have a lot of LTAs.** **New pricing agreements really start to kick in at the end of the year**, and LTAs come into play **when customers are really constrained** (contract length, pricing, new products). ⚠️ Reads directly against the TD Cowen "LTAs give visibility… until they don't" bear already on this page: the uncovered slice is precisely the **lowest-margin, most-competitive** line.
- **Transceiver margin target raised in the telling.** *"Don't love the margins of this business."* Better on **1.6T because they're first** and demand is strong (mix helping), plus **manufacturing optimisation — they're "crappy" now.** Target: **"eventually get to a mid- to high-30s margin here."** ⚠️ vs the page's standing datapoint of transceiver GM **"in the 20s" with line of sight to the 30s** (Hurlston, 2026-05-07) — this is an **upgrade of the transceiver-margin ceiling**, not a restatement. Old value retained above and in Changelog.
- **FCC / policy — same thread as [AAOI](AAOI.md), same ask.** They and others are **helping government understand the supply chain**; government is **worried about Chinese content and the associated risks**, is **taking its time**, and they hope it **works thoughtfully to create a clear offramp.** Separately: *"Lots of Chinese companies provide transceivers, so if the government wants to change things they need a clear offramp… avoid disrupting the supply chain given how strong demand is."* The read on official posture: **the approach seems to be about keeping the industry together**, with government aware it is disruptive and **possibly considering ways it can help.**

**🆕🔴 2026-08-12 — THE MORNING-AFTER SELL-SIDE SWEEP, AND IT RESOLVES THE PAGE'S #1 OPEN QUESTION IN THE BULLS' FAVOUR: MANAGEMENT RAISED THE OUT-YEAR OPERATING-MARGIN TARGET TO 40–44% (FROM 38–42%) AND PULLED THE $1.25B TARGET MODEL INTO THE SEPTEMBER QUARTER. Every house raised numbers; three raised PTs.** The call detail (not in the release, and therefore net-new to this page):

- **🔴 OUT-YEAR OM TARGET RAISED: management "is already expecting the out-year operating-margin target to track closer to 40%–44% (vs. 38%–42% prior)"** (JPM / Joseph Cardoso, 2026-08-12). ⚠️ **This is the first formal move in the long-term margin frame since the March-2026 Analyst Day and it directly answers the "margin durability through the transceiver ramp" debate that the print itself left open.**
- **The target-model timeline, restated and pulled in (MS / Meta Marshall, 2026-08-12):** the **$1.25B mid-term target** thrown out in March was framed as 9–12 months away (i.e. Dec-26 to Mar-27 quarter) — **guided to in the September quarter instead**. On the **$2B long-term target** (originally 9–12 months after the mid-term), management said it **does not need the Greensboro facility operational**, with the target reachable in the **September-to-December 2027 quarter window**. Barclays corroborates: *"Pulled-in the $1.25B revenue guidance from OFC to the September quarter from Dec-March prior and expects the $2B revenue mark to still follow 9-12 months after"* (Barclays / Tom O'Malley, 2026-08-12).
- **🔴 NPO IS ADDITIVE TAM, NOT A CPO SUBSTITUTE — and it has a date.** Management said **NPO is additive to the TAM, ramping in late 2027 for scale-up applications**, while **CPO production plans "remain on track, with an increased demand signal"** (JPM, 2026-08-12). Barclays: **scale-up CPO at the lead customer (Barclays: "we think NVDA") on track for shipments 2H CY27 for CY28 deployments**; other customers *and NVDA* are looking at NPO-based solutions **"which will be incremental to LITE's scale-up optical opportunities"** (Barclays / O'Malley, 2026-08-12). MS adds the qualitative texture: management sees **"a wide variety of engagements on NPO, even from their lead CPO customer,"** across **mid-power to high-power lasers**, and cautions that **"one shouldn't naturally assume that competitive differentiation / margin opportunity is less with the mid-power lasers"** (MS, 2026-08-12). ⚠️ **This is the direct answer to the 08-07 Jefferies risk logged in the Changelog below ("you might not need a high-powered laser" for MPO/NPO scale-up) — management says the mid-power lane is not obviously less differentiated. It is a management assertion, not evidence; the dispute stays open, but LITE has now answered it on the record.**
- **FOUR INCREMENTAL OPPORTUNITIES "largely absent from, or only lightly embedded in, the current framework" (JPM, 2026-08-12) — net-new to this page:**
  1. **Merchant CW lasers** — expected to be **a meaningful portion of datacom chip revenue in coming quarters**, *without* disrupting the EML capacity-expansion trajectory, and **at accretive margins**. ⚠️ Note the reversal of polarity: this page's TD Cowen bear treats the CW/SiPh shift as the *oversupply* vector; LITE is now selling CW **merchant** and **pricing up on superior yields**.
  2. **ELS — first order secured for delivery in calendar 2H27, at above-corporate margins.**
  3. **In-tray OCS use cases — 2028 timeframe.**
  4. **Pump lasers — shipments +80%+ y/y in F4Q, multiple three-year LTAs signed with embedded price levers, supporting a 4x shipment increase over the next several quarters.**
- **🔴 MANAGEMENT PUSHED BACK DIRECTLY ON THE CHINA/InP-CAPACITY BEAR:** it has seen **"no impact from newly announced Chinese InP capacity,"** citing differentiation in **EMLs, high-/mid-power lasers, and even CW lasers (where it is pricing UP on superior yields)**, and cautioned that **several of those Chinese suppliers are not yet delivering into the market** (JPM, 2026-08-12). ⚠️ Same evidentiary caveat as the 07-29 IR meeting: this is management's own assessment, unverified externally.
- **The operating detail behind the 50.4% GM (Jefferies / Blayne Curtis, 2026-08-12):** **EML demand still running 30%+ ahead of supply**; **pump lasers sold out** against the planned 4x increase, **underwritten by largely take-or-pay agreements**; **200G EMLs above 25% of EML revenue, on a path to majority volume by mid-2027**; **1.6T Tx adoption intensifying from fiscal Q1**. **GM crossed 50% well ahead of the $2B quarterly run-rate management had originally attached to that milestone** — Jefferies attributes it to **utilisation, richer laser mix and yield improvement ("fundamental and durable")**, while acknowledging **backlog repricing added a tailwind that should keep flowing as LTAs reset**. MS's version of the mix math: **200G at ~25% of units and "probably more like 40% of revenue," from 5%/10% (unit/revenue) two quarters ago**, with LITE on track for a **50% increase in EML units y/y by end of calendar year**; **the CW-vs-EML margin gap is closing as they shrink the die size**.
- **🔴 OCS — the net-new competitive datapoint: "the view that they will win MAJORITY SHARE AT GOOGLE in C27 surprised us"** (Jefferies, 2026-08-12). OCS **on track to deliver its first triple-digit revenue quarter in F1Q**, though **no upside to the $400M figure for 2H26**. ⚠️ Majority share at Google in C27 is a step up from the "three customers / sole-source-ish" framing this page carried and is the single most valuable OCS datapoint since the Q2FY26 call.
- **⚠️ THE BEAR THAT SURVIVED, AND IT IS A MARGIN-SHAPE BEAR NOT A DEMAND BEAR (Jefferies, 2026-08-12):** *"GMs in the Cloud Light business are improving on yield, utilization, and initial 1.6T ASPs, but **the steep ramp of that lower-margin revenue likely softens the potential for margin expansion over the NTM** before the UHP laser opportunity for scale-up begins to unfold more meaningfully in 2H27."* Same shape as the standing transceiver-dilution bear, now dated: **the dilution window is the next ~12 months, and the offset is 2H27.**
- **⚠️ On CPO slippage, a specific and net-new timing mark (Jefferies, 2026-08-12): "while NVDA's plans for scale up deployments may push to 1H 2028, LITE's timeline has not changed, with ultra-high power laser shipments still expected to begin in 2H27."** ➜ Read precisely: **the customer may slip while the supplier ships on time** — LITE builds to its own schedule against take-or-pay. That decouples LITE's revenue timing from the NVDA deployment date the whole CPO debate has been anchored on.
- **What still didn't happen: no formal NPO deal announcement.** MS: management said **formal announcements "may not be made for a couple of quarters,"** which is why the reaction stayed muted; MS explicitly names the next catalyst as **"a more formal contract / insight into how NPO opportunity plays out, and what type of margin profile that business would carry,"** and does not see a clear catalyst **"until the fall and into OFC next year."** MS also concedes its own error plainly: *"We have been far too conservative on LITE given perceptions that margin would eventually face pressure from capacity coming on in the space."*
- **PT/rating moves, 2026-08-12:** **JPM (Cardoso) OW, PT $1,280 ← $1,165** (Dec-27); **Jefferies (Curtis) BUY, PT $1,200** (+46% to PT, price ref $820.59); **MS (Marshall) Equal-weight, PT $1,000 ← $900**; **Barclays (O'Malley) OW, PT $1,000 unchanged** (+21.9%); **R&Co Redburn (Mike Harrison) Buy reiterated** — *"Strong quarter, better outlook,"* and Redburn is the one house that puts a unit on the CPO content: **"we estimate 25m lasers per GW"** for scale-up CPO. **Estimate revisions — Jefferies: CY26 rev +4% / CY27 +16%, CY26 EPS +11% / CY27 +26%, now +14%/+17% vs consensus; CY26 EPS to $15.17 from $13.70.**
- **⚠️ The one dissonant read on the day: MS's "why stay where you are, if it has been the wrong place?"** — MS lifted the PT but **held Equal-weight**, arguing the stock **already discounts high-teens CY28e buyside expectations** and that **potential FCC rules only elongate the margin runway** (explaining "in part the 40% run off bottom"). So the marginal seller's argument is now purely **positioning and catalyst timing**, not fundamentals. Barclays holds the same structural view it has carried: **"volume ramp of CPO is closer to the end of the decade,"** with **NPO "an interesting solution to bridge the gap."**

**🆕🔴 2026-08-11 (AMC) — Q4FY26 PRINTED: A CLEAN BEAT ON EVERY LINE AND A SEPTEMBER GUIDE ~8% ABOVE THE STREET, AND THE STOCK WENT NOWHERE. The target model arrived "more than a quarter ahead of schedule" (LITE Q4FY26 release / Hurlston, 2026-08-11).**

| Metric | Q4FY26 actual | Street | Guide | Delta |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|
| Revenue | **$1.006–1.010B** | $987.7–990M | $960M–$1.01B | beat, at/above high end |
| Components | **$649.4M** | $637–643M | — | beat |
| Systems | **$356.9M** | $344.5–350M | — | beat |
| Non-GAAP GM | **50.4%** | 48.5–48.8% | — | **+~600bps vs Street** |
| Non-GAAP OM | **36.6%** | 35.2–35.5% | 35–36% | beat, above guide |
| Non-GAAP EPS | **$3.23** | $2.97 | $2.85–$3.05 | **+28c / +9%** |

**F1Q27 (Sept) guide — the line everyone was waiting on: revenue $1.225–1.275B (midpoint $1.250B) vs Street $1.151–1.16B (~+8%), non-GAAP OM 39.5–40.5%, non-GAAP EPS $4.05–4.35 (midpoint $4.20) vs Street $3.58–3.61 (~+16–18%)** (LITE release, 2026-08-11; consensus per UBS/TMTB/Vital Knowledge, 2026-08-11). Implied sequential revenue growth **+24%**.

- **Hurlston's framing, verbatim from the release:** *"Lumentum is positioned at the heart of a secular industry shift. As AI compute workloads increase in both speed and bandwidth, data center architects are turning to optical links as a primary means of connectivity. While our Q4 results demonstrate broad-based traction, key growth drivers such as **OCS solutions and our cloud module business, where we are advancing 1.6T adoption**, are beginning to layer in. **Increasing demand for ultra-high-power CPO lasers, an initial order for ELS modules**, as well as our breadth of **NPO engagements** are the first signs that **optics are starting to penetrate in-rack connectivity, significantly upping our optical TAM** … our trajectory continues to accelerate as AI demand drives our Q1 revenue guidance midpoint to $1.25 billion, **reaching our target model more than a quarter ahead of schedule**"* (LITE Q4FY26 PR, 2026-08-11, relayed by UBS / Robert Ruple, 2026-08-11 — sales commentary, not UBS Research).
- **🔴 The scoreboard against this page's own bogey table — the guide beat every published broker base case and landed just under the Jefferies/whisper bar.** UBS bogey $1.15–1.20B ✅ cleared; MS $1.175–1.20B ✅ cleared; MS explicitly said pulling the **$1.25B run-rate forward to the Sept quarter** was the bull outcome but *"unlikely to see this quarter"* — **LITE did exactly that** (MS / Meta Marshall preview, 2026-08-11 via MS TMT desk). Jefferies/whisper $1.25B+ ⚠️ met at the midpoint, not exceeded. **The whisper is why a 28c beat and a 16% EPS guide-beat produced a flat-to-down tape (-0.4% to -0.7% after hours).**
- **JPM's read on the non-reaction is the most useful sentence of the day:** *"the tepid response feels like an **expectations problem**: last week, a few folks said my Bars seemed aggressive, but **by the end of the day today folks were saying the company had to do $1.3b in revenue guide to work**… so this feels somewhat **reminiscent of the TXN print**"* — the EPS beat was **13c ahead of JPM's Buyside Bars** and GM beat consensus by **610bps** (JPM / Joshua Meyers, "LITE… First Blush", 2026-08-11 — sales commentary, not JPM Research).
- **UBS desk:** *"Reports a beat and raise. All-in-all it seems like fairly solid print with the stock flat/down presumably on **elevated expectations** despite Q4 results"* — mgmt cited **broad-based OCS and cloud-module traction as the industry moves to 1.6T**, plus **rising CPO-laser demand and initial ELS-module orders** (UBS / Robert Ruple, 2026-08-11). Impl vol into the print **~9.06%**.
- **Vital Knowledge:** *"the numbers and guide are both great, but **expectations were fairly elevated**"* (Adam Crisafulli, 2026-08-11).
- **⚠️ What the print did NOT settle** — the two structural debates this page carries are still open: (1) whether transceiver-heavy capacity additions keep diluting incremental GM (the 50.4% print argues no, for now), and (2) **scale-up CPO timing**, where management gave "first signs" language on in-rack penetration and an initial ELS order but no dated scale-up ramp. The "significantly upping our optical TAM" claim is a management assertion, not yet a quantified TAM revision.

**🆕 2026-08-11 (pre-print) — A BUY-SIDE EXPERT CALL SET THE BAR AT $1.3B AND EXPLAINED, IN ADVANCE, EXACTLY WHY THE PRINT WOULD NOT WORK (Papa Sylla, buy-side analyst, call relayed 2026-08-11 — before the AMC print).** This is the cleanest pre-print bogey statement on the page and it was right about the mechanism:
- **The pattern he described:** LITE *"typically beats current earnings by ~5% and guides the next quarter ~10% above expectations."* On that arithmetic **"1.15 might not be good enough because that's where the street is… It has to be at least 1.2, 1.3. I think what I heard is like 1.3. That would allow them to beat the market by 10%."** ➜ **Actual guide midpoint $1.25B — inside his range but below the $1.3B whisper he heard. Result: flat tape.**
