- **What he wanted from the guide (and got only partially):** *"what they are looking to see as well is the CPO numbers to start to kind of play an important role, particularly in the guidance in September because that's when **CPO for scale-out is coming in with the NVIDIA Spectrum switches**."* His own run-rate estimate for **LITE's CPO portion: ~$100M in calendar 3Q and $250M+ in 4Q** (his estimate, explicitly hard to map 1:1 given internal-vs-supplied mix). Scale-up CPO he still dates **late 2028 / 2029** — later than management's late-cal-2027, closer to the internal expert's ~2030 already logged on this page.
- **Supply/pricing:** reiterates LITE **sold out through 2028** and argues that constraint supports **~15% price increases** on optical components.
- **🔴🔴 CORRECTED 2026-08-13 (re-read of the raw call transcript) — THE SPEAKER'S "LIGHT" IS INNOLIGHT, NOT LUMENTUM, AND THE 21h INGEST DECODED IT BACKWARDS. THE CORRECTED READING REVERSES THE SIGN OF THE CHINA-BAN TRADE FOR THIS PAGE.** The prior version of these two bullets (share ~35–40% attributed to LITE; "de-risking could take hyperscaler allocation to LITE from 40% → 30%") is moved to `## Changelog` (2026-08-13). What he actually said, decoded against the surrounding text:
  - **The ban target is a *Chinese* transceiver maker — the US would not ban a US supplier.** *"The chance of Trump actually banning China… that would be in my view such a bad move."* **"Light" = Innolight**, put at **~35–40% of the transceiver market**, so *"if you remove [Innolight] from the market you are severely increasing the supply-demand imbalance"* and **NVDA and the hyperscalers push back**. Cross-check: Damnang puts Innolight at **~27% of the global data-center optical-transceiver market with ~90% of revenue earned outside China** (Damnang / "The Optical Edge" Substack, 2026-08-04) — same order of magnitude, different basis.
  - **🔴 THE PART THAT IS ACTIVELY BEARISH FOR LITE AND WAS LOST ENTIRELY: INNOLIGHT IS LUMENTUM'S BIGGEST LASER CUSTOMER.** *"For [[COHR]] I totally agree, Coherent in particular would benefit from this… but for Lumentum, most of their business is still the laser, not the transceiver. So if you move [Innolight out of] the market — [Innolight] is a big customer, probably their biggest customer in the laser — then you are hoping Coherent absorbs all of those lasers quickly as well. So for Lumentum it's not as good as for Coherent."* ➜ **A ban on Chinese transceivers removes LITE's largest merchant EML buyer while handing COHR transceiver share. That is why the tape split the way it did on 08-04: COHR +10%+ vs LITE +5–7% (his own observation; actual prints were COHR +12% / LITE +9%).**
  - **The retaliation vector, correctly attributed:** China supplies **~70%** of the feedstock, *"so if you ban [Innolight] in the US, they will ban Lumentum and others from getting the [InP] in the first place"* — i.e. the retaliation lands on **LITE's input**, not on LITE's market access. He frames the ban chatter as **"tactical negotiation."**
  - **The 40% → 30% number is the hyperscalers' allocation to INNOLIGHT, not to LITE:** *"in two years, when they were thinking about, okay, we allocate 40% to [Innolight], now might decrease it to 30% because they want to de-risk from China. So that is positive."* ➜ **This is a share-gain tailwind for LITE and COHR, not a share leak away from LITE.** ⚠️ **Net effect on the page's China thread: LITE's *access* risk is lower than previously logged and its *demand* risk is higher (loss of the Innolight laser socket). The two partially offset; COHR is the unambiguous winner in his framing.**
- **Growth frame he attributes to the sell-side/buy-side consensus:** FY26→FY27 sales **+100%**, FY27→FY28 **+80%** on CPO maturation.
- _Source hygiene: this is a buy-side expert/client call summary, not sell-side research; treat the run-rates and share figures as the speaker's estimates. Logged because it is the pre-print bogey that explains the tape._

**🆕 2026-08-10 (PM) — THE PRINT WAS TOMORROW (Tue 2026-08-11 AMC) AND THREE INDEPENDENT SETUPS LANDED TODAY. The common thread: the numbers are not the debate; the debate is CPO timing and margin durability.**

**(1) UBS — Neutral, PT $960; the only house here with a formal below-buyside caveat (UBS / David Vogt, via Robert Ruple, 2026-08-10).** Impl vol **~9.7%**, desk sentiment **8.5/10**.
- **Sept-quarter (JunQ) model:** revenue **"at least $1,003M"** vs the **$960M-$1,010M guide**, gross margin **"at least 48.5%, up ~60bps q/q"**, and — because supply constraints mean *"Lumentum likely under-ships underlying demand"* — **operating leverage drives diluted EPS 3-4% above UBS's $3.00**.
- **The December-quarter guide is the actual event:** UBS expects **$1,150M-$1,200M** (UBSe $1,149M at the midpoint, **~120% y/y growth**) but flags *"**investor expectations are slightly higher despite constraints**."*
- **🔴 The bear that is not about this quarter:** *"**growth in the Sept and Dec quarter is likely to be 'PEAKISH' as comps become much tougher**."* Plus: **OCS ramping to "at least $400M across the Sept and Dec quarters combined"**, and the two things pressuring the multiple — **"concerns that CPO deployments could be pushed out and INCREASING SUPPLY OF InP"** — *despite* strong positive EPS revisions over three months. ⚠️ Note the InP point cuts directly against the [[AXTI]]/substrate-squeeze thesis this wiki carries.
- **(2) MS — cautious-leaning preview, and prefers COHR on margins (Morgan Stanley / Meta Marshall, via Amir Amerian desk note, 2026-08-10).** Buyside expectations *"have become balanced"*; she assumes **LITE's Sept revenue and GM guide come close to / in line with the March-2027 targets respectively**, but sees **less chance LITE formally pulls forward the Analyst Day targets**, and **more margin upside for [[COHR]]** in this set. Management commentary expected to emphasise **NPO rather than CPO**, but *"the results will depend more on **conventional pluggable capacity, yields and customer deployments**"* — and *"this set of results **will NOT solve the longer-term debates**: execution on the CPO ramp and durability of margins."*
- **(3) The buy-side bogey list (Crux Capital / Gaetano, "$LITE Pre-Earnings Thoughts", 2026-08-10) — useful precisely because it is a bogey sheet, not a model.** *"The bar has been raised… both of these stocks are **up around 50% since the late-July lows**."* Named bogeys: revenue **>$1B** (*"such a psychological number"*); the **>$2B quarterly revenue + ~40% OM long-term target** reaffirmed or pulled in; **EML undersupply still ~30%** and **>50% y/y EML unit growth by the Dec-2026 quarter** on track or earlier; **200G EML ASP ~2x 100G**; **cloud transceiver revenue grew >40% q/q last quarter** with the business to **double over 4-5 quarters** and LITE *"front of line on 1.6T"*; **narrow-linewidth laser shipments +120% y/y and pump lasers +80% y/y** last quarter with **undersupply worse than EMLs (>30%)**; **scale-across component market $1.5B → ~$4B by 2029**; **OCS ~$400M in 2H CY26** against a **~$10B OCS TAM**; scale-out CPO *"meaningful revenue in the December quarter, with a multi-hundred-million-dollar purchase order due during H1 2027"*; **scale-up requiring ~10x the optical content of scale-out** (Hurlston), with **NPO potentially involving MORE optical lanes than CPO**; **Greensboro/6-inch a 2028 story supporting >$5B revenue**; and **non-GAAP gross margin +500bps last quarter** to be held. ⚠️ **The one bogey imported from another page: whether the DSP/TIA shortage [[AAOI]] flagged also gates LITE** — the author notes LITE already cited electrical components as a driver of its inability to ship more transceivers. ⚠️ Retail/newsletter source, self-declared position — logged as **buy-side expectation-setting**, not as research.

**🆕 2026-08-09 — an independent channel calls the high-power laser shortage "really bad" and names Lumentum (Irrational Analysis, 2026-08-09).** Writing up **Aeva's launch of an optical-connectivity business with a first customer signed**, he guesses the customer is **Innolight** ("someone pinged me as I was writing and confirmed Innolight, with suspicion the hyperscaler is Amazon") and appends the trade explicitly: *"High-power laser shortage is really bad everyone. **HINT HINT LONG LUMENTUM HINT HINT.**"* His broader framing from the prior week is that the CW/high-power laser lane for NPO/CPO is **low-margin and only serveable by a narrow set of suppliers** — the same constraint that shows up on this page as the InP/laser-capacity thread. Treat as an opinionated, self-declared long/short semis channel, not sell-side; the value is that a **new, non-traditional entrant (Aeva) is being pulled into the laser supply chain at all**, which is itself the shortage datapoint. Cross-ref [AAOI.md](AAOI.md), [COHR.md](COHR.md), [AXTI.md](AXTI.md).

UBS also carried **LITE** in its 2026-08-08 earnings-preview slate (UBS / Tech Musings, 2026-08-08). Separately, **FCC 26-50 extends US technology controls to chips, optical transceivers and supply-chain origins** — a new regulatory variable for the transceiver chain (DIGITIMES, 2026-08-10; also flagged as "FCC optics curbs" by FundaAI Weekly, 2026-08-08).

- **Revenue $808.4M, +90% YoY**, record quarter, above guide midpoint (Hurlston/Ali, Q3FY26).
- **Non-GAAP GM 47.9%** (+540bps QoQ, +1,270bps YoY); **non-GAAP OM 32.2%** (+700bps QoQ, +2,140bps YoY); **non-GAAP EPS $2.37**. GAAP EPS $1.50 (Ali, Q3FY26). Already tracking toward the low end of LITE's 39%–42% long-term GM range, with the EML/CPO/OCS mix all accretive to corporate (JPM Hardware & Networking, 2025-12-15).
- **EML supply-demand imbalance "greater than 30%"** — i.e. undershipping customer demand by ~30%, and widening even as capacity is added (Hurlston to Koontz, Q3FY26). Targeting **>50% growth in EML units by Dec-Q 2026**; record EML shipments +100% YoY. LITE holds ~50% AI EML unit share at 100G/lane and expects to hold ~50% at 200G/lane; Citi modeled AI EML sales ~$700M+ in FY26E and LITE as the key supplier to ~50% of 800G/1.6T EML-based transceivers (Citi LITE initiation, 2025-08-29). Tight EML supply drives pricing power on both legacy 100G/lane and new 200G/lane (JPM, 2025-12-15).
- **OCS:** "multiyear, multibillion-dollar purchase agreement"; backlog well past $400M, majority shipping in 2027, three customers (Q2FY26 + Q3FY26). Dec-Q calendar exit-run-rate well above the prior ~$100M peg. Citi modeled OCS at $100M+ quarterly by FY28E on the three key customers (Citi initiation, 2025-08-29). TD Cowen (Hold) frames OCS upside positively but flags that modeling beyond Google's TPU scale-up use case is highly speculative (TD Cowen, 2026-03-11).
- **CPO/scale-out:** ~$50M Dec-Q (cal Q4 '26); an incremental multi-$100M UHP order clicking in 1H'27; scale-up CPO first shipments late cal-2027 (Q2FY26). Pump-laser constraint flagged as "biggest… somewhat unanticipated" bottleneck (Hurlston to Rakesh, Q3FY26). CPO ramps "more meaningfully" from the Dec-Q this year through cal-27, then another inflection in cal-28 as Greensboro comes online; **first scale-up CPO demand (NVL576 / Feynman) starts around early cal-28** (LITE IR call, 2026-05-11). NVDA's scale-out CPO adoption now tracking **>50%** vs ~40% a few quarters ago — primary-customer CPO demand has stepped up over time (JPM "Networking" notes, 2026-05-26).
- **UBS CPO timing / TAM read-through (UBS "Asia Semiconductors — Sector Keys", Sunny Lin, 2026-07-02):** UBS's dedicated CPO section ("lighting the path to AI acceleration") sees CPO **maturing to industry adoption in ~three years**, with **traditional pluggable transceivers coexisting and staying mainstream for the next 2-3 years**. Base case: the whole optical-transceiver SAM grows at a **22% CAGR from ~US$12bn (2025E) to ~US$32bn (2030E)**, of which **CPO reaches ~20% → ~US$7bn by 2030E**. UBS explicitly flags **mixed implications for traditional optical suppliers "eg, Lumentum"** — CPO integrates the optical engine near the switch/XPU (better bandwidth/power), but pluggables keep most volume near-term. On timing: NVDA may push CPO scale-out (Spectrum-X) from **H226E on Rubin racks**; scale-up CPO for GPUs may mature for adoption in **2029E, if not earlier** (OE demand inflecting to 30-50m+ units in 2028E as NVDA migrates the scale-up switch to CPO). Read-through: a modestly more conservative CPO-adoption slope than management's, consistent with the wiki's existing CPO-timing bear thread — but LITE (with COHR) stays the light-source beneficiary as adoption ramps. (UBS "Asia Semiconductors — Sector Keys", 2026-07-02)
- **MS rebuilds the LITE model for CPO adoption; "clearest beneficiary" of optical into scale-up (MS "Bringing More Light to Scale-Up Networks — Updated Scale-Up Primer", Meta Marshall / Joseph Moore, 2026-07-13):** MS sizes the scale-up market at **~$70bn by 2030 (>4x last year's ~$17bn-by-2029 est)**; copper stays longer but CPO is "eventual," with meaningful adoption not until NVDA's **Feynman generation (small 2028, stronger 2029+)** and **OCP in October the next catalyst** — recent optical-name sell-offs "overdone." MS names GLW/LITE/COHR the **clearest beneficiaries of the eventual move of optical into scale-up** (evidenced by NVDA equity investments) and rebuilt the LITE model to incorporate CPO penetration %. LITE-specific: levered to the optical-engine + laser content as scale-up bandwidth outruns pluggables, with mgmt framing **NPO an intermediate step / CPO the likely next-gen convergence point**; **scale-up optical shipments begin late 2027, products in-market early 2028**, first scale-up opportunity sized at **3-4x initial scale-out CPO**. Model raised: **2027E revenue $5,374M → $5,668M (+89% y/y), 2027E adj EPS $17.31 → $18.37**; FY28 adj-EPS sensitivity spans **~$22.6-$31.0** across the scale-out (7.6-27.6%) × scale-up (1.5-5.5%) CPO-adoption grid. (MS "Updated Scale-Up Primer", 2026-07-13)
- **Mizuho reiterates Outperform, PT $1,100 — LITE a "critical supplier" to the DC-capex ramp (Mizuho "DC Capex Ramping to >$1.4T in 2028E; FCF Outlook Improving", Vijay Rakesh, 2026-07-11):** Mizuho names LITE among the "critical suppliers" (with CRDO) to accelerating AI DC capex — consensus **$1.2T 2027E / >$1.4T 2028E**, ~**$50B/GW**, cumulative **106GW 2026-30E (~$5.3T, ~4x combined 2025-26E spend)**. LITE base-case PT **$1,100 (~33x F28E P/E)** on a sole-sourced-CPO-UHP-laser-supplier-to-NVDA + OCS-ramp thesis; **>50% AI EML unit share at 100G/lane, expected to hold ~50% at 200G/lane**; optical-cable TAM >70% CAGR (C24-29E), InP-laser deployments >50% unit CAGR. (Mizuho / Vijay Rakesh, 2026-07-11; price ref $802.01)
- **🆕 MIZUHO RAISES THE LITE PT TO $1,140 (from $1,100) AND KEEPS OUTPERFORM — and the analytically useful line is about NPO CONTENT, not the target (Mizuho "AI Server Call: 2027 VR with HBM and NPO/CPO, Adv. Packaging EMIB-T/CoPoS Ramps", Vijay Rakesh with Dale Gai and Kevin Wang, 2026-08-16; price ref $926.14).** ⚠️ **THESIS DRIFT APPLIED: the prior Mizuho mark on this page — Outperform, PT $1,100 (~33x F28E P/E, price ref $802.01, 2026-07-11) — is superseded and moved to `## Changelog` (2026-08-17). The bullet immediately above is retained as the July record.** 🔴 **The content claim: "initial NPO/CPO ramps tied to Quantum-X/Spectrum-X should begin in late 2026 and accelerate into 2028 with VR-Ultra, with CPO/Spectrum-X reaching ~80–100K units in 2027E and 130K+ in 2028E, Spectrum-X6/7 80%+ of mix. **Laser suppliers' capacity is FUNGIBLE between NPO and CPO, with SIMILAR TO HIGHER CONTENT ON NPO (positive for LITE)**."* ➤ **This is the third house to take LITE's side of the NPO-content dispute (with Citi 07-16 "NPO laser ~= CPO" and Redburn 08-12 "the mid-power part is HARDER"), against Jefferies' 08-11 *"it's not actually true."* Note the shape of the argument matters: Mizuho's is a CAPACITY-fungibility claim (the same fab serves both) rather than a per-socket ASP claim, so it defends the revenue base without settling the content question.** Supporting frame: **VR200 NVL72 on track for a SepQ launch and DecQ volume ramp, potentially 2.5–3K NVL72 racks in 2026E driven by [[META]] and xAI; 2027E >50K VR racks; CSP capex +111%/+50% in 2026E/2027E to ~$1T/~$1.5T.** LITE is named among Mizuho's preferred names into 2027E (**DELL / [[CRDO]] / [[NVDA]] / [[AVGO]] / LITE**). ⚠️ **One internal inconsistency in the note, logged rather than adopted: its CSP RPO/backlog figure moves ~$735B in 1Q25 → ~$2.3T now, which the summary calls "up 3.5x y/y" and the body "up >250% y/y" — the endpoints are 3.1x / +213% and are NOT a y/y comparison. Endpoints logged; neither growth rate adopted.**
- **Citi debuts an InP supply model, opens an *upside* Catalyst Watch, and raises FY27E EPS on pricing (Citi "InP Supply Constraint Will Likely Persist," Papa Sylla, 2026-07-16):** Citi's new InP (EML+CW) supply model, paired with its China optical-demand model, shows **supply staying below demand for three years** — total laser-chip supply ~280M (2025) → ~1.1B (2028E, ~+60% CAGR), 6-inch the biggest lever (**4.36x more chips/wafer**), with the **EML supply gap ~33%/20%/11% (2026-28)** and CW widening to **19%/55% (2027/28)** as CPO/NPO high-power demand ramps. As the leader in **100G/200G EML and UHP lasers**, LITE captures the pricing power: Citi now models an **additional 15% price increase in FY2027E**, lifting **FY2027E/FY2028E EPS +6%/+5% (next-FY EPS $20.03 → $21.18)**; **Buy, PT $1,100 unchanged (price $752.00)**, and Citi **opens a 90-day *upside* Catalyst Watch** into the Jun-Q print (event date **11 Aug 2026**) + ECOC (Sep 20-24) on favorable pricing, an intact CPO roadmap and technology leadership. Pre-earnings mgmt callback color: **NPO in most cases needs a laser similar to CPO (~400mW faceplate vs ~150mW inside-the-box), where LITE is differentiated (few can build 150mW+ CW)** — even if NVDA shifted to NPO around TSMC packaging constraints it would take the same LITE ELS + laser chip (no negative impact); **NPO activity has *increased* since NVDA's CPO announcement**. NVDA is **still not even a top-10 / 10%+ customer (low-single-digit %)** — early days, so a CPO delay could even be favorable; mgmt thinks investors overlook the OCS + transceiver stories. On China: LITE sees a technical **"brick wall" between standard CW and 150mW+ CW** that new entrants would struggle to cross; the "70% China InP" figure is "likely a raw-material stat" (>half of substrate production is Japan-based). (Citi InP note, 2026-07-16)
- **Scale-across / pumps — new growth leg.** "Scale across" pump-laser components today run **mid-$10s (~$40–60M/qtr)** and are guided to **3–4x output/revenue over the next 4–6 quarters**; LITE holds **70–80% of the pump-laser market and similar on ITLAs**, and is "essentially the kingmaker in terms of allocation" — pump supply is behind demand by **~50–60%**, more pronounced than EMLs, driving **mid-double-digit % price increases** on pumps (Michael Hurlston, JPM LITE mgmt call, 2026-05-07). Demand is not just Ciena/Meta — **Nokia, Cisco, Molex and Ciena** are all placing scale-across orders, splitting the back-end of the network across a DCI framework (LITE IR call, 2026-05-11). The high-margin **charge-pump line (~$30M/qtr run-rate) doubles next quarter** on scale-across demand, with gross and operating margins better than even EMLs (LITE IR call, 2026-05-11). Pumps share the Santa Fe fab with phase-one CPO, forcing Caswell UK into faster/higher-volume service for CPO (JPM LITE mgmt call, 2026-05-07).
- **House sizing — scale-across lands in the "Telecom – Other" line, and the model may be conservative on the 2028 tail (Capstone model + SemiAnalysis TAM, 2026-07-06).** In the Capstone consolidated-optics model, LITE's scale-across pump + coherent-component revenue sits in **Telecom → "Telecom – Other"** (the other Telecom sub-line, WSS/ROADM, is held flat at **~$431M**). That line runs **$352M (2024) → $590M (2025) → $1,050M (2026E) → $1,198M (2027E)** — ~25% of total revenue in 2026, falling to ~15% by 2027 as Datacom/AI dominates; **all** the Telecom-segment growth is here, and the **+78% 2025→26 jump is the scale-across pump ramp** (mgmt: pumps ~$40–60M/qtr, guided **3–4×** over 4–6 quarters). Within the $1,050M (2026), the AI-scale-across pump piece is ~$200M; the rest is legacy telecom-transport coherent (ITLAs/modulators/receivers/ROADM pumps). **Scenario read:** SemiAnalysis's scale-across *content* TAM (**$3B'26 → $14B'28**, ~116%/yr — [optical-cpo theme](themes/optical-cpo.md)) is steeper and longer than the model's **+14% 2027 plateau (~$1.2B)** implies; a LITE laser slice of ~$0.7–1.0B by 2028 (pumps + coherent) would push **"Telecom – Other" toward ~$1.3–1.6B by 2028** — i.e. the scale-across tail isn't fully in the model. _Directional (house analysis); anchors: Capstone model `Modelo consolidado incl. LITE.xlsx` (2026-06-16), LITE pump disclosure (JPM mgmt call, 2026-05-07), SemiAnalysis scale-across TAM (2026-07-06)._
- **Transceivers — sole-source at Google.** LITE has reached the "front of the line" at its primary transceiver customer (Google), holding **100% share on a couple of key 1.6T SKUs** near-term (more temporal than the OCS moat — Innolight/Coherent will catch up in a quarter or two); Google's new **Virgo back-end network is an incremental optics opportunity that lifts optics attach per TPU** (Hurlston/Yuen, JPM LITE mgmt call, 2026-05-07). Transceiver business expected to **roughly double over the next ~4 quarters** despite manufacturing/margin still the soft spot (JPM LITE mgmt call, 2026-05-07). LITE is feeding ~**20% of its transceiver mix with its own CW lasers** (in response to a CW-laser-diode decommit from an LD supplier), borrowing from InP capacity and modestly moderating EML revenue growth (JPM LITE mgmt call, 2026-05-07; JPM "Networking" recap, 2026-05-08).
- **OCS Sep-Q inflection quantified:** OCS ~$70M in the June quarter steps to **~$170M/quarter in 1H-FY27** (≈$100M of sequential growth from OCS alone), making the September quarter the primary revenue driver; **Dec-Q OCS ≥$200M, hopefully ~$250M** run-rate (JPM "Networking" recap, 2026-05-08; LITE IR call, 2026-05-11). Non-NVIDIA customers are now seeking to lock the **remaining Greensboro fab capacity**, pressuring NVDA to make a further fab investment for visibility (JPM "Networking" notes, 2026-05-26). ~**90%+ of fab allocation is set in take-or-pay agreements**; incremental units above committed take-or-pay are **auctioned off** at healthier pricing (JPM LITE mgmt call, 2026-05-07).
- **Q4FY26 guide:** rev **$960M–$1.01B**, non-GAAP OM **35%–36%**, non-GAAP EPS **$2.85–$3.05** on ~102M shares (Ali, Q3FY26). At $805M Q3 print they are ~halfway to the OFC target of **$2B quarterly rev / 40% OM in 18–24 months** (Hurlston @ JPM, 2026-05-18).
- **Capacity:** 5th InP fab acquired in Greensboro NC (six-inch from the start, ~2yr to HVP → 2028); 40% Sagamihara expansion front-loaded (>half delivered in Dec-Q); Caswell UK and Takao Japan being pulled into service; California fab expansion scaling UHP lasers for AI CPO (JPM, 2025-12-15). CapEx $125M, capacity-led. Cash $3.17B post-NVIDIA (Ali, Q3FY26). Datacom (EML + transceivers) modeled +98% in FY26E, +45%/+26% FY27E/FY28E by Citi (FY25→FY28 CAGR ~53%) (Citi initiation, 2025-08-29).

- **🔴 MANAGEMENT MEETING (VP of IR) — the most direct management evidence on this page since the Q3FY26 call, and it both confirms and complicates the supply-gated bull (Lumentum mgmt via Aurelion Research "A Conversation with Lumentum", 2026-07-29 — joint publication with PhotonCap; ⚠️ the authors state interview responses are *paraphrased* from their notes and "do not represent verbatim statements by Lumentum or its management," so treat quoted phrasing as the authors' rendering of management's answers).** Aurelion Research met **Kathryn Ta, VP of Investor Relations**. What management said, in her framing:
  - **Capacity is being added far harder than the scarcity narrative implies — the single most important new number here.** "**Two fabs in Japan produce most of the world's supply of EML lasers, and capacity there is up eight-fold in two and a half years**," offered as representative of adds made elsewhere. **Lumentum now runs five fabs, including a new one starting to ramp that is not yet producing wafers**; every site has had tools added and several have had the **building shell expanded** to fit more. ⚠️ **Aurelion's own flag is the honest one: "Eight-fold EML capacity in two and a half years cuts against a pure scarcity thesis. Lumentum is racing to relieve the bottleneck that gives it pricing power"** — which they choose to read as confidence in durable demand rather than as a warning. Note this is *capacity*, a different metric from the page's existing **"EML shipments >8x FY20→FY26E"** (OFC deck, 2026-03-17) and **">50% EML unit capacity end-CY26 vs end-CY25"**; carry all three, none supersedes the others.
  - **Copper's wall now has a management date: "late 2027."** Today's co-packaged optics is "**opportunistic rather than forced**" — NVDA deploys it because a faster, more power-efficient laser frees up power that can be redirected to the GPUs, buying system performance — and "**copper has not run out of headroom yet**." She puts **copper hitting the wall in late 2027**, which is when **the first scale-up CPO deployments arrive, or MPO fiber connectors** where customers want a different configuration, **driven by link lengths beyond roughly three meters connecting rack to rack**. On the **Credo/Broadcom claim that six meters of copper reach is achievable: "On paper it is, but the power you would have to push through that copper makes it impractical, so the switch to optical happens around that point regardless."** ⚠️ **This is management endorsing the EARLIER end of the CPO-timing debate, and it sits directly against Lumentum's own Director of Silicon Photonics R&D on 07-23 ("CPO trial ~2028, hyperscaler deployment ~2030, GPU-GPU copper for at least one more generation"). Both stay on the page. A management confirmation of late-'27 scale-up outranks an expert's 2030 deployment mark on the same axis — but a divergence of that size inside one company is itself the datapoint, and it is the cleanest reconciliation task into the 8/11 print.**
  - **Three defences against silicon-giant integration, in her framing.** (1) **Power — "competition thins out as power ratings climb": producing 150 to 400 milliwatt lasers at high volume, high yield and high reliability is difficult, and "she does not see many players able to do for Nvidia what Lumentum does today."** (2) **Survivorship — Lumentum and Coherent are "the two companies left standing from telecom," an industry she describes as historically punishing to serve; Coherent "is stronger at the systems level and is working to catch up on the component side."** (3) **China — new entrants are bringing on large amounts of capacity, "mostly in lower power lasers"; Lumentum ran the comparison internally and found materially higher yields in its finished transceivers using its own lasers, with better spec uniformity unit to unit and better reliability in testing and in the field.** ⚠️ Aurelion's caveats, which are load-bearing: on power, "saying you win at the top end also admits you may lose at the bottom"; the **telecom-survivorship argument is "the weakest"** because "Broadcom and Marvell have plenty of money, and they don't need the best laser in the world. They only need one that is good enough to put on their own chip"; and the **China evidence is entirely management's own internal lab comparison — "nobody outside the company has checked it"** — which is why Aurelion holds "China is not a near-term factor" **with the least conviction of its four calls**.
  - **Ranking the core competencies — and she raised VCSELs unprompted.** Asked which competency prevails over 3/5/10 years, she put **indium-phosphide laser chip manufacturing first "and she was not close on the ranking,"** framed as **architecture agnostic**: "customers cannot escape needing photons, and those photons will most likely come from indium phosphide whatever the design"; link counts grow either way. **MEMS and optical switching second, expected to become "another pillar" — it already handles spine replacement and cluster scale-out for Google** and is a high-volume opportunity, though being a systems supplier is new territory and the form factor is much larger than telecom, raising the reliability/performance bar. She brought up a **VCSEL branch on the roadmap for chip-to-chip photonics on her own**, while still expecting InP to be the answer. Aurelion's read of the risk she left alone: **the laser-chip business works no matter how systems are designed, but optical switching "depends on which design wins, and the customer base is thin. Google is the risk there."**
  - **Share, mix and concentration.** Lumentum holds **roughly 60% of the EML market and is "one of the only, if not the sole, supplier of the highest speed version at volume"**; **revenues have nearly doubled year over year, with margins swinging positive**; **components (laser chips) ~65% of revenue and systems (transceivers, optical switches, MEMS) ~35%** (Aurelion company-introduction section). ⚠️ **The ~60% EML figure is a different basis from the ~50% AI EML *unit* share this page carries from Citi/Mizuho (total EML vs AI EML) and is NOT treated as superseding it.** **Two LITE customers accounted for 26% and 12% of revenues in fiscal Q3 2026** — materially more concentrated than the FY25 10-K's 16.0%/15.4% (see Risks). Aurelion's bear #5 builds on it: NVIDIA is customer, shareholder and owner of rights to future capacity, "these roles are fundamentally unaligned, and its position as a buyer is economically larger than its investment stake, so its incentives skew toward price discipline rather than support," while capacity rights "cause tension among other customers whose supplier is entangled with a competitor that has claims on output."
  - **Token economics — management refuses the frame, and the refusal is the useful part.** Asked whether cheaper-per-token models (Kimi K3, GPT 5.6, the latest Grok) and elastic token demand shape capacity expansion or efficiency innovation, management answered that **Lumentum sits at the "plumbing" layer of the data center, "two or three steps removed from any token economics."** The number they optimise is **power efficiency per bit, and the route to it is higher power lasers** — "the more bits moved per square area of indium phosphide, the more value delivered. Speed and efficiency are the same problem to them; faster transmission is how joules per bit comes down." Aurelion's read: **incentive alignment — "higher power and higher speed make the link more efficient and carry higher ASP at the same time. Lumentum gets paid to build what its customers actually need."** Full read-through on [themes/tokenmaxxing.md](themes/tokenmaxxing.md).
- **🔴 THE AUTHORS' OWN ANALYSIS — and PhotonCap's technical dissent is where the edge is (Aurelion Research/PhotonCap "A Conversation with Lumentum", 2026-07-29). None of this is management; it is the publication's investment analysis (Aurelion) and independent technical commentary (PhotonCap).**
  - **PhotonCap's engineering caveat on the eight-fold number — the sharpest new argument on this page against the *shortage-premium* trade (as distinct from the demand thesis):** "**Wafer starts and qualified yield at the newest spec are different numbers. A fab-level capacity multiple does not relieve a 200G per lane shortage one to one, because the binding constraint is yield at the highest power bins, not floor space… Building capacity takes money. Hitting the top bin takes years of practice. That is why the eight fold number overstates the relief, and why the shortage premium itself is not durable while the margin that survives lives in yield and mix, not in the shortage price**" (PhotonCap technical comment, 2026-07-29). **Read it precisely: it defends the earnings while attacking the reason the market currently pays for them.**
  - **PhotonCap sharpens the moat definition, and it is more usable than "high power."** The moat is "**not peak power. It is qualified usable power at operating temperature, multiplied by spectral quality, reliability, and high volume yield**," with four concrete terms: **RIN** (output flicker — under PAM4's four brightness levels, too much flicker makes the receiver confuse levels), **COD** (catastrophic optical damage, where the laser's own light burns its output facet — "the wall you hit first as power climbs"), **SMSR** (how cleanly one wavelength is emitted vs stray ones) and **FIT** (failures per billion device hours — "with millions of lasers in a data center, even a slightly high FIT means something dies every day"). The structural point: "**Headline specs are what challengers close first. Distribution tails, noise, and lifetime statistics close last, because they take volume learning and years of field data rather than design. Lumentum's claimed edge sits exactly there, and for the same structural reason it cannot be externally verified yet.**" This is the technical spine under the page's existing Irrational-Analysis linewidth/RIN moat thread (05-08).
  - **THREE light-source platforms, not one — the strongest net-new bull item, and it corrects management's own understatement.** PhotonCap: "'A VCSEL branch on the roadmap' is weaker than the reality. **Lumentum has manufactured GaAs VCSELs at consumer volume for smartphone 3D sensing for years, with more than 10 billion emitters shipped per company disclosure.** At **OFC 2026 it showed that base extended to AI: a 1060nm VCSEL and photodetector 2D monolithic array co-packaged with a host ASIC, targeting slow and wide scale-up protocols such as UCIe and PCIe, with operation above 150°C and channel sparing.** So what sits on the roadmap is not VCSEL technology. It is commercial adoption in AI chip-to-chip links." And because **VCSELs live on GaAs, not InP, Lumentum holds three light-source platforms at once — InP EML, InP high-power CW, and GaAs VCSEL — "a stronger architecture hedge than the paraphrase suggests"** (PhotonCap technical comment, 2026-07-29). Directly relevant to the InP-vs-VCSEL lane-share debate already on this page (LightCounting: InP 91%→79% of AI optical lanes 2025→2030F, VCSEL 9%→21%).
  - **The EML-vs-CW product map, stated plainly — "reading Lumentum as an EML company misses half of this article."** An **EML** integrates two things on one InP die: a DFB laser that generates light and an electro-absorption modulator that blinks it into data — a finished modulated source, and "**today's 800G and 1.6T pluggable transceivers hang off it. That is the line that is sold out right now.**" A **CW laser** is the opposite — unmodulated continuous light, with the modulator on a silicon-photonics chip: "**that is the external light source for CPO and SiPh transceivers, and the high power line built for Nvidia sits here.**" Net: "**EML is today's cash flow and high power CW is the next mix shift**" (PhotonCap technical comment, 2026-07-29).
  - **The CPO-delay scenario has an EML cushion — this reframes the page's largest bear thread.** PhotonCap splits the delay risk by *cause*: "**If CPO slips on ASIC or packaging integration while bandwidth demand keeps growing, demand does not disappear. It stays in pluggables longer, and pluggables are EML intensive, which is the line that is sold out today. An integration driven delay pushes CW and ELSFP revenue out while extending the EML cycle. If the delay instead comes from a broader AI capex slowdown, both architectures get hurt.**" Corollary: **the cannibalization question — whether CPO eats into the ~35% of revenue that is transceivers and systems — "flips into a cushion in the delayed CPO scenario," because "CPO is one rung, and a delayed rung keeps traffic on the rung beneath it."** ⚠️ **"The June selloff punished the whole optical complex without this distinction" — i.e. the -8% 2026-06-10 move already logged on this page was, on PhotonCap's read, an undifferentiated de-rate.**
  - **Net-new channel numbers on the CW/CPO ramp (Aurelion bull #4, citing Global Semi Research's 2026-06-10 direct response to the SemiAnalysis note):** supply-chain checks put **NVIDIA's guidance to Lumentum and Coherent for high-power continuous-wave lasers at roughly 40MM units in January, rising to roughly 100MM by April and May**, which **back-solves to CPO switch shipments of roughly 200,000-300,000 in 2027, scaling toward 600,000-800,000 in 2028**, and lines up with **TSMC's COUPE (Compact Universal Photonic Engine)** plan. ⚠️ **Aurelion's own weighting: "These are channel figures rather than company disclosure, so we weight them accordingly." Log as channel, not disclosure.** Against it, the bear it was written to rebut: **SemiAnalysis's 2026-06-09 institutional note argued scale-out CPO shipments would be revised down through FY27 and scale-up pushed to FY29**, on the grounds that **CPO output is driven by a single, very limited ASIC and that ASIC integration is difficult with challenging underlying economics** — the note behind the June selloff, and the specific mechanism behind it is net-new to this page.
  - **A supply datapoint the page did not have: 800G transceiver production is modeled to fall short of demand by about half in FY27, with shortfalls extending through 2029 (a McKinsey study cited in Aurelion's bull case).** Alongside it, the LTA mechanics as Aurelion frames them: **all EML capacity is locked in long-term agreements through FY27**; customers coming back for more than their LTAs cover **pay premium prices**; **customers who do not sign LTAs "risk losing their entire allocation of supply"**; and general analysts expect **double-digit price increases in 200G EMLs in FY26** given the lack of second-source supply. Also: **management describes the supply-demand imbalance as roughly 30% greater demand than is physically suppliable** — a direct confirmation of the ">30%" figure already on this page from the Q3FY26 call.
  - **Capacity timing — slightly EARLIER than this page's 2028 mark, and the second NVDA-funded fab is described for the first time.** The **Greensboro NC fab bought from Qorvo is being converted from gallium arsenide to indium phosphide as Lumentum's fifth InP fab, with management guiding revenue contribution roughly six quarters out from the May call — "which puts it in late 2027 at the earliest"** (vs this page's Greensboro "six-inch from the start, ~2yr to HVP → 2028" and "UHP ramp by early 2028"). Separately, **"the new U.S. facility funded alongside the Nvidia investment… is a ground-up build rather than a conversion"** — the first description on this page of that second project, where the page previously carried only a watch-item for a "phase-four fab announcement." The bear framing on both: **"capacity commitments are being baked into current contracts at peak pricing against volumes that do not arrive for at least another 18 months," and "LITE is expanding EML production by 40% annually"** — if volume growth decelerates by the time capacity lands, **"operating leverage will work against them on a substantially larger operating asset base."** ⚠️ The **40%/yr EML expansion** is a lower run-rate than the page's mgmt-sourced ">50% growth in EML units by Dec-Q 2026"; both stated, neither superseded.
  - **Competitive marks worth tracking.** ⚠️ **Aurelion's bear #1 is Coherent's 6-inch line: switching from 4-inch to 6-inch produces "roughly 2.25 times the usable area per wafer, yielding more laser die at a lower cost per die," and "Lumentum still runs 4 inch wafers and is trying to catch up"; Coherent's InP lasers are "also sold out through 2027."** The bite: **because Lumentum framed its own advantage as bits per square area of indium phosphide, "a competitor with more than twice the usable area per wafer makes direct headway on that metric."** Note the tension with the 07-23 expert call already on this page, which confirmed **LITE's purchase of a 6-inch III-V fab in North Carolina** — current volume production 4-inch, Greensboro 6-inch; both stated, not reconciled. On second sourcing, PhotonCap is blunt: "**second sourcing is in progress, not a hypothesis. NVIDIA's technical blog names three ELS vendors, Lumentum, Sumitomo, and Coherent. The same day Lumentum got its $2B, Coherent got an identical $2B. Coherent's OFC 2026 release formalizes its 6 inch InP ramp and a 400mW CW laser, and AAOI announced sampling of a 400mW narrow linewidth laser in December 2025. The time axis is what matters. Competing capacity lands outside the FY27 LTA window**" — which is exactly where the 2028-capacity argument picks up. On China, PhotonCap updates this page's Zetta/DSBJ thread: **Zetta Semiconductor "no longer fits either box, not the low power dismissal and not the imminent catch-up call" — a mass-producible 100G PAM4 EML at OECC 2025 and a 200G-per-lane device at ACP in November 2025, so "device level performance deserves to be taken seriously now"**, with the open question being whether it holds the full spec distribution across operating temperature and lifetime (unit-to-unit variation, RIN) through hyperscale qualification — "that gets proven by years of volume data, not by announcements."
  - **Tape and sell-side spread — three PT/rating datapoints net-new to this page.** LITE sits **~20% below its all-time high**, coverage "roughly split": **TD Cowen cut its price target to $800 from just over $990; UBS re-affirmed a hold; Citi reiterated a buy with a $1,100 price target and opened a catalyst watch; other price targets range from $900 to $1,400.** Aurelion's framing: "**most of the photonics supercycle has been realized and played out**, as photonics stocks at large have seen earnings increases and re-rates over the past 52 weeks. So, the relevant debates revolve largely around the future state of shortage, pricing, and CPO scale-up." ⚠️ **The TD Cowen PT cut ($990 → $800) is net-new** — the page carried TD Cowen at Hold (2026-03-11) with no PT recorded, so nothing is superseded, but this is a *fresh negative* mark from the page's cleanest bear. **UBS's hold on LITE is also new** (the UBS content on this page was Sunny Lin's Asia CPO work, not a LITE rating). **Citi Buy/$1,100 + catalyst watch confirms the 07-16 note already on the page.** The **$1,400 top / $900 bottom widen the previously recorded ~$1,100-$1,270 PT cluster in both directions.**
  - **Aurelion's four AI-trade implications, condensed:** (1) the compute bottleneck is not only memory — there are shortages "multiple orders of magnitude removed from direct token generation"; (2) **"the optics thesis depends on compute domains growing. It is not fully dependent on a large-scale copper shortage"** — NVDA moving **from 72-GPU domains toward as many as 576 by 2027**, with copper "already confined on an NV72 rack," means multi-rack domains must be optical regardless of copper's efficiency, so **CPO scaling in FY27/FY28 "can do so in parallel with copper and still see commercial success"**; (3) if intelligence commoditizes, value migrates to layers dependent on **physical scarcity** — but **"training is the most communication intensive workload, and the way that optics have traditionally traded is around training economics. Inference is less network intensive… so a mix shift toward inference does not translate to a 1:1 demand for optics,"** with the gap closing via longer context windows, heavier routing, disaggregated prefill/decode and agentic usage plus geographically distributed inference driving DCI; (4) Chinese competition is unlikely to matter near-term — **the call they hold with the least conviction.** Also net-new mechanism: **Rubin "relies on bi-directional signaling to reach its bandwidth targets, and bi-directional signaling over copper degrades substantially at 448G per lane,"** with unknown time-to-market for 448G copper while "optics can scale along modulation, fiber count, and wavelength multiplexing."

### Long-term frame — OFC 2026 Investor Briefing (2026-03-17, deck read directly)
The structural targets management is underwriting (own deck — fills the gap where the page previously only had the "$2B/40% OM" target second-hand via JPM):
- **Target financial model (the margin bridge by revenue level):** at the **$2B/quarter (~$8B annualized) target → GM 49–52% / OM 38–42%** (~40% OM), vs an interim $5B-annualized step at GM 45–48% / OM 33–37%, off an FY26-consensus base of ~$2.9B annualized at GM 43% / OM 27%; **>1,000bps OM expansion targeted in <2 years**. **New growth drivers = 60% of the revenue mix at $2B/qtr** (vs ~25% at the $805M Q3FY26 base). Directly corroborates/quantifies the house model ($8.1bn / ~43% OM 2027E).
- **Greensboro UHP fab acquired from Qorvo, deal closed 2026-03-17 = ~$5B annual revenue capacity**, fully-equipped compound-semi fab, UHP ramp by early 2028 (above-$2B/qtr leg).
- **Demand/capacity CAGRs:** ~**85% CAGR in InP optical-lane demand** (EML+CW+UHP) CY26→CY30; **UHP-laser units >200% CY25→CY30 CAGR**; **OCS units >150% CY25→CY28 CAGR**; EML shipments **>8x FY20→FY26E**; +50%+ EML unit capacity end-CY26 vs end-CY25.
- **Optical-AI TAM $18B (2025) → $90B+ (2030F), ~40% industry-revenue CAGR** (LightCounting/650 Group/Lumentum est.); 2030 bandwidth split scale-up 45% / scale-out 55%. Scale-up CPO Phase 1 framed as **3–4x larger than scale-out CPO**.
- 🔴🔴 **THE FIGURE-2 OCS RATIO FRAMEWORK, APPLIED TO GOOGLE'S *VIRGO* — the most concrete OCS-content uplift on this page, and it is a FIVEFOLD step on the spine (third-party note applying LITE's OFC framework, 2026-08-18; ⚠️ source unconfirmed — see caveat).** The deck's **OCS Port-to-xPU ratio by use case** (Figure 2) has a third use case — **Spine and Super-Spine (Scale-Out / Scale-Across)** — and **Virgo hands the spine and super-spine layers to OCS for inter-cluster routing**, which is precisely that use case. The quantified consequence: **the OCS Port-to-xPU ratio for Spine/Super-Spine rises from 0.2:1 to 1:1 — a FIVEFOLD increase.** 🔴 **The optical-MODULE uplift is larger still, and the reason is structural: OCS is deployed only at the SPINE, whereas optical modules are needed at BOTH ENDS OF EVERY LAYER (in-rack switch, leaf and spine) — and it compounds because in-rack switches go from ONE to FOUR–SIX per rack, multiplying uplink ports. So the scale-out optical-module ratio widens from today's 1:1–1:2 to 1:3–1:4, with speeds moving from 800G to 1.6T.** The physical anchor given for all of it: **a single fabric spanning 130K+ TPUs at ~47 Pb/s of bisection bandwidth.** ✅ **Why this matters for the OCS thesis specifically: it is a per-xPU CONTENT ratio, so it compounds with TPU unit growth rather than substituting for it — and it is an independent mechanism for the deck's own "OCS units >150% CY25→CY28 CAGR" and the 2030 scale-up-45%/scale-out-55% bandwidth split above.** Held against the filed base on this page — **FY26 10-K OCS >$90.0m, a derived ≈$52m June-2026 quarter (≈$208m exit run-rate), the $400m 1H-FY27 target and the first triple-digit OCS quarter guided in FQ1'27** — a 5x spine ratio is the kind of demand mechanism those step-ups require, which is exactly what the page has been missing on the architecture side. ⚠️ **Discipline: these are RATIOS from a framework, not an order book — no Virgo deployment timing is given in the excerpt, and no OCS dollar figure follows from the ratio without a TPU-unit and ASP assumption (this page deliberately does not derive one here).** Pairs with the scale-UP side (Google's rumoured 6D Torus doubling ICI links 6→12, "far more optics and OCS capacity") on [themes/optical-cpo](themes/optical-cpo.md) — **Google's roadmap is raising optics intensity on BOTH axes at once.** ⚠️ **SOURCE NOT STATED IN THE PASTE — attribution UNCONFIRMED.** The text reads as an excerpt from a longer written note (it references *"Figure 2 below"*) that applies **Lumentum's own OFC framework** to Google's Virgo architecture. It arrived alongside, and is the exact scale-OUT complement of, the @SemiAnalysis_ LinkedIn post on the scale-UP/ICI side (6D Torus) — plausibly the same author, **but that is not confirmed. Do not cite the analysis to a house until the source is verified; the RATIO FRAMEWORK itself is Lumentum's (OFC, Figure 2), the Virgo application is the note author's.**
- **Record backlog by product:** laser chips (EML/CW) **>2 years**, UHP **multi-year**, OCS **multi-year**, cloud transceivers & DCI/telecom **>1 year**.
- **NVDA co-development, concrete:** two joint demos at OFC — LITE UHP laser, and LITE 200G EMLs, both inside an **NVIDIA 1.6T 2xDR4 OSFP** module. Hybrid manufacturing strategy now engaging **7+ contract manufacturers** (tariff/supply-chain agility). (All: LITE OFC 2026 Investor Briefing, 2026-03-17.)

## Capstone estimates (house model)
_Source: Capstone official model (`Modelo consolidado incl. LITE .xlsx` — consolidated optics, 2026-06-16)._

| | 2024 | 2025E | 2026E | 2027E |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| Revenue ($bn) | 1.4 | 2.1 | 4.2 | 8.1 |
| EPS — adj ($) | 0.94 | 4.13 | 12.81 | 30.02 |
| Operating margin | ~4% | ~18% | ~32% | ~43% |

**House read:** revenue $1.4bn → $8.1bn (24-27E; +49/+101/+92%) driven by Datacom & AI (~77% of the mix in 2027 incl. transceivers/OCS/CPO); adj EPS $0.94 → $30.02. (Capstone official model — consolidated optics, 2026-06-16)

## Debate / thesis
- **Capstone model (house view):** revenue $1.41B→$2.11B→$4.24B→$8.14B (2024-27E; +49%/+101%/+92%) on Datacom & AI ($0.46B→$2.53B→$6.27B, ~77% of mix by 2027) incl. transceivers / OCS / CPO ramps; adj EPS $0.94 / $4.13 / $12.81 / $30.02; op margin ~4%→43%. (Capstone official model — consolidated optics, 2026-06-16)
- **Bull:**
  - **Demand isn't the question — supply is.** Hurlston: "there's no demand problem, which is an enviable situation… our issue is execution" (Samik/JPM fireside, 2026-05-18). FundaAI framed Hurlston's Mizuho remarks as "a supply problem, not demand" (briefing, 2026-06-11).
  - **Locked book, pricing power.** ~90%+ of fab allocation under take-or-pay LTAs through cal-2027; incremental units auctioned at "even healthier pricing"; LTAs killed the old quarterly price-down ritual (Hurlston/Ali, Lumentum followup call, 2026-05-07). "Sold out through 2028" possible; expects to fill the entire order book through 2028 within two quarters (MS, morning briefing 2026-05-15).
  - **Four growth legs still ahead.** "The vast majority of this growth is still ahead of us" (Hurlston, Q2FY26). Scale-up CPO is "a brand new market that didn't exist before" — >10x the InP need of scale-out (Yuen/Hurlston, Q2FY26 + JPM 2026-05-18). TD Cowen (Hold) independently sizes scale-up CPO external light sources as a **>$9B fully greenfield TAM by 2030E** (~25% port CPO penetration, ~$17/source), with LITE and COHR the key beneficiaries — and argues scale-up CPO ports rapidly eclipse scale-out (TD Cowen, 2026-03-11).
  - **NVDA validation + moat.** Jensen's three photonics investments = Lumentum, Coherent, Corning — "Not Credo. Not copper" (@Midnight_Captl, briefing 2026-05-12). $2B NVDA stake + take-or-pay; OCS Google design win (R64 OCS, in production). Hurlston: high-power laser reliability proven in subsea ("Mariana Trench") — multi-year head start vs new entrants (JPM, 2026-05-18). CPO content math favors LITE: 8 EML chips into a transceiver ≈ one UHP laser in content, but where LITE is ~50% share at the EML level it is **sole or primary source on NVDA's CPO UHP laser** — so transceiver displacement by CPO is more than offset by UHP content (Citi LITE initiation, 2025-08-29).
  - **800G+ datacom / DCI TAM.** 800G+ datacom transceiver market ~$7bn in 2025, telecom/DCI transceivers ~$1bn, both ~+40% CAGR through end of decade; LITE positioned to grow in line or faster on laser-component leadership across datacom and telecom (JPM Hardware & Networking, 2025-12-15). Scale-across/DCI is an incremental ~$10bn+ TAM where LITE plays in coherent pluggables, RLS and amplifiers (JPM Hardware & Networking, 2025-12-05).
  - **Earnings power skews up.** JPM bull-case EPS in the low-teens, vs JPM base ~$11 and sell-side consensus ~$9.50; buy-side whisper has crept to low-teens on OCS, CPO and portfolio-wide pricing (JPM Hardware & Networking, 2025-12-15). JPM ranks LITE **#5 in its 2026 best ideas** and **#2 in optical (COHR > LITE > CIEN > GLW > FN)**, the rank order driven by relative valuation/expectations, not fundamentals (JPM, 2025-12-15).
  - **Sell-side PTs (May '26, briefings):** R&Co Redburn (Mike Harrison) **Buy, PT $1,270**, rev 20% above consensus, 153-page launch "Copper if you can, optical because you must"; JPM (Chatterjee) **OW, PT $1,130** (FY27E $19.40 / FY28E $32, 35x C28F); Citi (Malik/Sylla) **Buy ~$1,100** (FY28E ~$30); Jefferies (Jeff Pu) **Buy $1,168**. The original Citi launch was **Buy, $165 PT** (pre-split; ~23x FY27E EPS ~$7.16, a 21% premium to ~19x AI-optics peers, justified on a 58% vs 40% EPS-CAGR basis), preferring LITE to COHR over 12 months on faster growth (FY26 rev +42%, 29% 3yr CAGR) and faster margin expansion (~850bps GM FY25→FY28; GM modeled 41.9%/43.3% FY27/FY28) (Citi LITE initiation, 2025-08-29).
  - **🔴 TRANSCEIVERS FINALLY HAVE A QUARTERLY DOLLAR BASE AND A DATED TARGET — AND THE BASE RECONCILES WITH THE FILED SYSTEMS LINE.** From a post-print callback: **June-quarter transceiver sales were "about $225 million," i.e. a "$900 million run rate"** (Wolfe notes Hurlston framed it on the call as *"around a billion dollar run rate"*), and **management thinks it can reach a $3BN TRANSCEIVER REVENUE RUN-RATE OVER THE NEXT FIVE QUARTERS while continuing to keep gross margins heading upward** — which Wolfe calls *"really quite a statement given that their transceiver business is running at low 20s or mid 20s in terms of gross margin"* (Wolfe Research "Q2 earnings-season takeaways call", 2026-08-14). ✅ **The $225m mark is the first quarterly transceiver DOLLAR figure on this page and it CROSS-CHECKS cleanly against the two filed anchors rather than competing with them: reported Q4FY26 Systems revenue was $356.9m and the FY26 10-K back-solves OCS to ~$52m, so transceivers + OCS ≈ $277m of $356.9m, leaving ~$80m for WSS/ROADM/other. Internally consistent — and it is the first independent corroboration of the ~$52m OCS derivation, which until now rested entirely on two company floors.** _(House arithmetic on published figures; not a company disclosure.)_ ⚠️ **Hold the target as bull and bear at once: $0.9bn → $3.0bn in five quarters is ~3.3x on the LOWEST-margin line in the company, so "GM keeps rising" and "transceivers triple" can only both hold if Components mix plus OCS/pump/ELS margin carry it — exactly the mechanism the FY26 10-K spelled out (laser-chip ASPs UP on 200G, cloud-transceiver ASPs DOWN on volume).** ➤ **And Wolfe supplies the governor, which is the same behavioural claim as the managed-quarter allegation under *Bear*: Hurlston *"is definitely running this business with margin expansion in mind… it limits the amount of transceiver revenue he's willing to do,"* though *"to the extent that they have much higher growth outside of the transceiver business at margins that are very attractive… he would be interested in tackling a bunch more transceiver business."* The transceiver line is a CHOICE VARIABLE, not a capacity outcome.**
  - **✅ THE LASER-QUALITY DEFENCE AGAINST THE CHINA-SUPPLY BEAR, from a house that has published on the InP-glut question directly.** Asked whether the Chinese-laser concern has been addressed, Wolfe is *"pretty comfortable"*: it expects the China-capacity anecdotes to keep coming (*"perception is reality… that narrative will continue to be around"*) but argues **there are "real differences in laser quality" — LITE's CW lasers *"are better than the CW lasers that are elsewhere in the marketplace. They're getting price premiums on those"*** — and that in a CPO/NPO rack cycle worth *"tens of billions… or hundreds of billions of dollars"* the buyer *"is going to go out and get the very best laser that I can get… and I probably don't want that to be a Chinese laser,"* because a laser performance issue three or four years into a program is *"a really catastrophic outcome."* Conclusion: *"the industry, the customers, hyperscalers, Nvidia, they're going to want to coalesce around the very best laser datacom chips in the marketplace. We think that increasingly points to Lumentum. I think the existence of additional supply doesn't automatically assume that the economics of Lumentum's business are going to be damaged."* On the glut itself Wolfe points to its own *Coolout* essay and concludes that on scale-up InP content **"even with all the supply that's coming on… the industry will still be under supplied"** (Wolfe Research "Q2 earnings-season takeaways call", 2026-08-14). ⚠️ **This is a QUALITATIVE quality-premium argument, not a capacity model — it does not answer TD Cowen's wafer arithmetic (CW dies ~60% of EML area ⇒ >4x transceivers per wafer) and Wolfe supplies no sizing for its own undersupply conclusion. Note also that Wolfe reads the AXT LTA as evidence FOR tightness rather than comfort: "they had signed a seven-year deal with Sumitomo… Suddenly, in the last three months, it wasn't enough."**
- **Bear:**
  - **Execution is the whole tape.** JPM's own framing: "Lots of irons in the fire; execution the concern" (Meyers, briefing 2026-05-08). Hurlston himself admits margin/manufacturing on transceivers is the soft spot.
  - **Transceiver margin drag.** LITE transceiver GM "in the 20s," vs Coherent mid-30s and Chinese/Innolight mid-40s; line of sight to 30s, still well below the ~50% corporate level even as the business roughly doubles over 4–5 quarters (Hurlston, followup call 2026-05-07). Citi pegged the internal-SiPho transceiver GM profile at 30–35% even after bringing CW lasers fully in-house (1HC2026), with cloud-module execution historically "choppy" and the biggest near-term margin swing factor (Citi initiation, 2025-08-29).
  - **InP merchant oversupply — the structural bear.** TD Cowen initiates **LITE at Hold and prefers COHR**: it sees simultaneous InP capacity adds (LITE +40% units Sep-25→Jun-26, Sumitomo 2.4x to 2028, Mitsubishi shifting ~$1.5bn capex toward optics) colliding with a mix shift to silicon photonics / CW lasers. Because CW dies are ~60% the size of an EML and yield better, a given wafer supports **>4x (Sumitomo math ~4.5x) the transceivers** even at flat wafer starts — priming the merchant market to tip into oversupply. Risk is acute for LITE specifically because Components is ~65% of revenue (vs ~2% at Sumitomo, ~0.5% at Mitsubishi) and its cash-flow base for capex is far smaller; COHR's vertical integration lets it dial down external laser purchases, compounding LITE's exposure. TD Cowen explicitly likens the LTA "visibility through 2027" to the 2021/22 analog-semis cycle: "LTAs give visibility… until they don't" (TD Cowen, 2026-03-11). The SiPh/CW shift is also an apples-to-apples revenue headwind as cheaper CW displaces higher-ASP EML (TD Cowen, 2026-03-11).
    - **Management's direct rebuttal (OFC 2026 briefing, 2026-03-17 — mgmt frame, not independent):** EMLs become the **de facto standard at 400G/lane (3.2T era)** because SiPho lacks sufficient bandwidth at 400G/lane and replacement materials are "unproven in mass production"; per LightCounting (Jan 2026) **InP holds 91% of AI optical lanes in 2025 and still 79% in 2030F** (VCSEL 9%→21%) — i.e. InP share erosion is modest, not a cliff. This is the structural counter to TD Cowen's oversupply thesis (the bear's reply: that's lane *share*, not wafer *supply* — the oversupply risk is the absolute InP capacity adds, which the deck's own 85% lane-demand CAGR is meant to absorb).
  - **Valuation/positioning.** JPM bear tenet: limited appetite to pay ~30x earnings (vs ~20x historical) with the buy-side already pricing in more pricing power than sell-side estimates embed (JPM, 2025-12-15). BofA (Vivek Arya) **Neutral, PT $1,100** — best-in-sector growth but "upside well priced in" (CY26/27E +18%/+9%) (briefing 2026-05-06). Barclays (Rand): sentiment "a lot more mixed than a few months ago… pushback on huge EPS numbers" (briefing 2026-05-20). @ResearchQf: "most extended vs 4yr avg," 20–40% correction plausible (briefing 2026-05-13). Stock fell -8% on 2026-06-10 on CPO-timing noise.
  - **CPO timing / cannibalization.** JEF (Beavington) flagged a CPO OIO pushout to 2030-31 on interposer overheating; MS (Marshall) and JPM (Chatterjee) call CPO concerns "overblown" and buy-the-dip, but the binary timing debate is the recurring wobble (briefings 2026-06-11/12). Scale-out CPO cannibalizes pluggable transceivers (Hurlston, JPM 2026-05-18). Note TD Cowen's offsetting view: CPO is far more likely in scale-up (greenfield) than scale-out, where pluggables stay dominant on interoperability/multi-vendor/architectural-fungibility grounds — making CPO an opportunity rather than a threat (TD Cowen, 2026-03-11).
  - **Substrate/equipment/China.** China controls 70%+ of global InP output (briefing 2026-06-13); substrate "mostly under control" via Sumitomo LTA but "tighter than I'd like"; equipment (reactors, etch) now an inhibitor (Hurlston, JPM 2026-05-18).
  - **🔴🔴 QUALITY OF EARNINGS — A SELL-SIDE ASSERTION THAT THE LAST TWO QUARTERS WERE *MANAGED*. This is the most consequential claim on this page that no filing supports, and it is filed under Bear deliberately.** Wolfe flags LITE as one of only two outliers on its print-vs-consensus tracker (the other being [[DELL]], for unrelated memory-pass-through reasons) and explains LITE's as deliberate revenue timing: *"talking to the company after the March quarter print, the thing we realized in the March quarter — and we think they did this in June as well — but we think they **turned off revenue before the end of the quarter**. So, Hurlston and [Cathy Todd], at one point, **admitted to us** that on the March quarter, they were **tracking so far ahead on margins and profitability that they shut down the revenue before quarter end**. So **this is a managed quarter. We suspect the same thing happened in Q2**"* (Wolfe Research "Q2 earnings-season takeaways call", 2026-08-14). ⚠️ **PROVENANCE FIRST, BECAUSE IT IS WEAK: a broker's SECOND-HAND ACCOUNT OF A MANAGEMENT ADMISSION, on a machine-transcribed call, with the finance name rendered phonetically as "Cathy Todd" and uncorroborated anywhere on this page. It is NOT a company disclosure and appears in NO filing, release or transcript here. Carry it as a testable allegation, never as fact.** ➤ **If true it cuts three ways, none of them priced: (i) REPORTED REVENUE UNDERSTATES TRUE DEMAND — the top line is a floor, not a measurement; (ii) THE BEAT PATTERN IS PARTLY DISCRETIONARY — a company that chooses when to stop shipping also chooses the size of its own beat, which devalues "beat on every line" as evidence and is why this sits under Bear; (iii) there is an UNBOOKED BACKLOG CUSHION of held-back revenue that appears in no disclosed number.** ⚠️ **The uncomfortable interaction is with this page's own margin case: if revenue is switched off precisely WHEN MARGIN TARGETS ARE ALREADY MET, then the 50.4% Q4 GM and the ~+610bps beat vs Street are partly an OUTPUT OF THE SAME DECISION rather than independent evidence of mix/yield durability — and that beat is currently the cleanest refutation the page claims against the transceiver-dilution bear (see the *Margin durability* row in *Signal vs management*). Both readings retained; neither resolved.** ➤ **The test is arithmetic, not anecdotal: a managed quarter should leave a fingerprint in q/q revenue linearity against a still-rising backlog/deferred position, and Wolfe's framing explicitly predicts the SAME behaviour in the September quarter. Score it at F1Q27.**
- **BACKGROUND / STRUCTURAL — the SemiAnalysis CPO reference book, Jan-26 vintage: a named LITE sole-source call, the technical case AGAINST laser integration, and one under-covered content risk (SemiAnalysis · Dylan Patel, Daniel Nishball, Myron Xie et al, "CPO Book", 2026-01-01).** ⚠️🔴 **_READ THE VINTAGE FIRST. This is SemiAnalysis's January-2026 reference "book" on co-packaged optics — a framework publication, ~7 months old. It is **NOT** the recent SemiAnalysis CPO push-out note that management refuted by name on 08-13 (and that [[COHR]]'s Jim Anderson refuted on 08-12); it predates that call entirely and says nothing about it. Nothing below supersedes ANY 2026 datapoint on this page — not the 08-13 ~$2bn/quarter-by-Sep-27 run-rate, not the 1.6T GM 30-35%, not the ≤25%-of-revenue transceiver cap, not the second NPO customer, not NVIDIA's 400mW ELS lasers or the Greensboro/Thailand capacity marks. Where it conflicts, the fresh number stands and the conflict is flagged, not resolved._** **(1) 🔴 The dated sole-source call, stated plainly.** On external laser sources for Nvidia's CPO switches: "**We expect Lumentum to be the SOLE SUPPLIER for Nvidia's initial batches of CPO switch shipments, and Coherent may enter as the SECOND SUPPLIER IN LATE 2026.**" An independent, third-party, pre-dated corroboration of the sole-source moat this page carries in the *Sinal* table — and, more usefully now, **a checkable second-source window that is live today**: the [[COHR]] entry it forecast for late-2026 is exactly the thing to verify against current supply-chain flow rather than assume. **(2) LITE's named slot in Nvidia's laser ecosystem.** At VLSI, Nvidia named its laser partners: "**Lumentum for single high-power DFBs**, Ayar Labs for DFB arrays, Innolume for quantum-dot mode-locked combs, and Xscape, Enlightra, and Iloomina for pumped nonlinear resonant combs." Nvidia was also "exploring **VCSEL arrays** as a potential alternative… suitable for 'wide-and-slow' applications," though SemiAnalysis did "not see this as an immediate priority for Nvidia" — a Jan-26 mark on the same InP-vs-VCSEL lane debate the 07-29 PhotonCap comment addresses, from the customer's side. **(3) ELS content math, per switch.** The Nvidia X800-Q3450 uses **18 ELS modules, each containing 8 continuous-wave DFB laser chips**; **each laser must deliver 24.5 dBm of optical power** to absorb connector, fiber-coupling and modulator losses, and **lasers plus thermo-electric coolers account for ~70% of ELS power consumption** ("high-output lasers generate more heat and degrade faster under thermal stress… the issue of high power requirements of lasers has not been fully solved"). Each 1.6T optical engine's 20-fiber FAU dedicates **4 fibers to the laser input**. ⚠️ **The book puts each CW DFB chip at ~350mW — DO NOT use this: the page's 08-13 first-party mark is NVIDIA's 400mW ELS lasers, and management's own ELS/OE power-tier framing (400mW ELS vs the lower-power OE part) is both fresher and first-party. The Jan-26 figure is logged for lineage only.** **(4) ★ The technical case AGAINST laser integration — the independent counter to the Aurelion/PhotonCap 07-29 integration bear.** The book states the industry has *converged*: "**The one approach where the industry has come to a consensus is to use an External Light Source (ELS).**" On-chip lasers (III-V bonded onto silicon) are rejected for three stated reasons: (i) "**lasers are known to be one of the most failure prone components within the system** – failures would have a **high blast radius** if integrated into the CPO engine as it would take down the whole chip"; (ii) lasers "are sensitive to heat," and co-packaging puts them next to "the hottest part of the system"; (iii) "**on-chip lasers typically struggle to deliver high enough power output**." Hence the pluggable OSFP-style ELS, which "simplifies field servicing in the fairly common case of laser failures" — "often, the laser is the most common point of failure." Read against Aurelion's own view that "Broadcom and Marvell… don't need the best laser in the world. They only need one that is **good enough to put on their own chip**": this source argues the physics pushes the opposite way — the laser wants to be **off** the package and **field-replaceable**, which is precisely the discrete-module business LITE is in. Independent, and dated seven months before that exchange. **(5) The China/commoditisation split, corroborating this page's own reconciliation.** CW-laser producers named: "**Broadcom (U.S.), Furukawa (Japan), Lumentum (U.S.), Coherent (U.S.), Yuanjie (China), and Shijia (China). Lumentum, Coherent, Furukawa and Broadcom usually charge higher prices than Chinese vendors.**" And the split this page reached independently: "Chinese manufacturers could see opportunities down the road, as CW laser sources are considered **relatively standardized and commoditized in general, but there is still somewhat of a MOAT around building the HIGH POWER laser sources that are required for CPO applications**." Direct Jan-26 support for the ≥100mW-tier distinction drawn in *Current state* — commodity CW localises, the CPO power tier does not. ⚠️ Also note **Broadcom is on that vendor list** — the platform-competitor role [[AVGO]]'s page carries from PhotonCap, sourced independently here. **(6) ⚠️🔴 The content risk that is under-covered on this page: multi-wavelength and comb light sources cut the NUMBER of discrete high-power lasers per unit of bandwidth.** All Jan-26 *vendor claims*, unverified, and every one of them is a laser-count deflator rather than a share shift: **Lightmatter's GUIDE** — "the industry's first Very Large Scale Photonics (VLSP) laser… integrates **hundreds of InP lasers onto a single silicon chip** to support up to **50 Tbit/s**," with self-repair via over-provisioned diodes, and the explicit claim that "**the Nvidia Quantum-X CPO Switch featuring 144 ports of 800G requires 18 ELSs, and Lightmatter claims that TWO GUIDE laser sources could cater to this same overall bandwidth requirement**" (a ~9:1 module-count reduction claim); **Xscape's ChromX** — a programmable comb at 4-16λ today, "plans to provide up to **128 wavelengths**," where "their solution requires **only one laser** whereas existing CPO solutions require multiple lasers"; **Scintil's LEAF Light** — wafer-scale InP integrating **8 or 16 lasers of different colours on one chip** in an OIF-defined **ELSFP** module, claiming "superior power efficiency… while **halving the energy required for each transmitted bit**" versus "existing co-packaged solutions, **including what Nvidia is currently adopting for Q3450 CPU switches**, which use a single wavelength with high modulation speed"; and **Ayar's SuperNova**, a 16λ MSA-compliant source **supplied by Sivers**. ⚠️ **Two-sided and not a thesis change: DWDM is being pursued precisely because CPO is fiber-count-limited, and higher λ-count sources are harder, higher-power parts — which is the tier this page argues LITE owns. But the unit-economics direction (fewer, richer laser modules per switch) is a real modelling question against the per-switch ELS content math in (3), and it is not currently priced anywhere on this page.** **(7) Minor, supporting the InP thread:** today's EML is "a continuous wave (CW) distributed feedback (DFB) laser and an **InP based EAM** coupled together"; an 800G DR8 uses **8 EMLs** at ~56 GBaud PAM4; and the structural cost problem — "**InP wafers tend to be small (3" or 6") and suffer from low yields — both factors drive up unit costs** for InP-based devices when compared to Silicon which can be built on 8" or 12" processes." (SemiAnalysis · Dylan Patel et al, "CPO Book", 2026-01-01) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/newslettersemianalysiscom_api_v1_post_pdf_postId178153689.html)
- **Where the sell-side stands:** Broadly constructive, PTs clustered ~$1,100–$1,270; the bull case is Redburn/JPM/Citi/Jefferies/MS; the swing voters are BofA Neutral on valuation and TD Cowen Hold on InP oversupply (the cleanest bear, preferring COHR). Independent (FundaAI, Crux, Irrational Analysis) overwhelmingly long. The actionable edge: the Street agrees on near-term demand and disagrees on (1) CPO ramp timing, (2) whether merchant InP eventually overshoots as SiPh/CW penetrates, and (3) whether the multiple (~30x vs ~20x historical) has run ahead of the 2028 numbers.

- **📚 ARCHIVE — DAMNANG (independent Korean semis Substack, launched 2026-03-15, ~9k subscribers, PAID; NOT A BROKER — no rating, no PT, no estimates anywhere in the set). Historical context only: nothing below supersedes any current number on this page.**
  - **The laser BOM economics, which are the arithmetic underneath the "components are where the margin lives" claim: an EML bare die runs **$10-20**, packaged into a TOSA **$50-100 or more**, and an 800G DR8 module carries **eight** of them — *"the laser cost overwhelmingly dominates the bill of materials."* Against that, an 800G SiPh module takes **2-4 CW lasers at $8-10 per 70mW unit = $20-40 per module, "less than half of what EML costs,"** and SiPh 800G penetration ran **~10% (2024) → 20-30% (2025)** (**DAMNANG (Substack)** — "The War of Light, A Laser Shortage", **2026-03-31**). ➤ **The mix shift this page tracks as a SHARE question is, at the BOM level, a ~2-3x derating in laser dollars per module — which is exactly why LITE moving into merchant CW (JPM, 2026-08-14) is volume-accretive and ASP-dilutive at the same time, and why the 10-K’s "laser-chip ASPs UP, transceiver ASPs DOWN" split is the mechanism rather than a coincidence.** The same piece marks the **NVDA investment price at $695/share**, reading the premium as *"the market pricing in Lumentum’s near-monopoly position in laser supply."***
  - **The multi-wavelength-array gap, which is the one place a small-cap is credibly ahead of LITE: *"Lumentum has entered CPO UHP laser production, but there is no public disclosure that it is shipping MULTI-WAVELENGTH ARRAYS as a production product"* — LITE has announced an **8-channel array** and a **CW WDM MSA-compliant ELS collaboration with Ayar Labs dating to 2022**, and has **UHP 400mW launched in 2025 with an 800mW SHP roadmap**, while [[COHR]] *"demonstrated an ELSFP but standalone array die productization remains limited"* (**DAMNANG (Substack)** — "Sivers Semiconductors: Verifying the Thesis After a 2,100% Run in Six Months", **2026-05-27**). ➤ **The structural point survives the five months and is testable now: hyperscalers have an incentive to seed an INDEPENDENT array supplier precisely BECAUSE LITE/COHR capacity is read as NVDA-linked. Score it against management’s "first meaningful NPO customer within the next four months" promise (mgmt via BTG, 2026-08-13) — if that customer is a hyperscaler rather than a systems vendor, the seeding argument weakens materially.**

- **🆕🔴 OFC — MARCH 2027: management says the $8bn OCS TAM given at OFC last year is *"SIGNIFICANTLY UNDERCALLED"* and that a new, higher number comes at OFC next March** (DB Technology Conference, 2026-08-27). ➜ **A dated, company-committed TAM revision — rare and directly falsifiable.**
- **🆕 NEXT QUARTER: OCS revenue to *"EXCEED $100 MILLION"*, off shipments *"doubling sequentially"*** (DB Technology Conference, 2026-08-27). ➜ **The nearest checkable number attached to the $40 FY28 earnings-power claim — if OCS does not clear $100m next quarter, the whole out-year bridge is in question.**
- **🆕🔴 NPO INFLECTION PULLED IN TO LATE-2027 / EARLY-2028 (from late-2028 / early-2029)** — and the gating condition is now explicit: **Lumentum wants NPO customers to underwrite capacity the way [[NVDA]] underwrote Greensboro *before* it invests further** (DB Technology Conference, 2026-08-27). ➜ **Watch for a SECOND multi-billion LTA-style commitment from a non-NVIDIA customer. Until one lands, the pull-in produces a shortage, not revenue.**

Either the cap moves, the $3bn arrives later than the $2bn, or one of the two marks is wrong: a live reconciliation item, not a resolved one.** _(House arithmetic on two published management marks.)_
