## Risks
- **Customer concentration:** Customer A 16.0% / Customer B 15.4% of FY25 net revenue (>10% each); transceiver demand now skewed to one sole-source customer (LITE 10-K FY25; Q2FY26). **Update — concentration has stepped up sharply on the latest disclosed quarter: "Two LITE customers accounted for 26% and 12% of revenues in fiscal Q3 2026"** (Aurelion Research "A Conversation with Lumentum", 2026-07-29). The FY25 10-K split (16.0%/15.4%) stands as the annual figure and is not superseded, but the top customer at **26% of a single quarter vs 16.0% of FY25** is the more relevant number for the concentration risk, and it lands alongside Aurelion's argument that **NVIDIA's roles as customer, shareholder and owner of rights to future capacity "are fundamentally unaligned," with its buyer position "economically larger than its investment stake," so its incentives "skew toward price discipline rather than support."** ⚠️ Note the cross-current with Citi's 07-16 mgmt callback on this page, where **NVDA was described as still not a top-10 / 10%+ customer (low-single-digit %)** — so the 26% customer is most likely *not* NVDA, and the concentration is elsewhere in the hyperscaler base. Both marks stated; not reconciled.
- **🔴 EML→SiPho SUBSTITUTION NOW HAS A CONTENT NUMBER AND A REASON IT IS EASY: "a SiPho transceiver has 50% REDUCED InP content versus an EML based transceiver while having significantly higher ACTIVE ALIGNMENT content… Everyone has an MZI-based PIC for 1.6T. It is TRIVIAL to pivot manufacturing from EML based to SiPho based. The issue is active alignment capacity/throughput, not the design itself"** (Irrational Analysis, 2026-08-04). ⚠️ **This sharpens the standing TD Cowen SiPh-substitution bear from a share argument into a per-module CONTENT argument — ~50% less InP per transceiver — and it removes the design-cycle friction the bull case has implicitly relied on (a pivot that is "trivial" cannot be slowed by qualification). Two offsets to hold beside it, from the same note: the value that leaves InP goes into ACTIVE ALIGNMENT, where the author says the Chinese module makers are "an order of magnitude better" than the West — so the pivot benefits Innolight/Eoptolink/TFC rather than LITE — and the SiPho PIC itself lands at [[TSEM]] (Tower), which LITE already uses. ⚠️ Net: a SiPho pivot moves content OUT of LITE's own InP fabs and INTO a foundry and an assembly step LITE does not own. Set against management's OFC-2026 rebuttal already on this page (EMLs de facto at 400G/lane; InP 91%→79% of AI lanes 2025→2030F per LightCounting) — unresolved, and the lane-share defence does not answer the per-module content point.**
- **⚠️ COMPETITIVE RANKING ON THE InP RAMP — a third party places LITE BEHIND [[COHR]]:** on Nokia's earnings call an analyst compared the InP ramp at COHR, *"already doing pretty well,"* and placed **LITE "a bit behind"** (relayed by FUNDA, "Deep|Optics", 2026-08-12). ⚠️ **Thin provenance (an analyst aside on a third company's call, via an independent newsletter) but it is the SECOND independent instance of that ranking, after Aurelion's bear #1 on 07-29 ("Lumentum still runs 4 inch wafers and is trying to catch up" vs COHR's 6-inch at ~2.25x usable area). It sits directly against JPM's 08-14 view that LITE gets the more significant FY27 GM expansion on EARLIER program ramps. Both marks stand; the divergence is a live reconciliation item, not a resolved one.**
- **⚠️ CUSTOMER CONCENTRATION IS NOW WORSE ON THE FILED ANNUAL BASIS, not just the quarterly one: Customer A 26.6% and Customer B 15.0% of FY26 net revenue ⇒ 41.6% combined, vs 31.4% in FY25** (FY26 10-K, 2026-08-17). ✅ **This CONFIRMS rather than contradicts the Aurelion 26%/12% quarterly mark above — the top-customer step-up is structural, not a single-quarter artefact — and it is the number to use in the concentration risk from here. Non-US shipment revenue 79.2%.**
- **🔴 CONVERTIBLE-NOTE CASH CALL, near-dated and net-new: all Notes are convertible at holder option in Q1 FY27, the whole balance sits in CURRENT liabilities as of 2026-06-27, and $757.8m of aggregate principal had already been requested for early conversion as of 2026-08-14 — with PRINCIPAL settled in CASH** (FY26 10-K). ⚠️ **~49% of the $1,554.3m outstanding, against a $3.17B cash balance. Not a solvency risk on those numbers, but it is a real claim on the cash the page treats as capex firepower for Greensboro and the second NVDA-funded fab, and the excess-value settlement is at the COMPANY's election — i.e. management chooses between more cash out and more dilution.**
- **Execution/manufacturing:** test-time and "boutique component" bottlenecks (gaskets, LEDs) gating OCS; six-inch InP edge-effects unproven at HVP; new-laser-recipe requals run ~6 months (followup call 2026-05-07).
- **InP merchant oversupply / SiPh-CW substitution:** simultaneous capacity adds across LITE/Sumitomo/Mitsubishi (+COHR internalizing) against a CW mix shift that multiplies effective supply (~4x+ transceivers per wafer); LITE most exposed given Components ~65% of revenue and smaller capex base; LTAs may mask the turn until it arrives (TD Cowen, 2026-03-11).
- **First concrete Chinese 200G EML entrant — watch-item, not yet a threat (Jefferies optical expert call · "Rick Li," 2026-07-14).** The call reiterates "no mature Chinese 200G EML vendor exists" today (200G EML remains dominated by Lumentum/AVGO/Sumitomo, un-substitutable, sold out through 2028) — but flags **DSBJ targeting mass production of 200G EML in 2H26**, the first crack in that moat. Directionally consistent with the broader China-localization pattern already on this page (CW lasers/Yuanjie done, passives >85% Chinese) — EML has simply been the furthest-out chokepoint; DSBJ's MP timeline is the thing to track.
- **Supply chain:** InP substrate dependence (Sumitomo LTA), semicap equipment lead times, China geopolitical/export exposure on InP (10-K FY25 risk factors; briefings).
- **Technology inflections / commoditization:** SiPh share gain at 1.6T, Chinese entrants, CPO/NPO cannibalizing pluggables; 3.2T reverts to EML (Hurlston, JPM 2026-05-18).
- **Multiple risk:** stock +136% YTD into May; ~30x vs ~20x historical (JPM, 2025-12-15); extended technicals; CPO-timing headlines drive sharp drawdowns.
- **⚠️🔴 EARNINGS-QUALITY / REVENUE-TIMING RISK (new, and not verifiable from disclosure): a broker asserts management admitted to shutting revenue off before quarter-end in March and suspects the same in June — *"this is a managed quarter"*** (Wolfe Research "Q2 earnings-season takeaways call", 2026-08-14). ⚠️ **Second-hand, no filing support, the finance name an ASR artefact — full treatment under *Debate / thesis → Bear*. Logged as a RISK rather than a fact because of what it would do to the evidentiary value of the prints: it makes revenue a floor, the beat size discretionary, and the GM beat partly an output of the same decision. It also cuts the other way on demand (an unbooked cushion), which is exactly why it is an earnings-QUALITY risk and not a demand risk.**

| **Scale-in / in-tray optics** _(new row, 2026-09-01)_ | 🔴 **The CEO put a clock on the tier himself, and this is the verbatim the page did not have: *"I think the thing that is going to be significant is the **scale-in**, and that’s the thing to watch over the **next 12 to 18 months**... Are you going to see it actually go **in-tray** and serve this high bandwidth connectivity **between memory and between GPUs**?"* (Hurlston, quoted from *"an investment conference on August 27"*).** ⚠️ **The publisher does NOT name the host. This page’s dated 08-27 event is the **Deutsche Bank Technology Conference**; the two are not asserted here to be the same session, and the quote is logged as a secondary rendering, not as a transcript.** | 🔭 **An independent publisher sizes the tier management named — the first bandwidth-tier ladder on this page. On [[NVDA]] Rubin public specs: scale-out via ConnectX-9 ≈ **1.6 Tb/s per GPU**; scale-up via NVLink 6 ≈ **14.4 Tb/s per direction (~9x scale-out)**; local HBM4 at **22 TB/s = 176 Tb/s**, another order of magnitude above — so *"each step inward is about 10x, and end-to-end the span is about 100x,"* and scale-in, which targets memory-class bandwidth, runs *"roughly an order of magnitude above scale-up per GPU, about 100x scale-out"* (FundaAI "Deep\|Optics: Scale-In Moves Optics Inside the Tray...", 2026-09-01). **Definition, stated up front: die-to-die and chip-to-memory optical links (XPU→HBM) over *"millimeters to about one meter."*** Timing: *"Scale-up optics ships starting in 2027 while **scale-in follows two to three years later, if not more**"* because the same lasers, engines, packaging and test flows must clear tighter in-tray thermal/reliability/test bars. Entry bar attributed to **LITE’s own CTO at ECOC 2025**: *"about **$0.10 per Gbps**, under **4 pJ/bit**, under **1 FIT**, and over **2 Tbps/mm** of density"* — and at that tier *"**silicon photonics and VCSELs are the leading candidates**... while **InP and TFLN fall short on density and power**."* LITE’s named asset is the **1060 nm monolithic 2D VCSEL-array platform** plus the IEEE 802.3 200G-per-lane multimode study group. | **✓ confirms the direction management put on the tape — and ⚠ NUANCES THE DATING HARD IN THE OTHER DIRECTION.** The CEO’s *"12 to 18 months"* is a **watch** window; the same note dates first scale-in product **two to three years after** the 2027 scale-up-optics ship date, i.e. **2029-2030+**, and adds *"no Hot Chips 2026 deck used scale-in for die-to-die optics."* ➤ **Nothing in this page’s FY27/FY28 bridge depends on scale-in and it must not be underwritten into one.** ⚠️ **The awkward second-order read for the LITE bull: at this reach the physics is said to favour **VCSEL and SiPh over InP** — InP scarcity is the moat this page runs on everywhere else. Logged as a variant read from an independent publisher with no model; supersedes nothing.** |
| **🆕 SUBSTRATE SUPPLY — the constraint management named itself** _(new row, 2026-09-02)_ | 🔴 **Management's own 08-27 words are the bar: the binding constraints on the Greensboro/NPO ramp are **TIME (qualification cycles — *"one year for NVIDIA"*), equipment lead times (reactors, e-beam litho), and SUBSTRATE** — *"despite a large deal with a Japanese supplier and another with [[AXTI]], **MORE SUBSTRATES MAY BE NEEDED**"*; and LITE is *"actively seeking SIMILAR COMMITMENTS from NPO customers BEFORE INVESTING in further capacity."* China exposure framed as a manageable tail risk mitigated by a Thailand/China/UK footprint (Lumentum mgmt, Deutsche Bank Technology Conference, 2026-08-27) | ✅🔴 **CONFIRMED BY THE COUNTERPARTY FOUR DAYS LATER, AND BY A MERCHANT COMPETITOR TWO DAYS AFTER THAT.** [[AXTI]] mgmt (BofA-hosted call, 2026-08-31): *"MORE THAN 18 MONTHS. It's **NOT BALANCED AT ALL**… we're on **ALLOCATION**… **we're NOT signing up for as much as the customer wants**"*, *"could be more like **2 YEARS OR MORE**"* — and only AXT and Sumitomo *"can move the needle"*, with **Sumitomo's doubling finishing only in 2028**, i.e. through the entire NPO pull-forward window. [[AVGO]] mgmt (Q3 FY26 call, 2026-09-02): *"demand for lasers — CW lasers, EML — is **FAR SURPASSING SUPPLY out there in the industry**."* ➤ **Supplier, customer and competitor now agree, on three separate days. [[AIXA]]'s *"easing by end-1H27"* (08-27) is the outlier of four dated views and should be weighted accordingly.** ⚠️⚠️ **TWO NUANCES THAT CUT AGAINST THIS PAGE. (a) The supplier says the China exposure is IRREDUCIBLE on this cycle's timeline — an ex-China greenfield is *"at least TWO YEARS away to PILOT production"* — and characterises LITE as having *"NO ALTERNATIVES"* and being *"VERY EAGER"* despite historically not wanting a China-routed supply chain. That is one-sided testimony from the party holding the leverage, and it does not match LITE's own "manageable tail risk" framing. (b) The supplier confirms it **has raised prices, will keep raising them, and has eliminated the China discount** (*"prices in China are COMPARABLE to the rest of the world"*) — a rising input cost running underneath the ~2x 200G EML ASP uplift. No magnitude given; do not model it.** | **✓ confirms the constraint · ⚠ nuances the China framing and the input cost** |

**Q1FY27 synthesis (opened 2026-08-11 · updated 2026-08-12):** the print resolved the *numbers* debate emphatically in the bulls' favour — beat on every line, GM +610bps vs Street, September revenue +8% and EPS +16% above Street, target model pulled in a quarter — and resolved **none** of the two debates that actually move the multiple: **scale-up CPO dating** (management's "first signs" language vs a 2029/2030 view from its own expert and from the buy side) and **margin durability through the transceiver ramp**. The flat reaction is the signal to trade off: a 16% EPS guide-beat that does nothing means the bar is now set at the whisper, not at consensus, into F1Q27. **➜ 08-12 UPDATE: the CALL (not the release) moved one of those two debates. Margin durability is now materially better answered — management RAISED the out-year OM target to 40–44% from 38–42%, and Jefferies dated the offsetting transceiver dilution ("softens margin expansion over the NTM," relief from 2H27). CPO/NPO dating did NOT resolve, but it changed shape twice: NPO got a date (additive TAM, late-2027 scale-up ramp) and, more usefully, LITE's shipment schedule was DECOUPLED from NVDA's deployment schedule ("NVDA's plans may push to 1H 2028, LITE's timeline has not changed… UHP shipments still 2H27"). Net: the bear that survives is positioning/catalyst-timing (MS: no formal NPO contract for "a couple of quarters," no catalyst "until the fall and into OFC next year"), not fundamentals — which is a materially weaker bear than the one this page carried into the print.**

**🆕 Q1FY27 synthesis — 08-17 update (the 10-K pass). The `Síntese` above is unchanged and stands; this appends what the FILING changed, which is a matter of MEASUREMENT rather than direction.** The print and the call left two debates open (scale-up CPO dating, margin durability) and the 10-K touches neither. What it does instead is give the page its first audited denominators, and two of them are worse than the page assumed. **(1) OCS is a ~$90m/yr business that was ~$52m in its best quarter, against forward claims of $400m in six months and ~$1.0–1.25bn/yr — an 11–14x ramp with a Thailand floor-space constraint attached, and a June quarter that quietly missed the ~$70m broker mark nobody scored. (2) Customer concentration at 26.6%/15.0% of FY26 is the FULL-YEAR figure, so the 26% top customer is structural.** Set against that, two things got better: **the charge-pump/scale-across leg is now FILED rather than IR-sourced, and laser-chip ASPs are confirmed RISING on 200G mix while transceiver ASPs fall — which is the company writing down the exact margin mechanism the bulls have been asserting.** The genuinely net-new item is the **convertible stack moving to current liabilities with $757.8m of principal already called for cash settlement** — absent from the release, absent from the call, and a real competitor for the capex dollars this page assumes are earmarked for Greensboro. ➤ **The trade into F1Q27 is unchanged in direction and sharper in test: OCS ≥$100m in the September quarter, or the $400M 2H-CY26 figure and the two bracketed relays above it all come down together.**

**🆕 Q1FY27 synthesis — 08-20 update (the Wolfe pass). The two syntheses above stand unchanged; this adds a THIRD kind of open question, and it is about the EVIDENCE rather than the forecast.** Wolfe’s 08-14 takeaways call is bullish on the ramp — LITE sits 3rd on its **top-ideas list** (behind [[CIEN]] and Celestica), is among the **largest upward revisions to Wolfe’s own 2028 sales expectations** *"with follow through on earnings"*, was a **post-print winner with T+2 follow-through**, ranks **#2 on the China-transceiver-ban theme**, and needs **no capital** — and it puts the first quarterly dollar figure on transceivers (**~$225m in June, ~$900m run-rate, target $3bn within five quarters**), which reconciles neatly with the filed ~$52m OCS back-solve inside the reported $356.9m Systems line. **But the same call asserts that the two prints the bulls are extrapolating from were MANAGED — revenue *"turned off before the end of the quarter"* once margin targets were met, on a claimed management admission.** ⚠️ **That changes no forecast on this page and is corroborated by nothing in the filings; what it changes is how much evidentiary weight the beat pattern can carry. The page’s two standing debates (scale-up CPO dating, margin durability) are now joined by a third: whether the prints are measurements or decisions. All three are testable at F1Q27 — alongside the newly dated Greensboro-LTA disclosure window and the OCS ≥$100m bogey.** ⚠️ **Also logged from the same call and NOT netted: an OM-target VARIANT (Wolfe: *"targeting 42 to 43%… at a $2 billion revenue run rate,"* versus *"38 to 40%… going back to the analyst day at OFC"*) that does not match the JPM 08-12 rendering already on this page (40–44%, raised from 38–42%). Same management target, two houses, the same week, two sets of numbers — do not average them.**

**🆕 Q1FY27 synthesis — 08-25 update (the full-transcript + Mizuho pass). The three syntheses above stand unchanged; this adds ONE number and ONE unresolved date.** The number is the **September OCS ceiling — *"below 150 million a quarter"* (JPM · Joe Cardoso, 2026-08-14)** — which moves no forecast but finally makes the **$400m 2H-CY26** figure ARITHMETICALLY testable rather than rhetorically testable: even at the TOP of JPM’s band, December has to carry **~$250m off a filed ~$52m June base**. **The date is CPO, and it is now a genuine three-way disagreement inside eleven days: management says NVDA demand for 2H-CY27 scale-up INCREASED and names the SemiAnalysis push-out report as wrong; JPM’s conference read is "CPO is not delayed, CY2028 the material year"; Mizuho, relaying SemiAnalysis directly, says "CPO is ~2-3 years away."** ⚠️ **Do not resolve this by counting houses. The useful observation is that Mizuho holds the bearish CPO date AND an Outperform on LITE at the same time, because its LITE case runs through NPO, EML and SiPho content rather than CPO — which is the same structure as management’s own $2bn-per-quarter bridge ("not a lot of scale-up optics"). ➤ Both the bull and the bear on this page can be right about CPO TIMING without either being right about LITE’s 2027, and that is the sharper way to hold the position into F1Q27.**

**🆕 Q1FY27 synthesis — 09-01 addendum (the FundaAI scale-in pass). The four syntheses above stand unchanged; this adds a TIER, not a number, and it cuts both ways.** 🔭 **FundaAI (Substack)** — *"Deep|Optics: Scale-In Moves Optics Inside the Tray, and the Bandwidth Opportunity Could 10x Scale-Up"*, **2026-09-01** ⚠️ **INDEPENDENT SUBSTACK PUBLISHER, NOT A BROKER — no rating, no price target, no house model** argues the next optical layer inward is **die-to-die and chip-to-memory links at millimetres to about a metre**, and that the memory tier it targets is *"the largest bandwidth tier in the system, roughly ten times the scale-up bandwidth per GPU and about one hundred times the scale-out."* **The bull half is that this page’s own CEO named it first** (*"the thing to watch over the next 12 to 18 months"*, 08-27) **and that the entry-bar spec is LITE’s own ECOC 2025 slide, not a third party’s ($0.10/Gbps, <4 pJ/bit, <1 FIT, >2 Tbps/mm).** **The bear half is threefold and should be held explicitly: (1) the publisher’s own timeline puts first scale-in product two-to-three years AFTER scale-up optics ships in 2027, i.e. 2029-30+; (2) it says InP *"falls short on density and power"* at in-tray reach, favouring VCSEL and SiPh — a different mix from the InP-scarcity trade this page runs on; and (3) it sizes the tier in BANDWIDTH ONLY, explicitly refusing a dollar TAM because *"per-lane and per-port pricing for in-tray optics is not yet set, and a dollar TAM would imply precision that does not exist."*** ✅ **What it DOES corroborate, from outside the sell-side: the **$2bn NVIDIA equity investment (March 2026, with multi-year purchase commitments)** this page carries at **+$2.02B to cash** (Q3FY26, 2026-05-06) — the rounded figure matches; and the **08-27 OCS marks** (*"shipments doubled sequentially,"* *"next-quarter OCS revenue above $100 million,"* Google referenced), which the note repeats and reads as **"we see Google as its most important OCS customer."** ⚠️ **Neither is new; both are now independently restated, which raises evidentiary standing and nothing else. No estimate, rating or bogey on this page moves.** ➤ **What to watch, in the publisher’s own words and directly testable here: *"Lumentum’s next two quarters against the CEO’s 12- to 18-month window and the above-$100 million quarterly OCS guide."*** (FundaAI "Deep|Optics: Scale-In Moves Optics Inside the Tray...", 2026-09-01)

Are you going to see it actually go **in-tray** and serve this high bandwidth connectivity between memory and between GPUs?"* (*"an investment conference on August 27"* — host not named by the publisher).** 🔴 **THE BANDWIDTH LADDER, WHICH IS THE GENUINELY NEW CONTENT: Rubin scale-out ConnectX-9 ~1.6 Tb/s per GPU → NVLink 6 at 1.8 TB/s per direction = 14.4 Tb/s (~9x) → local HBM4 at 22 TB/s = 176 Tb/s (~another 10x).** ⚠️ **The note is explicit that *"the thesis does not require optics to displace the HBM PHY, and local HBM stays electrical for as far as we can see"* — the 176 Tb/s figure is a COMPARISON POINT, not an addressable number. The addressable piece is *"the memory traffic that no longer fits inside the package"*: on the note’s own assumption that an external pool takes **20-40% of local HBM traffic**, that is **35-70 Tb/s per XPU**, and against the **7.2 Tbps** flagship pooled-fabric module announced at FMS this month it implies **five to ten such ports per XPU**, with *"first deployments starting below the band."* ⚠️ **The 20-40% band is the publisher’s ASSUMPTION (based on ~30 GB of KV cache per million-token sequence and context windows growing ~30x a year), not a disclosure — do not carry it as a datapoint.** 🔴 **THE LITE-SPECIFIC ENTRY BAR, ATTRIBUTED TO LITE’S OWN CTO AT ECOC 2025: *"parallel signaling, very high volumes, about $0.10 per Gbps, under 4 pJ/bit, under 1 FIT, and over 2 Tbps/mm of density. **Silicon photonics and VCSELs are the leading candidates at this tier, while InP and TFLN fall short on density and power.**"* ⚠️ **Read that against the InP-scarcity moat this page runs on: at in-tray reach the note routes value to VCSEL/SiPh, not InP. LITE’s named in-tier asset is the 1060 nm monolithic 2D VCSEL-array platform, pushed through the IEEE 802.3 200G-per-lane multimode study group — plus its OCS franchise.** ✅ **CORROBORATIONS, NOT NEW NUMBERS: NVIDIA’s **$2bn equity investment in March 2026 with multi-year purchase commitments** (this page: **+$2.02B to cash**, Q3FY26 call 2026-05-06 — rounded figures agree) and the **08-27 OCS marks** (*"OCS shipments doubled sequentially,"* *"guided next-quarter OCS revenue above $100 million,"* Google referenced), with the publisher adding its own read: **"we see Google as its most important OCS customer"** and **"Lumentum is also the leading OCS supplier."** ⚠️ **TIMELINE, AND IT IS THE BEAR SIDE: *"Scale-up optics ships starting in 2027 while scale-in follows two to three years later, if not more"*, and *"no Hot Chips 2026 deck used scale-in for die-to-die optics."* The term itself is contested — [[NVDA]] uses "Scale-In" at Hot Chips 2026 for a **BlueField-4 DPU services network (800 Gb/s per Vera Rubin compute tray)**, [[META]] for **chiplet-level DMA inside MTIA 400**, and Azure for autoscaling instance removal. **Three different technical meanings; the LITE meaning is the in-tray optical one, and a headline using the word proves nothing about which tier is being discussed.** ⚠️ **SOURCE CLASS: an independent Substack publisher with no rating, no PT and no model — the note carries NO dollar TAM by design. Nothing here supersedes a primary or broker-modelled figure on this page.** (FundaAI "Deep\|Optics: Scale-In Moves Optics Inside the Tray...", 2026-09-01) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Funda_on_Optics.html) |

Three dated positions, logged and NOT netted. ➤ The reconciliation is more useful than the date fight: Mizuho holds the bearish CPO date and the Outperform SIMULTANEOUSLY, because its LITE case runs through NPO, EML and SiPho content rather than CPO — structurally the same bridge management itself described to $2bn/quarter ("not a lot of scale-up optics"). LITE’s 2027 does not need CPO to work.** 🔴 **WHAT MIZUHO IS ACTUALLY FAVOURING, since the title says "Copper/Optics Upside" and the two are usually read as opposites: BOTH, and CPO is the thing it is bearish on. The upside call is *"strong copper"* (AEC, i.e. [[CRDO]] and [[ALAB]]) AND *"NPO SiPho/InP"* (i.e. LITE, [[COHR]], [[AAOI]]) — the bear leg is confined to CO-PACKAGED optics specifically. ➤ **That is a COHERENT position rather than a hedge: NPO and pluggables both consume external InP light sources, so pushing CPO out extends the EML/CW cycle rather than shrinking the optical TAM. Read that way, "CPO 2-3 years out" is bullish LITE’s next eight quarters and only bearish its 2029 mix.** ⚠️⚠️ **AND THIS IS NOW THE SHARPEST LIVE DISAGREEMENT IN THE OPTICAL COMPLEX, so it is logged in full and DELIBERATELY NOT RESOLVED. Four dated positions on one question:** **(1) Lumentum management, 2026-08-13 — NVDA demand for 2H-CY27 scale-up CPO has INCREASED, *"Nvidia is all in on CPO,"* SemiAnalysis named as wrong.** **(2) JPM · Joe Cardoso, 2026-08-14 — the supplier consensus at its conference is *"CPO is NOT delayed, with CY2028 the material year."*** **(3) Mizuho relaying SemiAnalysis, 2026-08-24 — *"CPO is ~2-3 years away."*** **(4) The DAMNANG archive on this page argues the OPPOSITE structurally, from physics rather than from channel checks: the CPO-Special series is titled "Why CPO Is Becoming INEVITABLE" and dates the copper wall to a convergence of reach, power and shoreline bandwidth density *"around 2027 to 2028"* — the same window management is defending (**DAMNANG (Substack)** — "Why CPO Is Becoming Inevitable (CPO Special Part 1)", **2026-03-18**; "The War of Light Has Begun", **2026-03-16**).** ➤ **The reconciliation this page should hold: INEVITABILITY and TIMING are different claims and the four sources are not actually arguing about the same one. ELS module margin is 50–60%," from his own BOM modelling at both InP-die and ELSFP-module level — the range driven by uncertainty on ASP, i.e. "how fast Lumentum is hiking prices on their kickass products with no real competition (Broadcom also sold out)."** ⚠️ **Set that against the ~50% corporate GM and the "transceiver GM in the 20s" the page already carries: it implies the UHP/ELS mix shift is worth several hundred bps of corporate GM on its own, and it is the quantitative version of JPM's 08-12 "ELS at ABOVE-CORPORATE margins."** 🔴 **THE ENGINEERING ARGUMENT, WHICH INVERTS THE STANDARD NPO BEAR: integrating lasers into the optical engine is TECHNICALLY WORSE, not better — "keeping the laser drive current clean is much more difficult if it is co-integrated with the optical engine," and keeping it at a stable temperature is "far more difficult when you have an optical engine with heaters and heavy IO electrical circuits kicking out dynamic noise." MODE HOPS (laser flicker ⇒ link-flap and re-train) and laser death are the failure modes; "if any laser dies, your entire optical engine is dead and so is whatever it is soldered to. Dead lasers in ELSFP is easy to deal with — just swap the module." His conclusion: "integrated lasers with optical engines (NPO, CPO, whatever) is a TERRIBLE IDEA. The only reason people are going for this path is there is a crippling photon/InP shortage… My view was that nobody would actually do this integration. Apparently several big fish are. In a shortage that is a bull for whoever owns the InP; in a glut it is a cost disadvantage — and it is a concrete mechanism behind the "mid-power NPO lasers are not obviously less differentiated" claim management made on the call.** Also: **"they reduced cavity length AGAIN while meeting the same linewidth requirement"** — the same die-shrink lever the 05-08 row on this page flagged as a reliability risk, now executed a second time without a linewidth give-up. Corroborates MS's 08-12 "CW-vs-EML margin gap closing as die size shrinks." |

| **08-12** | **`[Q1FY27 window]` R&Co Redburn · Mike Harrison — "To The Point: Strong quarter, better outlook"** (Research; **Buy, PT $1,270.27**, price ref $820.59, mkt cap $72,803m; written 11:55 / published 11:58 UK, priced off the 08-11 close) | **produto / demanda** | **bull** | *"Underneath what was a reasonable print and guide… the underlying drivers in the business are strengthening."* 🔴 **The only house on the day to put a unit on scale-up CPO content — and the full derivation is now on this page (read the primary, 2026-08-13 ingest):** starting from Hurlston's pre-print **8,000 lasers per rack**, Redburn assumes that pertains to the **VR Ultra NVL576** (Oberon, 8 × MGX NVL racks × 72 Rubin Ultra GPUs) and builds **1GW facility → 1.15 PUE → 870MW IT → 90% to GPU racks → 783MW of rack power → at 250kW/rack ⇒ ~3,130 racks/GW ⇒ 225,391 GPUs/GW ⇒ 111 lasers per GPU ⇒ 25m lasers per GW.** ⚠️ **Every step is a stated assumption (PUE, the 90% GPU-rack allocation, and a 250kW rack draw vs media reports of 190–230kW for VR200 NVL72) — carry the 25m as an ESTIMATE with a documented ladder, not a company number.** 🔴 **The scale-out comparison is what makes it useful: NVDA discloses 576 TX lanes per Quantum-X InfiniBand CPO switch and 32 lanes per External Light Source ⇒ 18 ELS/switch × 8 lasers = 144 lasers per CPO; a Lambda ~41,000-GPU cluster needing 1,440 scale-out CPO switches ⇒ ~207,360 lasers ⇒ ~5 lasers per GPU. So SCALE-UP CARRIES ~22x THE LASER DENSITY OF SCALE-OUT PER GPU** — the cleanest quantification on this page of why scale-up is the generational leg. Redburn expects the number to go **UP** at Kyber (144-GPU rack, and NVDA's own blog says NVL576 uses *"copper and direct optical connections"* while Kyber scales up *"using similar direct optical interconnects for rack-to-rack"*). 🔴 **OCS, net-new and specific: the 1Q27 guide "includes the first triple-digit OCS revenue quarter"; LITE's INTERNAL OCS manufacturing capacity "tops out at c$1.25bn of revenue per year," and it has started adding contract-manufacturer capacity (assumed on stream in 6–12 months) — which implies management sees OCS revenue ABOVE $1.25bn/yr by 2027. Google is expected to keep making some OCS internally, but LITE "expects to absorb the majority of Google's incremental OCS volumes and become their leading supplier in 2027"** (corroborates the Jefferies "majority share at Google in C27" surprise logged the same day, from a second house). New SKUs: higher- and lower-port-count OCS incl. **in-tray offerings for GPU redundancy**. **NPO lasers split into two categories: mid-power 120–150mW integrated inside the optical engine, and a high-power ELS laser "similar to the 400mW laser used for CPO"; management rejects the view that NPO weakens LITE, arguing the mid-power part is HARDER because of integration and power density in a small optical engine.** **Scale-across trend table (y/y): narrow-linewidth lasers 70% → "8th consecutive quarter of growth" → 120% → 130% (1Q–4Q26); pump lasers "record quarter" → 90% → 80% → 80%. Pump share 70–80%, sold out for the foreseeable future, 3-year LTAs signed, 4x shipment increase expected over the next several quarters as Rose Orchard ramps.** **Mix: 200G now 25% of EML revenues; little change in the EML supply/demand imbalance; Japan-fab efficiencies have freed capacity to diversify into 200G CW; the CW-vs-EML GM gap has narrowed on smaller die AND LITE can charge a PREMIUM for CW because higher reliability lifts customers' transceiver yields.** **Numbers vs Visible Alpha: 4Q26 rev $1,006m (+2%), GM 50.4% (+170bp), OM 36.6% (+120bp), EPS $3.23 (+8%); 1Q27 guide rev $1,250m (+8%), OM 40.0% (+250bp), EPS $4.20 (+15%). The 1Q27 guide hits the OFC-26 intermediate $1.25bn target one quarter early and its OM guide is already at the MIDPOINT of the 38–42% band OFC attached to the $2bn/qtr level; management indicated the target OM range could rise 100–200bp "although the upside versus FY28 consensus appears modest" (VA cons FY27 $5,765m / 39.2% OM; FY28 $8,670m / 42.8% OM).** Systematic: IDEAS score 84.2/100, Ests-Mom EPS 85.4 but **3m change −10.2**; relative perf **−25.3% 3m / +488.3% 12m**. |

Lack of clarity on CPO/NPO and CONCERN ON LASER CAPACITY COMING ONLINE FROM CHINESE PLAYERS biases us more conservatively into prints, but bias more positive towards COHR given potential margin catalyst."** **The bogeys, stated: F4Q revenue ~$1bn, non-GAAP GM ~50%, non-GAAP OM ~37% — "the reason for LESS GROSS MARGIN ACCRETION Q/Q would just be MOST OF THE CAPACITY COMING ONLINE IN TRANSCEIVERS vs ramps of OCS / CPO yet." F1Q guide expected ~$1.175-1.2bn revenue with ~51% GM / ~38% OM, i.e. "JUST SHORT of Analyst Day targets of $1.25bn for the March '27 quarter."** ⚠️ **"POSITIVE CATALYST FOR LITE WOULD BE BRINGING FORWARD THE $1.25BN REVENUE RUN RATE TARGET TWO QUARTERS to the September quarter, SOMETHING WE THINK UNLIKELY ON PRINT." That is the single event that would move the stock, and MS is explicitly betting against it — worth holding as the asymmetry rather than the base case.** **THE ARCHITECTURE CALL IS THE MOST USEFUL PART, AND IT IS A SHIFT: "Expect NPO to receive GREATER STRATEGIC EMPHASIS this quarter as the next optical architecture approaching commercial viability… management commentary is likely to place relatively greater emphasis on NEAR-PACKAGE OPTICS (NPO) VERSUS CPO, given NPO's positioning as a MORE SERVICEABLE AND LESS COMPLEX step toward moving optics closer to the XPU. NPO also appears capable of attracting a BROADER CUSTOMER BASE, particularly among NON-NVIDIA PLATFORMS seeking improved power efficiency without immediately adopting a fully co-packaged design. CPO remains an important longer-term opportunity, but NPO increasingly looks like the MORE TANGIBLE BRIDGE." MS's own view on the risk: "we don't see DELAYS to CPO timing… our concern is more around DEGREE OF ADOPTION with NPO seeming to gain more traction." Near-term results stay tied to conventional pluggables — 800G adoption and the 1.6T ramp — not to emerging architectures, and "much of the underlying laser and optical-component stack is SHARED between NPO and CPO," so both companies are positioned across the transition.** **Substrate supply — the [[AXTI]] read: "AXTI suggests the InP SUBSTRATE BOTTLENECK IS BEGINNING TO EASE, leaving INTERNAL FAB CAPACITY AS THE MORE IMPORTANT CONSTRAINT." AXTI on track to DOUBLE InP substrate capacity in 2026 and expand slightly beyond in 2027; China export permits "becoming more regular" though still requiring a supporting customer order; supply tight enough that customers use legacy 2-inch wafers alongside 3-inch and 4-inch, with 6-inch progressing but not in volume production. LITE's long-term supply agreement RESERVES SUBSTRATE CAPACITY THROUGH 2031; COHR's agreement supports 6-inch development.** ⚠️ **The valuation reset is real and cuts the other way: LITE/COHR now sit at ~20x FY28 P/E versus 25x buyside CY28 earlier in the quarter, "a MORE BALANCED SETUP" after hyperscaler EPS follow-through and the AI-capex selloff. Key risks named: a broader AI valuation correction, slower capacity execution, and continued EML constraints. MS does not see FQ4 as the catalyst that resolves the CPO/NPO argument — "OCP likely greater positive catalyst."** (Morgan Stanley · Meta Marshall / Antonio Jaramillo, 2026-08-03) |

| 07-29 | Aurelion Research/PhotonCap · Lumentum mgmt meeting (Kathryn Ta, VP IR) | supply | **mixed** | 🔴 **The capacity answer is the story, and it cuts both ways.** Management, asked about capacity in the context of elastic token demand: "**they are adding as fast as they can. Two fabs in Japan produce most of the world's supply of EML lasers, and capacity there is up eight-fold in two and a half years**," offered as representative of adds elsewhere; **five fabs now, including a new one starting to ramp that is not yet producing wafers**; tools added at every site, building shells expanded at several. **Supply/demand imbalance restated at roughly 30% greater demand than is physically suppliable; all EML capacity locked in LTAs through FY27; customers exceeding LTA coverage pay premium prices; non-signers "risk losing their entire allocation of supply"; double-digit 200G EML price increases expected in FY26 on no second source; a McKinsey study has 800G transceiver production short of demand by ~half in FY27, with shortfalls through 2029.** ⚠️ **Aurelion flags the contradiction itself: "Eight-fold EML capacity in two and a half years cuts against a pure scarcity thesis. Lumentum is racing to relieve the bottleneck that gives it pricing power" — they read it as confidence in durable demand.** **PhotonCap dissents on mechanism: "wafer starts and qualified yield at the newest spec are different numbers. A fab-level capacity multiple does not relieve a 200G per lane shortage one to one, because the binding constraint is yield at the highest power bins, not floor space… the eight fold number overstates the relief, and… the shortage premium itself is not durable while the margin that survives lives in yield and mix, not in the shortage price."** Net for the model: demand/supply gap confirmed by management, **but the *reason to pay* migrates from shortage price to yield and mix.** (Lumentum mgmt + Aurelion Research + PhotonCap technical comment, "A Conversation with Lumentum", 2026-07-29) |
| 07-29 | Aurelion Research/PhotonCap · Lumentum mgmt meeting | produto | **bull** | 🔴 **Two forward items: management re-dates copper's wall, and PhotonCap establishes a third light-source platform.** **Management: co-packaged optics today is "opportunistic rather than forced" — NVDA deploys it because a faster, more power-efficient laser frees power that gets redirected to the GPUs — and "copper has not run out of headroom yet." She puts copper hitting the wall in LATE 2027, which is when "the first scale-up CPO deployments arrive, or MPO fiber connectors" for customers wanting a different configuration, driven by link lengths beyond roughly three meters rack to rack.** On competency ranking over 3/5/10 years: **InP laser chip manufacturing first, "and she was not close on the ranking," framed as architecture agnostic ("customers cannot escape needing photons"); MEMS/optical switching second and expected to become "another pillar," already handling spine replacement and cluster scale-out for Google; a VCSEL branch on the roadmap for chip-to-chip, raised by her unprompted.** **PhotonCap's correction is the net-new bull item: "'A VCSEL branch on the roadmap' is weaker than the reality. Lumentum has manufactured GaAs VCSELs at consumer volume for smartphone 3D sensing for years, with more than 10 billion emitters shipped per company disclosure. At OFC 2026 it showed that base extended to AI: a 1060nm VCSEL and photodetector 2D monolithic array co-packaged with a host ASIC, targeting slow and wide scale-up protocols such as UCIe and PCIe, with operation above 150°C and channel sparing." Because VCSELs live on GaAs not InP, LITE holds three light-source platforms at once — InP EML, InP high-power CW, GaAs VCSEL — "a stronger architecture hedge than the paraphrase suggests."** Also from PhotonCap, the frame that reprices the CPO-delay bear: **an integration-driven CPO delay "pushes CW and ELSFP revenue out while extending the EML cycle," because demand "stays in pluggables longer, and pluggables are EML intensive, which is the line that is sold out today" — so cannibalization "flips into a cushion in the delayed CPO scenario."** ⚠️ **The late-'27 mark contradicts LITE's own Director of Silicon Photonics R&D (07-23: CPO trial ~2028, hyperscaler deployment ~2030, GPU-GPU copper for ≥1 more generation). Left on the page, flagged.** (Lumentum mgmt via Aurelion Research + PhotonCap technical comment, 2026-07-29) |
| 07-29 | Aurelion Research/PhotonCap · Lumentum mgmt meeting | competicao | **mixed** | 🔴 **Management's three defences, and the authors' three rebuttals — plus the cleanest statement yet that second-sourcing is already happening.** **Management: (1) "competition thins out as power ratings climb" — 150 to 400 milliwatt lasers at high volume, high yield and high reliability are difficult, and "she does not see many players able to do for Nvidia what Lumentum does today"; (2) survivorship — "Lumentum and Coherent are the two companies left standing from telecom," with "Coherent stronger at the systems level and working to catch up on the component side"; (3) China — new entrants add capacity "mostly in lower power lasers," and Lumentum's internal comparison found materially higher yields in its finished transceivers using its own lasers, better spec uniformity unit to unit, better reliability in test and field.** ⚠️ **Aurelion's rebuttals: the power argument "is the one we believe," but "saying you win at the top end also admits you may lose at the bottom"; the telecom-survivorship argument is "the weakest" because "Broadcom and Marvell have plenty of money, and they don't need the best laser in the world. They only need one that is good enough to put on their own chip"; and the China evidence "came from Lumentum's own lab… Nobody outside the company has checked it," which is why they hold the China call with the least conviction of their four.** **Bear #1 is Coherent's 6-inch line: 4→6-inch gives "roughly 2.25 times the usable area per wafer," more die at lower cost, and "Lumentum still runs 4 inch wafers and is trying to catch up"; COHR's InP lasers "also sold out through 2027." The bite: LITE framed its own edge as bits per square area of InP, so "a competitor with more than twice the usable area per wafer makes direct headway on that metric."** **PhotonCap on second sourcing: "second sourcing is in progress, not a hypothesis. NVIDIA's technical blog names three ELS vendors, Lumentum, Sumitomo, and Coherent. The same day Lumentum got its $2B, Coherent got an identical $2B. Coherent's OFC 2026 release formalizes its 6 inch InP ramp and a 400mW CW laser, and AAOI announced sampling of a 400mW narrow linewidth laser in December 2025. The time axis is what matters. Competing capacity lands outside the FY27 LTA window."** And on China, an update to the page's Zetta/DSBJ thread: **Zetta "no longer fits either box, not the low power dismissal and not the imminent catch-up call" — mass-producible 100G PAM4 EML at OECC 2025, a 200G-per-lane device at ACP in November 2025, so "device level performance deserves to be taken seriously now"**, with hyperscale qualification (spec distribution across temperature/lifetime, unit-to-unit variation, RIN) the remaining gate — "proven by years of volume data, not by announcements." **PhotonCap's moat definition, which is the most usable framing here: not peak power but "qualified usable power at operating temperature, multiplied by spectral quality, reliability, and high volume yield" (RIN / COD / SMSR / FIT) — "headline specs are what challengers close first. Distribution tails, noise, and lifetime statistics close last."** (Lumentum mgmt via Aurelion Research + PhotonCap technical comment, 2026-07-29) |

| 07-29 | Aurelion Research/PhotonCap · Lumentum mgmt meeting | demanda | **neutral** | **The token-economics exchange — management declines the frame, and that is the answer worth having.** Asked directly whether models getting cheaper per token (Kimi K3, GPT 5.6, the latest Grok) and elastic token demand shape capacity expansion or efficiency innovation: **"Lumentum is at the 'plumbing' layer of the data center, two or three steps removed from any token economics. The number they optimise is power efficiency per bit, and the route to it is higher power lasers. The more bits moved per square area of indium phosphide, the more value delivered. Speed and efficiency are the same problem to them; faster transmission is how joules per bit comes down."** **Aurelion's read is the investable part: incentive alignment — "higher power and higher speed make the link more efficient and carry higher ASP at the same time. Lumentum gets paid to build what its customers actually need,"** with hyperscaler cost-per-token pressure running "all the way down to the plumbing." Their broader caution on the inference mix shift belongs here too: **"training is the most communication intensive workload, and the way that optics have traditionally traded is around training economics. Inference is less network intensive… so a mix shift toward inference does not translate to a 1:1 demand for optics"** — closing over time via longer context windows, heavier routing, disaggregated prefill/decode and agentic usage, plus geographically distributed inference driving DCI. Net: **"optics at large are insulated from model commoditization, because they benefit from bits moved rather than tokens generated."** Cross-filed [themes/tokenmaxxing.md](themes/tokenmaxxing.md). (Lumentum mgmt via Aurelion Research + Aurelion "Thoughts", 2026-07-29) |

**Quarter synthesis:** the intra-quarter flow is confirmatory of management's thesis — BTG corroborates the LITE/COHR moat on the UHP light source for CPO and that demand exceeds supply (the constraint is capacity, not orders). The debate has not shifted from demand to fundamentals, but rather to the **multiple**: BofA holds at NEUTRAL ("upside well priced in") with the only nuance being the price, not the product. **07-11/07-13 add two constructive marks:** Mizuho reiterates Outperform (PT $1,100), tagging LITE a "critical supplier" to the >$1.4T-2028E DC-capex ramp, and MS's updated Scale-Up Primer names LITE a "clearest beneficiary" of optical moving into scale-up (rebuilding its model for CPO % and *raising* 2027E rev/EPS to $5,668M/$18.37) — MS's ~$70bn-by-2030 scale-up TAM and "sell-offs overdone" framing directly counter the CPO-timing wobble, while pushing meaningful scale-up CPO adoption out to Feynman (small 2028, stronger 2029+), consistent with the existing CPO-timing bear thread. **07-14 adds two more:** Rothschild/Redburn's AI-monetisation strategy screen names **LITE a North American top Buy** with the strongest screen profile of the group (adding ~80% capacity into surging demand, NVDA-backed; 6-mo sales-growth Δ +57% / FCF +194.5%) — a strategy-level endorsement, no new PT; and a fresh **650 Group/Weckel** expert call gives the market-level optical map (CPO partial-2028/meaningful-2029, NPO the interim step for Amazon, OCS ~$4.5–5B, DCI ~$30B/thirds, copper pushed longer, 3.2T not material till 2029) — slightly more measured than management on CPO timing but supportive of pluggables/OCS near-term. **07-16: Citi's new InP supply model** hardens the supply-gated bull for LITE specifically — supply below demand through 2028 (EML gap 33%/20%/11%), LITE the 100G/200G EML + UHP-laser leader capturing the pricing power (Citi bakes in a further +15% FY27E price increase, raising FY27E/28E EPS +6%/+5%), and Citi opens an *upside* Catalyst Watch into the Jun-Q print (11 Aug) — with the NPO-laser requirement (~similar to CPO) and NVDA's still-tiny direct exposure both framed as under-appreciated positives. **07-23: a Lumentum Director expert call (silicon-photonics R&D)** ties the two threads together — the supply-gated bull (SiPh foundry / lasers / DSP all sold out, LTAs required, laser-power-sharing to economize) alongside the CPO-timing bear (trial ~2028 / hyperscaler deploy ~2030, GPU-GPU copper for ≥1 more generation, SiPh confined to neo-clouds today) — while confirming the 6-inch III-V NC fab as a capacity commitment and the durable China EML moat (3-5-yr gap that "won't close" on substrate access), and putting units on the market (~63M 800G this year). **07-29 is the most consequential mark of the quarter because it is management, not a broker or an expert: an Aurelion Research meeting with VP-IR Kathryn Ta (published jointly with PhotonCap) confirms the two pillars — the ">30% greater demand than is physically suppliable" imbalance, all EML capacity under LTAs through FY27, and copper hitting the wall in "late 2027" with the first scale-up CPO deployments arriving then (link lengths beyond ~3m rack-to-rack) — while simultaneously handing the bears their best number: Japanese EML capacity is up EIGHT-FOLD in two and a half years, LITE now runs five fabs with a sixth ramping, and EML production is expanding ~40%/yr. Aurelion says so itself ("cuts against a pure scarcity thesis"), and PhotonCap converts it into the sharper claim: "the eight fold number overstates the relief… the shortage premium itself is not durable while the margin that survives lives in yield and mix, not in the shortage price." The practical consequence for the model is a shift in what you are underwriting — from scarcity pricing to yield/mix durability — not a cut to demand.** **Two genuine contradictions are now on the page and stay there: (i) management's late-'27 scale-up CPO date vs LITE's own Director of Silicon Photonics R&D on 07-23 (trial ~2028, hyperscaler deploy ~2030, GPU-GPU copper ≥1 more generation), and vs MS's 07-28 markdown of 2028 CPO *scale*; (ii) "Lumentum still runs 4 inch wafers and is trying to catch up" (Aurelion bear #1, against Coherent's 6-inch at ~2.25x usable area) vs the 07-23 confirmation that LITE bought a 6-inch III-V fab in North Carolina.** **Net-new and additive: PhotonCap establishes a THIRD light-source platform (GaAs VCSEL — >10bn emitters shipped, a 1060nm VCSEL+PD 2D monolithic array co-packaged with a host ASIC at OFC 2026 for UCIe/PCIe scale-up, >150°C, channel sparing) alongside InP EML and InP high-power CW — a materially stronger architecture hedge than the page previously carried; the frame that an integration-driven CPO delay EXTENDS the EML cycle rather than killing it (cannibalization "flips into a cushion"); channel numbers on the CW ramp (NVDA guidance to LITE+COHR ~40MM CW units in January → ~100MM by April/May, back-solving to ~200-300k CPO switches in 2027 and 600-800k in 2028, per Global Semi Research); the McKinsey 800G shortfall (~half of demand in FY27, through 2029); a second, ground-up NVDA-funded US fab distinct from Greensboro; Greensboro revenue contribution "late 2027 at the earliest" (~6 quarters from the May call, slightly earlier than the page's 2028 mark); and a sharply higher concentration read (two customers at 26% and 12% of fiscal Q3 2026 revenue). Sell-side dispersion widened: TD Cowen cut to $800 from just over $990, UBS re-affirmed a hold, Citi held Buy $1,100 with a catalyst watch, the full range now $900-$1,400, stock ~20% below its all-time high.** **LITE reports 8/11 AMC — the late-'27-vs-2030 CPO timing split and the yield-vs-shortage margin question are the two things to press.**

Aurelion: eight-fold "cuts against a pure scarcity thesis"; **TD Cowen PT $990→$800, UBS hold, Citi Buy $1,100 + catalyst watch, range $900-$1,400**, stock ~20% below ATH, "most of the photonics supercycle has been realized"; Greensboro (ex-Qorvo, GaAs→InP, 5th InP fab) revenue **~6 quarters from the May call = late 2027 at the earliest**, plus a **second, ground-up NVDA-funded US fab**; **EML production +40%/yr** vs peak-priced commitments 18 months ahead of volume; **COHR 6-inch = ~2.25x usable area/wafer, "Lumentum still runs 4 inch"**, COHR InP sold out through 2027; **Global Semi Research channel check: NVDA CW guidance to LITE+COHR ~40MM units Jan → ~100MM by Apr/May → ~200-300k CPO switches 2027, 600-800k 2028** (lines up with TSMC COUPE); **SemiAnalysis 2026-06-09: scale-out CPO down through FY27, scale-up to FY29 on a single limited ASIC**; **McKinsey: 800G transceiver output ~half of demand in FY27, shortfalls through 2029**; NVDA 72→**576-GPU domains by 2027**, Rubin bi-directional copper degrades at **448G/lane**. PhotonCap: **eight-fold "overstates the relief" (yield at top power bins, not floor space) → shortage premium not durable, margin lives in yield and mix**; moat = **qualified usable power at temperature × spectral quality × reliability × volume yield (RIN/COD/SMSR/FIT)**; **EML = today's cash flow, high-power CW = next mix shift**; **three platforms — InP EML + InP high-power CW + GaAs VCSEL (>10bn emitters; OFC 2026 1060nm VCSEL/PD 2D monolithic array co-packaged with host ASIC for UCIe/PCIe, >150°C, channel sparing)**; integration-driven CPO delay **extends the EML cycle** (cannibalization "flips into a cushion"); **NVDA blog names three ELS vendors — Lumentum, Sumitomo, Coherent**; COHR OFC 2026 6-inch InP + **400mW CW**, AAOI **400mW narrow-linewidth sampling Dec 2025**; **Zetta 100G PAM4 EML at OECC 2025 + 200G/lane at ACP Nov 2025** ("no longer fits either box"); **NVDA's own material puts CPO at 3.5x lower power than pluggables**; **AMD+Cerebras July joint inference solution** as evidence inference moves a lot of data.

- **🔴🔴 2026-09-02 (/run-inbox — 2 READ-THROUGH sources, both COMPANY PRIMARIES: (a) AXT mgmt (Gary Fisher, CFO) + Leslie Green (IR), BofA-hosted call, 2026-08-31; (b) Broadcom mgmt (Charlie Kawwas / Hock Tan), Q3 FY26 earnings call, 2026-09-02 — optical/laser section only). 1 new `## Current state` block, 1 correction bullet appended to the existing 09-02 AVGO block, 2 `## Intra-quarter` log rows, 1 NEW `Sinal vs gestão` row (**Substrate supply**), 2 `## Sources` entries.   ✅🔴 **THE POINT OF THE RUN: LITE'S OWN 08-27 CONSTRAINT IS NOW CONFIRMED BY THE COUNTERPARTY AND BY A COMPETITOR, ON THREE SEPARATE DAYS. Management said *"despite a large deal with a Japanese supplier and another with [[AXTI]], MORE SUBSTRATES MAY BE NEEDED."* The supplier, 08-31: *"MORE THAN 18 MONTHS. It's NOT BALANCED AT ALL… we're on ALLOCATION to customers. We're on SHORTAGES with customers. **We're signing key long-term supply agreements, but we're NOT signing up for as much as the customer wants**"*, and *"at least 18 months and could be more like 2 YEARS OR MORE."* A merchant competitor, 09-02: *"demand for lasers — CW lasers, EML — is FAR SURPASSING SUPPLY out there in the industry"* ([[AVGO]] / Hock Tan). ➤ **Customer, supplier and competitor now agree. [[AIXA]]'s 08-27 *"InP substrate bottlenecks easing by end-1H27"* is the outlier of four dated views, and the tension this page has been holding since 08-27 is resolved in LITE's favour by the supplier itself.** ➤ **Filed as a new `Sinal vs gestão` row rather than a bullet, because it re-scores a management statement.**
  🔴 **THE MOST MATERIAL NEW FACT FOR THE GREENSBORO/NPO BRIDGE: the JAPANESE half of LITE's substrate book does not add capacity until 2028. *"SUMITOMO announced that they would double capacity as well, and **they'll FINISH THAT IN 2028**"* — against AXT doubling in 2026 (*"with GREAT EASE AND CONVICTION… we probably are almost there already, and we will SURPASS that goal"*) and **doubling again in 2027** (*"we have a plan to do that. It's fairly RISK FREE"*). ➤ **LITE has pulled its NPO ramp into late-2027/early-2028. On the supplier's own timeline, essentially every incremental substrate wafer available to Lumentum before 2028 has to come from AXT. ⚠️ It is AXT characterising Sumitomo's schedule — the least disinterested source available for it — and it needs a Sumitomo confirmation before it is underwritten.**
  ⚠️⚠️ **THE UNCOMFORTABLE ITEM, LOGGED IN FULL BECAUSE IT IS ABOUT LITE BY NAME AND IT CONTRADICTS LITE'S OWN FRAMING: *"**LUMENTUM historically has said they REALLY DON'T WANT A SUPPLY CHAIN TO RUN THROUGH CHINA, but they're VERY EAGER, they're a great partner of us today**"* and *"**[[COHR]] AND LUMENTUM IN PARTICULAR have been RETICENT IN THE PAST to have a supply chain through China and are TODAY RECOGNISING it's a reality of the day that we're all in this together**"* (Green), with Fisher's *"we explain the logic and… **they really have NO ALTERNATIVES. So everybody cooperates.**"* ➤ **Two readings, both carried: it validates the scarcity from the seller's side, and it is a bargaining-power problem on LITE's most critical input. ⚠️ ONE-SIDED TESTIMONY — this is the SELLER characterising the BUYER and LITE has not said it. It sits in direct tension with LITE's 08-27 presentation of the China exposure as a manageable tail risk mitigated by a Thailand/China/UK footprint. Both are on the page; neither is netted.** **And the exposure is irreducible on this cycle's clock: an ex-China greenfield is *"at least TWO YEARS away to PILOT production"* (~4+ years to volume), so the available mitigation is LOGISTICAL — routing via Taiwan or Europe and doing *"something meaningfully transformative to the wafer"* — not geographic.**
  ⚠️🔴 **SUPERSEDED — MAGNITUDE OF BROADCOM'S CAPACITY ADD: ~~*"we have ALREADY DOUBLED and will increase even more"*~~ (Jefferies Asia · Conor O'Mara, verbatim call notes, 2026-09-02 — a RELAY) → the primary has Charlie Kawwas saying the US and Singapore **EML / CW / VCSEL indium-phosphide factories are *"MORE THAN TRIPLING THEM YEAR ON YEAR"***, with capacity *"already expanded for this year"* and *"increasing it significantly for the NEXT TWO YEARS"*, and **Q3 capex $532m → Q4 guide $1.4bn**. **The relay's quoted line is left intact where it is attributed to Jefferies; the corrected magnitude is stated in `## Current state` and in the new Full-log row.** ➤ **This matters specifically on THIS page because the offset already logged here — *"the other duopolist is ADDING CAPACITY… a ~2x ASP uplift on a supply-short node is exactly the condition that invites capacity"* — turns on the size of the add. A >3x y/y multi-year expansion is a bigger threat to the 200G EML ASP than a one-time doubling, and it lands directly against management's own 3.2T *"double whammy"* call, which assumes the duopoly still is one at that node.**
  **OTHER NET-NEW LITE-RELEVANT CONTENT FROM THE AXT CALL, one line each: LITE named among AXT's qualified tier-one customers (with Intel, Broadcom, [[COHR]]), *"most of them tell us that we have the BEST PRODUCT QUALITY"*; the LTA differentiation confirmed from the seller's side — of three LTAs, *"the COHERENT agreement is really focused on SIX-INCH"*, consistent with MS 08-03 (LITE's reserves CAPACITY through 2031); 🔴 **AXT now RATIONS LTAs — *"we want to be able to maybe PICK the guys that are going to be the biggest NEEDLE MOVERS"* — which makes LITE's existing reservation a competitive asset and makes the *"$43.5m + $43.5m is a floor, not a ceiling"* inference this wiki drew on 08-13 harder to execute, because incremental volume now has to be WON rather than bought**; ⚠️ **input cost — pricing GLOBALISED (China ASPs now comparable to RoW, removing the China-discount argument), *"we ARE raising prices. We HAVE been raising prices and they're GOING TO KEEP GOING UP"*, though *"NOT the price leader"*, with the third-party press price levels DISPUTED by management as *"overstating… stretching"* and no substitute given — logged as directional, NOT modelled**; ✅ **demand-duration agreement — *"NPO… will probably start in 2027… continuing to ramp over the course of '28"*, with **scale-in** (board-to-board within a rack, *"barely started yet"*) hitting harder in **2H 2027**, i.e. the supplier independently uses the same term Hurlston named as his 12-18-month monitorable**; **GaAs/VCSEL — high-end VCSEL lasers *"ALSO NEED AN INDIUM PHOSPHIDE DETECTOR"*, so *"it can be GOOD FOR US BOTH WAYS"*, which means InP CONTENT erosion is smaller than the EML SHARE erosion in management's own 70-80%→40-50% arithmetic**; **six-inch — AXT is now INSTALLING SIX-INCH FURNACES and can core four-inch ingots out of six-inch boules, so @insane_analyst's *"there is a reason Lumentum and AXTI both avoiding 6-inch"* is now half-retired and the open question is whether LITE follows**; **backlog >$100m confirmed but actively DEPRECATED by AXT as a metric (*"it's really NOT ABOUT ORDERS, IT'S ABOUT CAPACITY"*), which is why LITE's *"more substrates may be needed"* is a statement about AXT's furnaces rather than about LITE's contract**; and 🔴 **[[NVDA]] summoned AXT in autumn 2025 despite AXT not being a customer — *"they buy modules that have indium phosphide lasers in the module, and we sell to the companies that can make those things. Coherent and LUMENTUM come to mind… they just wanted to PREP US THAT WE NEED TO GO FAST"* — which dates the NVDA-driven urgency in this chain BEFORE the LTAs.**

The 400mW number stays in the body; the 350mW is recorded for lineage only and must not be used.**
  Net-new: **(1)** 🔴 a dated third-party **sole-source call — "we expect Lumentum to be the sole supplier for Nvidia's initial batches of CPO switch shipments, and Coherent may enter as the second supplier in late 2026"** — independent corroboration of the sole-source moat, plus a **now-live, checkable [[COHR]] second-source window**. **(2)** LITE's named slot in Nvidia's VLSI laser map — "**Lumentum for single high-power DFBs**" (vs Ayar DFB arrays, Innolume QD combs, Xscape/Enlightra/Iloomina resonant combs), and Nvidia's VCSEL-array exploration flagged as "not an immediate priority." **(3)** per-switch ELS content math (**18 ELS modules × 8 CW DFB chips**; **24.5 dBm** required per laser; **lasers + TECs ~70% of ELS power**; 4 of the 20 fibers per 1.6T-OE FAU go to the laser). **(4)** ★ the **technical case against ON-CHIP lasers** — blast radius, heat proximity, insufficient output power → "the one approach where the industry has come to a consensus is to use an External Light Source" — an independent, earlier counter to the Aurelion 07-29 "good enough to put on their own chip" integration bear. **(5)** the CW vendor set (AVGO / Furukawa / LITE / COHR / Yuanjie / Shijia) and the **commoditised-CW vs high-power-moat** split, which corroborates this page's own ≥100mW-tier reconciliation. **(6)** ⚠️🔴 an under-covered content risk: **multi-λ / comb / VLSP sources deflate the NUMBER of discrete high-power lasers per switch** — Lightmatter's GUIDE claiming **2 modules could replace the 18 ELSs** in a Quantum-X CPO switch, Xscape ChromX (one laser, up to 128λ), Scintil LEAF Light (8/16λ ELSFP, claims to halve energy per bit vs "what Nvidia is currently adopting for Q3450"), Ayar SuperNova (16λ, supplied by Sivers). All unverified Jan-26 vendor claims, logged as a modelling question against the ELS content math — **not a thesis change.** **(7)** InP cost structure (3"/6" wafers, low yields vs 8"/12" silicon) supporting the existing InP-chokepoint thread. Cross-reads [[COHR]], [[AVGO]], [[MRVL]], [[GLW]], [[TSM]], [themes/optical-cpo](themes/optical-cpo.md).

NET: walling Chinese capacity into a domestic market makes it cheaper, not smaller — Barclays' C28 overshoot arriving from the other direction.**

- **2026-07-31 (wiki-ingest) — Lumentum has reversed course on single-sourcing InP substrates, which is both a supply-security signal and the retirement of a bear argument.** No rating or PT superseded. **The item: Lumentum has signed a long-term InP substrate supply agreement with AXT — despite having, only months ago, expressed confidence in a ~7-YEAR SUMITOMO agreement.** ⚠️ **A 7-year incumbent agreement proved insufficient, and the Lumentum CEO has been "open recently about how they need to MULTI SOURCE AGGRESSIVELY and secure as much as possible"** (AXT 2Q26 call, primary, via buyside notes, 2026-07-31). **The inference worth carrying: substrate COST is not the binding concern — AVAILABILITY is.** Supporting context: **AXT is shipping record InP wafer volumes to both LITE and COHR "and they still want more"; AXT is ahead of schedule doubling InP capacity in 2026 with the InP revenue opportunity tripling by end-2026 and ~$130M/quarter exiting 2027 (~$520M annualised); backlog >$100M; and "demand outpaces supply, no matter how fast they add capacity."** Demand mechanism: **800G→1.6T with MORE LASERS PER TRANSCEIVER, higher-spec 6-inch substrates, and hyperscalers advancing toward NPO and CPO.** ⚠️ **One boundary on the read-through: AXT still lacks US export permits, with ~$66M of Q3-realisable revenue not requiring one — a US permit unlock would be upside no one is currently forecasting.** Positioning: LITE is on BofA's **second-wave** AI re-entry list of names still 30-50% off highs (with GLW, MRVL, SNDK, KLAC, INTC); named among JPM's beneficiaries of **~$750bn 2026 hyperscaler capex.** **Events: JPM COHR + LITE callback 2026-08-14 10am ET; LITE at the JPM HW & Semis Management Access Forum 08-12.** Cross-filed [[AXTI]], [[COHR]], [[GLW]], [[POET]], [themes/optical-cpo](themes/optical-cpo.md).
- **2026-07-30 — Aurelion Research / PhotonCap "A Conversation with Lumentum" (2026-07-29), a JOINT INDEPENDENT publication (not a bank): management meeting + investment analysis by Aurelion Research, technical comments by PhotonCap. 2 Current-state bullets, 5 intra-quarter rows (supply/produto/competicao/valuation/demanda), a re-scored Sinal-vs-management table, a Risks update, a synthesis rewrite and a Sources entry. NOTHING SUPERSEDED — but three tensions are now on the page and are deliberately left unresolved.** **Why it ranks: this is management (Kathryn Ta, VP Investor Relations), which outranks the brokers and experts that make up most of the quarter's flow. ⚠️ Provenance caveat carried on the page: the authors state interview responses are paraphrased from their notes and "do not represent verbatim statements by Lumentum or its management."** **CONFIRMED from management:** the **">30% greater demand than is physically suppliable"** imbalance (already on the page from Q3FY26); **all EML capacity locked in LTAs through FY27** with non-signers risking their whole allocation; **five fabs** (consistent with the Greensboro 5th-fab line already on the page); **components ~65% / systems ~35%**; **first scale-up CPO deployments at "late 2027"** — which confirms mgmt's existing late-cal-'27 mark and, critically, **contradicts LITE's own Director of Silicon Photonics R&D from 07-23 (CPO trial ~2028, hyperscaler deployment ~2030, GPU-GPU copper for at least one more generation) and sits against MS's 07-28 markdown of 2028 CPO *scale*. Contradiction #1, left on the page and flagged in the Sinal table — a management confirmation on timing is stronger evidence than an expert's, and the divergence inside one company is the alpha.** **CONTRADICTS / COMPLICATES the scarcity trade, from management's own mouth: Japanese EML capacity is up EIGHT-FOLD in two and a half years (two Japan fabs make most of the world's EML supply), a new fab is ramping but "not yet producing wafers," tools have been added at every site and several building shells expanded — and Aurelion flags it itself: "Eight-fold EML capacity in two and a half years cuts against a pure scarcity thesis. Lumentum is racing to relieve the bottleneck that gives it pricing power." PhotonCap converts it into the sharper claim: "wafer starts and qualified yield at the newest spec are different numbers… the binding constraint is yield at the highest power bins, not floor space… the eight fold number overstates the relief, and… the shortage premium itself is not durable while the margin that survives lives in yield and mix, not in the shortage price." Net: the thing you underwrite shifts from shortage PRICE to yield/mix DURABILITY. No estimate on this page changes; the Sinal-vs-management "Demand vs supply" row moved from a clean ✓ to ✓ + ⚠ (old value: "✓ confirms (supply is the constraint)" — retained in the cell).** **Contradiction #2, left open: Aurelion's bear #1 states "Lumentum still runs 4 inch wafers and is trying to catch up" against Coherent's 6-inch line (~2.25x usable area per wafer, more die at lower cost, COHR InP sold out through 2027) — while the 07-23 expert call on this page confirmed LITE's purchase of a 6-inch III-V fab in North Carolina. Current volume production 4-inch, Greensboro 6-inch; both stated, not reconciled.** **Contradiction #3, left open: two customers at 26% and 12% of fiscal Q3 2026 revenue (added to Risks) vs Citi's 07-16 mgmt callback that NVDA is still not a top-10 / 10%+ customer (low-single-digit %) — so the 26% customer is probably not NVDA. The FY25 10-K split (Customer A 16.0% / Customer B 15.4%) is NOT superseded; it stands as the annual figure, with the quarterly 26%/12% added as the more current concentration read.** **NET-NEW, additive:** PhotonCap's **third light-source platform** — GaAs VCSEL alongside InP EML and InP high-power CW, with **>10 billion emitters shipped** for smartphone 3D sensing and an **OFC 2026 1060nm VCSEL + photodetector 2D monolithic array co-packaged with a host ASIC targeting UCIe/PCIe slow-and-wide scale-up, >150°C, channel sparing** ("what sits on the roadmap is not VCSEL technology. It is commercial adoption in AI chip-to-chip links") — a materially stronger architecture hedge than the page carried, and directly relevant to the LightCounting InP-91%→79% / VCSEL-9%→21% lane-share debate; the **CPO-delay-extends-the-EML-cycle** frame (an integration-driven delay pushes CW/ELSFP right while extending EML, so cannibalization "flips into a cushion"); **Global Semi Research channel numbers** (NVDA CW guidance to LITE+COHR ~40MM units in January → ~100MM by April/May, back-solving to ~200-300k CPO switches in 2027 and 600-800k in 2028, aligned to TSMC COUPE — **channel, not company disclosure, per Aurelion's own weighting**); the specific **SemiAnalysis 2026-06-09 mechanism** behind the June selloff (scale-out CPO revised down through FY27, scale-up pushed to FY29, on a single very limited ASIC with difficult integration economics); the **McKinsey 800G shortfall** (~half of demand in FY27, extending through 2029); **a second, ground-up NVDA-funded US fab** distinct from the Greensboro conversion (the page previously carried only a "phase-four fab" watch-item); **Greensboro revenue contribution ~6 quarters from the May call = "late 2027 at the earliest,"** slightly earlier than the page's Greensboro-2028 mark (both retained); **EML production expanding ~40% annually** (lower than the page's mgmt-sourced ">50% EML unit growth by Dec-Q 2026" — both retained, neither superseded); **~60% of the EML market** (a different basis from the ~50% AI EML *unit* share from Citi/Mizuho — explicitly NOT treated as superseding it); PhotonCap's **RIN/COD/SMSR/FIT moat definition** ("qualified usable power at operating temperature, multiplied by spectral quality, reliability, and high volume yield"; "headline specs are what challengers close first… distribution tails, noise, and lifetime statistics close last"); the **EML-vs-CW product map** ("EML is today's cash flow and high power CW is the next mix shift"); **NVIDIA's technical blog naming three ELS vendors — Lumentum, Sumitomo, Coherent** ("second sourcing is in progress, not a hypothesis"), with **COHR's OFC 2026 6-inch InP ramp + 400mW CW** and **AAOI's 400mW narrow-linewidth sampling (Dec 2025)** landing outside the FY27 LTA window; and a **Zetta Semiconductor update** (mass-producible 100G PAM4 EML at OECC 2025, 200G/lane at ACP Nov 2025 — "device level performance deserves to be taken seriously now"), which extends the DSBJ/China-EML watch-item already in Risks. **NEW RATING/PT MARKS (none superseded — none were previously on the page): TD Cowen cut its PT to $800 from just over $990** — the first PT *reduction* logged here, from the page's cleanest structural bear (Hold since 2026-03-11, no PT previously recorded); **UBS re-affirmed a hold on LITE** (prior UBS content here was Sunny Lin's Asia CPO work, not a LITE rating); **Citi reiterated Buy $1,100 with a catalyst watch (confirms 07-16)**; the full sell-side range is now **$900 to $1,400**, widening the page's previously recorded ~$1,100-$1,270 cluster in both directions; stock **~20% below its all-time high**, with Aurelion's framing that "most of the photonics supercycle has been realized and played out." The Sinal table's **Valuation** row keeps its original "⚠ nuance (multiple already prices it in)" and adds the dispersion read. **Token-economics exchange cross-filed to [themes/tokenmaxxing.md](themes/tokenmaxxing.md); CPO/copper/laser-supply content to [themes/optical-cpo.md](themes/optical-cpo.md); peer/competitive content to [COHR.md](COHR.md); read-throughs to [NVDA.md](NVDA.md), [AVGO.md](AVGO.md), [AMD.md](AMD.md).** LITE reports **8/11 AMC**.
- **2026-07-26 (/wiki-ingest — Outlook, weekend batch). 2 intra-quarter rows. Nothing superseded.** **The Nokia optical-DCI read-through already on this page now has its number, and it is a ~2x beat against a bogey that was being debated the day before: AI order intake of €2.8bn vs a €1.3-1.5bn buy-side bogey** (JPM/Scott Silver) — the best-quantified evidence of AI/cloud optical order momentum on this page, and upstream of exactly the transceiver/laser content LITE sells. **The reaction is the lesson, not the beat: Nokia reversed from +7% at the open to roughly flat, after already falling ~40% from its highs, with JPM writing "I struggle to identify what more the company realistically could have delivered today beyond raising guidance."** Same pattern as INTC, TSM, ASML, GOOGL and GEV in the same week — demand confirmed, tape not paying. **Second, a supply-side item that cuts against the moat over a 2028 horizon rather than a 2026 one: JPM raised WIN Semiconductors (OW, PT NT$336 → NT$373) 2026 revenue growth to 30% from ~10% (Optical +50%+, Infra +40%+) on earlier-than-expected optical receiver chip (PD) mass production — "late 2Q vs 2H26" — with utilisation going 65% (2Q) → 70%+ (3Q26e) and capex reiterated at NT$2-3bn, "significantly higher than the annual capex in prior years."** Two details matter here. The demand concentration: **WIN has only ONE PD project in mass production, yet that single customer's order scale alone takes AI-related optical comm to a high-single-digit mix of WIN's total revenue in 2H26e, "more than doubled vs 2025"** (JPM: "likely a major US design house"). And the competitive one: **a CW laser project with "a major US optical chip IDM," mass production at YE27** — the first dated timeline on this page for merchant-foundry capacity entering the CW/UHP-laser bottleneck that the BTG expert work framed as a two-supplier moat. Not a 2026 threat; a 2028 one, and worth tracking against the LITE/COHR laser thesis. Tape context: **optical multiples derated -15% since the June 22 SOX peak, having peaked earlier than the rest of the complex** (Jefferies); WIN itself is **-38% over three months vs TaiEx +12%**. LITE reports **8/11 AMC**. Cross-filed [[COHR]], [[AAOI]], [[AXTI]], [themes/optical-cpo](themes/optical-cpo.md).
